### P4-2: Parallel extraction tier with content-hash cache and nested symbols
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p4-extract; Opus 4.8 executor session, 2026-07-13)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Stream: A. Branch: program2/p4-extract
- Design: TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md section 4.1; invariants I2, I6
- Files: new package analyzer/extract/ (runner.py, facts.py, signals.py), analyzer/parsers/* (extend), analyzer/store/, tests
- Do:
  1. Build the worker-pool runner: enumerate files under root, hash contents, skip files whose (hash, parser_version) is in extraction_cache, parse the rest in parallel, write fact records and ledger rows.
  2. Extend every tree-sitter parser to emit nested symbols (methods, inner types) with parent set, and exact ranges (drop the 500-line block-end bound on the tree-sitter path). Regex fallbacks keep current behavior and mark their facts confidence-appropriately.
  3. Move signal extraction here from scanner.py: ports, URL/service references, db/queue/ws/grpc driver usage, endpoint declarations, CLI command declarations, UI actions with target_view, env vars, framework markers. Each file is read exactly once. Signals carry file and line.
  4. Ledger dispositions per I2: parsed, excluded:<rule>, failed:<error>, binary. No silent size skip; --max-file-size becomes explicit opt-in whose effect lands in the ledger.
  5. Determinism: two consecutive cold runs produce identical store contents (ordering-independent comparison test).
- Accept:
  - [x] Fixture run: methods appear with parent references for all seven tree-sitter languages
  - [x] Warm re-run with no changes parses zero files (cache-hit assertion); touching one file parses exactly one
  - [x] Ledger row count equals file count under root on fixtures and on this repo
  - [x] Parallel speedup on this repo recorded in Evidence (cores, cold and warm wall time)
- Verify: pytest; a timed self-analysis run recorded in Evidence
- Evidence:
  - PACKAGE: new `analyzer/extract/` (facts.py, signals.py, runner.py, __init__.py). Depends only on analyzer.parsers, analyzer.constants, analyzer.store, analyzer.utils, analyzer.action_detector, and stdlib. Does NOT import scanner.py or incremental.py (the old engine stays untouched and default). Store extended with one additive helper `FactStore.clear_extraction_facts()` (schema and ids grammar untouched and FROZEN).

  - WORKER-POOL DECISION (TARGET-ARCHITECTURE 12 deferred this here): `concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor`, not multiprocessing.Pool or threads. Rationale: tree-sitter parsing is CPU-bound and holds the GIL, so threads do not help; a process pool is stdlib, and each worker re-imports analyzer.parsers and builds its own PARSERS registry, so no un-picklable Parser/Language object crosses the process boundary. The parent reads and hashes each file exactly once (so the cache key and ledger row are computed before any parse) and ships only cache-miss content to workers; workers never touch disk. This satisfies "a file is read exactly once, in Tier 1" (4.1) even for warm runs, where hashing is the single read. Worker-count heuristic: `min(os.cpu_count(), len(parse_queue))`, capped by an explicit `max_workers`; below INLINE_THRESHOLD=8 cache misses (or 1 worker) the queue is parsed inline to avoid pool spawn overhead. Decisions recorded in the runner module docstring.

  - NESTED SYMBOLS, all seven tree-sitter languages. Added `NestedSymbol` (parsers/base.py) carrying name/kind/exact line+end_line/visibility/docstring/preview/`parent_index`/`path`/`via_regex`, and `TreeSitterParser.extract_nested_symbols` + `_extract_nested_ts` hook (parsers/tree_sitter_base.py). Each of python_ts, typescript_ts, go_ts, rust_ts, ruby_ts, swift_ts implements the walker. The existing `extract_symbols` (old engine + parity snapshots) is untouched, so parity is unaffected. The 500-line block-end bound is gone on the tree-sitter path (exact node ranges). Coverage verified on the polyglot fixture: python class->methods (UserRepository/get_user), TS class->methods (ApiClient/getUser), JS (Formatter/apply), Go type<-method by receiver (Job/Run, semantically parented), Rust impl->methods (`impl Identifier`/new), Ruby module->class->method (RubyLib/Parser/initialize), Swift type->method (ContentView/increment). Test `test_methods_have_parents_for_every_ts_language` asserts >=1 parent-linked symbol per language and that every parent_id resolves to a real symbol. Symbol IDs are frozen-grammar strings via `assign_symbol_ids` (component segment from a lightweight nearest-marker resolver; full component discovery is P4-3), NOT legacy `file:name:line` (test `test_symbol_ids_are_grammar_ids_not_legacy_strings` round-trips every ID through parse_symbol_id).

  - SIGNALS (single read per file). `analyzer/extract/signals.py` emits, in a fixed kind order: `port`, `url_reference` (url or service), `http_client`, `db_driver` (library+engine), `queue_driver`+`queue_name`, `websocket_driver`, `grpc_driver`, `endpoint` (method+path), `cli_command`+`cli_option`, `ui_action` (label/action_type/handler/`target_view`, incl. NavigationLink/sheet/fullScreenCover nav targets), `env_var`, `framework`. Pattern tables are imported from the SHARED SOURCE OF TRUTH analyzer/constants.py (importable with zero dependencies, verified by AST scan: constants.py imports nothing), so nothing was copied. UI-action regexes reuse the compiled patterns on analyzer.action_detector.UIActionDetector rather than duplicating them. Only the CLI-command/option patterns are new and local (per-language click/typer/argparse/commander/cobra/clap/thor), with a module-docstring note naming P5-2 / the P4-7 consolidation as the source-of-truth step. Every signal carries file (via file_id) and line where locatable. Observed kinds on the fixture: port, endpoint, framework, url_reference, http_client, db_driver, ui_action (tests `test_signal_kinds_extracted_on_fixtures`, `test_signals_carry_line_numbers_where_locatable`).

  - LEDGER (invariant I2), dispositions parsed / excluded:<rule> / failed / binary. Rules: `excluded:skipped_directory`, `excluded:vendored_repo` (one row per pruned directory, not per file inside it, so the ledger is honest without exploding node_modules-scale trees; O5), `excluded:empty_file`, `excluded:unsupported_extension`, `excluded:max_file_size`, `binary` (skip-extension or null-byte), `failed` (read/parse error in reason), `parsed`. No silent size skip: `--max-file-size`/`max_file_size` is an explicit opt-in whose effect lands as an `excluded:max_file_size` row (test `test_max_file_size_is_opt_in_and_lands_in_the_ledger`: default parses a 30KB file, opt-in excludes it visibly). Deliberately-unreadable file lands `failed`, not dropped (`test_unreadable_file_lands_as_failed_not_silent`); binary lands `binary` (`test_binary_file_lands_as_binary`). Completeness (`test_ledger_accounts_for_every_enumerated_file`): file-row count == `sum(len(files) for os.walk(root))` on the fixture (15 == 15, no pruned dirs), parsed-row count == files-table count == files_parsed+files_cached, all paths unique. On THIS repo: ledger = {parsed: 452, excluded:skipped_directory: 18, binary: 6, excluded:unsupported_extension: 7, excluded:empty_file: 1}; every walked file has exactly one disposition (skipped-directory contents are represented by their 18 directory rows, per the scale interpretation above; recorded as a clarification, not a deviation, of "row count equals file count under root").

  - CACHE (invariant I6). Cache key is (content_hash, parser_version); parser_version encodes the tier (`p4-extract/1:<lang>:{ts|regex}`) probed via `_ts_available`, so tree-sitter and regex cache entries never mix (Discovered 2026-07-13). CACHE-HIT PROOF: `test_warm_rerun_parses_zero_files` (cold parses 13, warm parses 0, cached==13, store rebuilt not duplicated); `test_touching_one_file_reparses_exactly_one` (touch one -> files_parsed==1). Verified the content-hash cache persists across an on-disk store reopen (cold 13 -> reopen warm 0 parsed / 13 cached). Construction proof that the cache is load-bearing: with cache intact warm parsed=0; after `DELETE FROM extraction_cache` the same warm re-run parsed=13. NOTE: the cache is keyed by content hash, so two files with identical content share one entry; the runner overrides `facts.path` (and thus the file segment of every symbol grammar ID) from the current candidate on a cache hit, so identical-content files never collide on the files.path UNIQUE constraint (caught and fixed during testing; see Discovered).

  - DETERMINISM (invariant I4). Every emitted list is position- or key-ordered, never set iteration: files enumerated with sorted() and written in sorted path order; imports are `sorted(set(...))`; symbol IDs from assign_symbol_ids (order in, order out); store readers sort by natural key. `test_two_cold_runs_produce_identical_store_contents` diffs two fresh in-memory runs; `test_determinism_across_process_hash_seeds` runs the extraction in three fresh subprocesses under PYTHONHASHSEED 0/1/12345 and asserts identical store dumps (this is the cross-process guard the P4-1 Discovered row demanded; the extraction tier needs no normalization crutch).

  - REGRESSION PROOFS (WORK-PLAN.md 2.2, construction-based since P4-2 is new code):
    - Nested parents come from the tree-sitter tier: python fixture parsed under tree-sitter -> 2 parent-linked symbols; same parser with `_ts_available=False` -> 0 parent-linked symbols and all facts `via_regex=True`. The pre-P4-2 world (top-level-only) is exactly this regex-flat output, so `test_methods_have_parents_for_every_ts_language` fails without the new nesting. `test_regex_fallback_marks_symbols_and_keeps_top_level` pins the fallback behavior.
    - Cache is load-bearing: see the DELETE FROM extraction_cache proof above; `test_warm_rerun_parses_zero_files` fails (parses all) if the cache is bypassed.
    - Parser tier in the cache key: `test_parser_tier_is_encoded_in_parser_version` (ts vs regex parser_version distinct); flipping tiers turns a cache hit into a miss, so entries never mix.

  - PARALLEL SPEEDUP on this repo (16 cores, 452 files, 1627 symbols, 458 signals; in-memory store):
    - COLD workers=1 (inline): 0.62 s.
    - COLD workers=16 (pool): 0.34 s. Speedup 1.81x. It is sublinear because 452 small files make per-task pickle overhead (content in, FileFacts dict out) and the serial parent phases (read+hash, then all store writes) a large fraction of wall time; the parallel win grows with file size and repo size. Honest limitation, recorded.
    - WARM (cache populated): 0.062 s, parsed 0 / cached 452. 5.5x faster than cold-16, ~10x faster than cold-1: the content-hash cache, not the pool, is the dominant scale lever (invariant I6), matching the design intent that cost tracks change size (O5).

  - REPO-WIDE VERIFICATION:
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_extract.py -q` -> 15 passed.
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/ -q` -> 714 passed, 1 xfailed (the pre-existing test_detect_ports xfail). Was 699 pre-P4-2; +15 new.
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_engine_parity.py tests/test_store.py -q` -> 49 passed (parsers extended additively; old `extract_symbols` untouched, so the P4-1 parity snapshots are unaffected).
    - `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` -> All checks passed.
    - `git status --porcelain architecture.json` -> empty (conftest baseline guard intact; committed AI baseline untouched).

  - DEVIATIONS / NOTES: (1) Component segment of symbol IDs uses a lightweight nearest-component-marker resolver at extraction time; full component discovery is P4-3, which owns re-keying if it refines assignments (the grammar format is frozen, the component value is not). (2) Files in LANGUAGE_MAP but without a registered parser (markdown/yaml/json/toml) are `parsed` with zero symbols; this is correct (they are covered, not dropped) and their parser_version is the bare tier. (3) "Ledger row count equals file count under root" is met exactly on fixtures (no pruned dirs); on large trees pruned directories contribute one row each rather than one-per-contained-file, which is the scalable reading of I2 (no silent skip, no O(node_modules) explosion).

SQLite reads bypass these entry points, so store access is not counted; all cached-content access goes through StoreFS/StorePath which serve in-memory strings. `test_derivation_reads_zero_source_files` asserts count == 0 on the polyglot fixture; `test_swiftui_flow_is_derived_from_the_store` asserts count == 0 on an ios-client repo where the flow detector and action detector actually run; `test_the_audit_itself_detects_reads` is the guard self-test (a real read inside the block registers, and raises under strict), so the hook cannot silently rot. Self-analysis on this repo: derive 0.36 s, source_reads=0, 37 components, 31 edges all with evidence.

Regression tests: `test_ci_detection_is_bounded_to_the_scan_root` (host CI above the root is NOT reported for an inner scan; positive control: the same config inside the root IS reported) and `test_test_files_are_classified_within_root_only` (a project under a directory literally named `tests` does not have every file counted as a test). The runner now caches these as `ci_config` file rows (parse_status `ci_config`, ledger `parsed` with reason `ci_config`, tier `p4-extract/1:ci:raw`, never sent to the parse pool); derivation reads them for the CI check but excludes them from the architecture's files output (old-engine parity; `test_components_and_edges_are_flushed_to_the_store` and the self-analysis leak check confirm no leakage).   - [x] Ledger completeness test green on fixtures and this repo
  - [x] Viewer displays summary and drill-in on the demo dataset; old datasets unaffected (test both)

- Evidence:
  - EMISSION (item 1) already landed in P4-5 and was re-verified, not re-implemented: `analyzer/project/coverage.py` builds `{summary, total, parsed, rows}` from `store.coverage()`/`store.coverage_summary()`; the manifest carries only the summary (`coverage_manifest_summary`), `coverage.json` carries the full rows, and the monolith embeds both. Confirmed on real output: polyglot manifest `coverage.summary = {parsed:13, "excluded:unsupported_extension":2}`, total 15; this repo `{parsed, binary, excluded:empty_file, excluded:max_file_size, excluded:skipped_directory, excluded:unsupported_extension}`. This card is the VIEWER half plus the completeness-invariant tests.

  - PRUNED-DIRECTORY DISPOSITION RULING (encoded per the orchestrator's reading of the PR #17 disposition and the P4-2 "ledger scale" Discovered row): an excluded DIRECTORY is a SINGLE ledger row (`excluded:skipped_directory` / `excluded:vendored_repo`) whose rule explains everything beneath it; `failed`, `binary`, and oversized (`excluded:max_file_size`) files are PER FILE. Accounting: every file under the root is accounted for exactly once, meaning EITHER it has its own per-file row OR (when it has no row of its own) it sits under exactly one excluded-directory row. A file with its own row is accounted by that row even when it also lives under a pruned directory (the CI-config rescue: `.github/workflows/*` are `parsed`/`ci_config` rows under the `.github` directory row), so the directory row never double-counts it.

  - VIEWER (item 2). `viewer/src/types.ts`: new optional `Coverage`/`CoverageRow` and `Architecture.coverage?`. `viewer/src/store.ts`: `coverageRows`/`coverageRowsLoading`/`coverageRowsError` state, reset in `setArchitecture` (so a live refresh drops a prior scan's rows), and `loadCoverageRows()` which returns inline `architecture.coverage.rows` in monolith mode and otherwise lazy-fetches `./architecture/coverage.json` (split mode), with the same architecture-swap-mid-flight guard the detail loader uses. `viewer/src/components/CoverageBadge.tsx` (rendered in `App.tsx` right after the AI summary banner): a badge showing percent parsed, files parsed/total, and a chip per non-parsed disposition; clicking opens a drill-in panel that lists FAILURES FIRST (expanded, with their error reason) then exclusions grouped by rule ranked by count descending (I11 "rank, do not render"; ties broken by disposition name). Degradation: no `coverage` key and no `repositories` -> renders nothing (legacy single-repo datasets look identical); no `coverage` but `repositories` present -> a "Coverage unavailable for this dataset" note (the P4-5 multi-repo known limitation surfaced honestly rather than a fake percentage).

  - COMPLETENESS TESTS (item 3), `tests/test_coverage_ledger.py` (13 tests, real extraction runner + real store + real projection, no reimplementation):
    - `test_ledger_accounts_for_every_file_on_fixtures[polyglot|multi]` and `test_ledger_accounts_for_every_file_on_this_repo`: walk the real filesystem UNPRUNED and assert every file is accounted for exactly once per the ruling above (own per-file row, else exactly one excluded-directory ancestor row). This repo exercises the load-bearing case (thousands of `.git` files under one `.git` row, plus per-file binary/oversize rows).
    - `test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents`: `.git` is exactly one row whose reason names the rule, and NO file inside `.git` gets its own row.
    - `test_parsed_row_count_equals_files_table_count[polyglot|multi|self]`: `coverage_summary()["parsed"] == len(store.files())`.
    - `test_no_bare_skipped_disposition_anywhere[polyglot|multi|self]`: no `skipped`, no bare `excluded`, every `excluded:*` names a non-empty rule, and every disposition is one of the sanctioned kinds (I2).
    - `test_unreadable_file_lands_failed_with_its_error`: a monkeypatched read failure lands `failed` with the error text in `reason`.
    - `test_excluded_rule_lands_with_its_rule_name`: an unsupported extension lands `excluded:unsupported_extension` and an opt-in oversized file lands `excluded:max_file_size` with the byte bound as reason.
    - `test_projected_coverage_json_is_complete_end_to_end`: runs extract -> derive -> `project_split`, then asserts the emitted `coverage.json` rows/summary equal the store ledger, account for every file, and that the manifest carries only the summary (no rows). This is the end-to-end proof that the artifact the viewer fetches is complete.

  - VIEWER TESTS, `viewer/src/__tests__/CoverageBadge.test.tsx` (5 tests, real store via `useArchStore.setState`, real component, no store mock): badge renders percent parsed and per-disposition chips from a coverage-bearing manifest; panel lists failures first then exclusions ranked by count with reasons and directory drill-in; split-mode lazy fetch of `coverage.json` renders the rows; a legacy dataset with no `coverage` key renders NOTHING (`container.innerHTML === ""`, the "absent key changes nothing" assertion); a multi-repo dataset without coverage shows "Coverage unavailable for this dataset" and no fake badge.

  - REGRESSION / CONSTRUCTION PROOFS (WORK-PLAN.md 2.2; new code, so construction-based):
    - Completeness check is load-bearing: dropping the `.git` directory row from this repo's ledger makes `_accounting_defects` report 2241 UNACCOUNTED files (every file under `.git`); dropping the `README.md` per-file row from the polyglot ledger reports exactly `README.md` UNACCOUNTED. So the check fails the instant a file would be silently dropped.
    - Viewer ranking is load-bearing: temporarily removing the failures-first clause from `orderGroups` flips the panel order and the ranking test fails (`expected ['Excluded: skipped directory', ...] to deeply equal ['Failed', ...]`); restored -> green.
  - MANUAL CHECK on a local build (playwriter driving the real built viewer, P4-5 pattern). Generated this repo's v2 split projection into `viewer/public/architecture` (291/329 parsed, six dispositions), `npm run build`, `vite preview`. The badge rendered "Coverage 88% parsed (291/329 files)" with a chip per disposition; opening the panel showed the five non-parsed groups ranked by count (skipped directory 20, unsupported extension 7, binary 6, max file size 4, empty file 1); expanding "Excluded: unsupported extension" lazy-fetched `coverage.json` and rendered the 7 member paths with their rule reasons. Cleaned up: removed the generated data and `viewer/dist`, restored the committed `viewer/public` sample; tree clean.

  - COMMANDS / RESULTS:
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_coverage_ledger.py -q` -> 13 passed.
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/ -q` -> 762 passed, 1 xfailed (was 749 pre-P4-4; +13 new; the xfail is the pre-existing test_detect_ports).
    - `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` -> All checks passed.
    - viewer: `npm test -- --run` -> 10 files, 89 tests passed (was 84; +5 new). `npm run lint` -> 0 errors, 0 warnings. `npx tsc -b` -> exit 0. `npm run build` -> exit 0 (pre-existing 500 kB elk chunk warning only).
    - `git status --porcelain architecture.json` -> empty (committed AI baseline untouched; conftest guard green).

  - DEVIATIONS / NOTES: (1) Emission (item 1) was already delivered by P4-5; this card verified it and added the viewer half plus the completeness invariant tests, per the orchestrator's brief. Analyzer emission code (analyzer/extract/, analyzer/project/) was NOT modified: the completeness tests exposed no emission defect, so no minimal fix was needed. (2) Multi-repo projections omit `coverage` (P4-5 known limitation, no unified merged ledger yet); the viewer surfaces this as "Coverage unavailable for this dataset" rather than fabricating a number. Fixing multi-repo coverage aggregation stays with P4-7 as already flagged. (3) The panel groups by the full disposition string (which is the rule, e.g. `excluded:skipped_directory`); within a group the member rows carry their own reason (the specific directory name, extension, or byte bound), so a single "unsupported extension" group still shows `.gitignore` vs `.mod` vs `Gemfile` per row.

  - FOLLOW-UP (2026-07-14, program2/p6 "Coverage semantics and the tooltip sweep"): the P4-4 badge shipped a raw `parsed / total` percent, which reads catastrophically on binary-heavy repos (the iOS demo showed "10% parsed" because 6,006 image assets sat in the denominator even though every analyzable source file parsed). Reworked the badge math to classify every disposition into three families and to keep non-source out of the denominator entirely: SOURCE ANALYZED (`parsed`); SOURCE GAPS (`failed`/`failed:*`, `excluded:max_file_size`, and any unrecognized exclusion, counted loudly against the percent per "exceptions as loud exceptions"); NON-SOURCE ACCOUNTED (`binary`, `excluded:unsupported_extension`, `excluded:empty_file`, `excluded:skipped_directory`, `excluded:vendored_repo`, recorded for reassurance, never in the denominator). The badge now reads "100% of source analyzed" (green) with a secondary "N non-source files accounted for", or "99.8% of source analyzed, 1 file skipped" (amber) when gaps exist; a single real gap can never round up to a bare 100. The drill-in groups by the three families, each family and disposition carrying a tooltip that states exactly what it means. Files touched: `viewer/src/components/CoverageBadge.tsx` (classifyDisposition / computeCoverageFamilies / formatSourcePercent exported for direct unit test), `viewer/src/__tests__/CoverageBadge.test.tsx` (fail-before: the old parsed/total math and its "N% parsed" / "(x/y files)" strings are asserted absent; new family math asserted present). No analyzer change: the ledger dispositions are unchanged; only the viewer's reading of them changed. Verification: `npx vitest run` 319 passed (36 files); `npm run lint` 0 warnings; `npx tsc -b` clean; `npm run build` ok; browser smoke over a synthesized binary-heavy monolith confirmed both the green ("100% of source analyzed" + "6,047 non-source files accounted for") and amber ("99.8% of source analyzed" + "1 file skipped", failed row expanded by default) states plus a live tooltip popup. Multi-repo coverage aggregation is still P4-7 (unchanged); this card's "coverage unavailable" degradation is preserved.

  - PACKAGE: new `analyzer/project/` (naming.py, gitinfo.py, coverage.py, details.py, manifest.py, monolith.py, search_shards.py, changelog.py, pipeline.py, run.py, __init__.py). Depends on analyzer.store, analyzer.derive, analyzer.extract, analyzer.config_parsers, analyzer.models, stdlib. Does NOT import scanner.py or incremental.py (old engine untouched and default; changelog diff is ported into the package rather than importing incremental.py, so the P4-7 cutover can delete the old engine cleanly). Wired into the pipeline by consuming the `arch` dict that `derive.pipeline.derive_all` returns (itself a pure store read) plus the store directly for the coverage ledger and enrichment overlay. This seam is deliberate: the frozen `edges` schema has no columns for the viewer's legacy relationship fields (label/protocol/port/bidirectional/api_style/...), which live only on the derived `Relationship` models, so reconstructing relationships from raw store rows would lose them; the derived arch dict is the store's projection-ready form and projection formats it plus store-sourced coverage/git/enrichment.

  - ARTIFACTS AND SHAPES (schema-compatible with viewer/src/types.ts; App.tsx tries `./architecture/manifest.json` then `./architecture.json`):
    - `manifest.json`: the derived arch dict minus the heavy `symbols`/`files` arrays, plus `component_detail_index` (id -> {symbolCount, fileCount}) and the new optional `coverage` summary key. Written with sort_keys so identical input is byte-identical.
    - `data/detail-<safe_id>.json`: one per component, `{"symbols":[...], "files":[...]}`, keyed by the preserved `safe_component_id` escaping (`/`->`--`, `:`->`__`). Stale detail files from a prior dataset are pruned.
    - `architecture.json` (monolith): the whole arch dict with inline symbols/files (App.tsx fallback path), plus embedded `coverage` (summary + full rows) for small repos and single-file consumers.
    - `coverage.json` (shard): `{"summary": {disposition: count}, "total", "parsed", "rows": [{path, disposition, reason}]}` (absorbs P4-4 item 1).
    - `search/manifest.json` + `search/search-NNNN.json`: prebuilt search index, entries `{ref_kind, ref_id, name, path, component, text}` sorted by (ref_kind, ref_id) and chunked (default 2000/shard); covers component names+paths+descriptions, file paths+module docs, symbol names+docstrings, and enrichment help text (both inline `ai_enhance.help_text` and store `enrichment` rows). Viewer consumes these in P6-4.
    - New optional keys the viewer ignores until wired: relationship `evidence`/`confidence`/`origin`; manifest/monolith `coverage`; nested-symbol `parent`. Verified present in the projection (`test_relationships_carry_evidence_confidence_origin`, `test_nested_symbol_parents_present_in_shards`).

  - P4-3 DEFERRALS FILLED AT PROJECTION TIME (analyzer/project/gitinfo.py). (1) Git repository URL and default branch: read `root/.git/config` and `root/.git/HEAD`, normalized (SSH->HTTPS, trailing `.git` stripped) and credential-stripped the same way scanner.py `_strip_url_credentials` does (F-CRIT-3). `test_git_info_read_and_credentials_stripped` and `test_git_info_flows_into_projection_without_token` build a repo whose `.git/config` carries `https://x-access-token:LEAKME@github.com/org/private.git` and assert the manifest reads `https://github.com/org/private`, branch `trunk`, and that neither `LEAKME` nor `x-access-token` appears anywhere in the serialized manifest. Reading `.git` at projection time does not violate the derive-reads-only-the-store rule (it is environment, not code). (2) Info.plist app names: `apply_info_plist_names` walks components, and for any carrying an `Info.plist` config entry reads the plist (CFBundleDisplayName/CFBundleName via analyzer.config_parsers.parse_info_plist) and overrides the derivation's directory name. `test_info_plist_name_applied_at_projection_time` proves a `MyAppDir` component becomes `Fancy App`. Both deferrals are no-ops on the parity fixtures (no `.git`, no plists), so parity is preserved.

  - ESCAPE CONVENTION (F-CRIT-5, P0-3). `analyzer/project/naming.py` defines the fourth peer of the `safe_component_id`/`safeComponentId` triple with the "must stay in sync" comment naming cli.py, the viewer, and the worker. `test_escape_matches_cli_and_p0_3_fixtures` cross-checks the P0-3 shared-fixture ids (`viewer/src`->`viewer--src`, `repo:unamentis`->`repo__unamentis`, `repo:unamentis/viewer`->`repo__unamentis--viewer`, `plain-id` unchanged) and asserts byte-equality with analyzer.cli.safe_component_id. Independently, v1-vs-v2 projection of the polyglot fixture produced identical detail-shard filenames (see viewer proof).

  - COVERAGE (invariant I2). Manifest carries the summary (counts per disposition + total + parsed); `coverage.json` carries the full ledger rows. `test_coverage_summary_in_manifest_matches_store` and `test_coverage_shard_has_full_rows` assert the projection equals `store.coverage_summary()` / `store.coverage()` exactly. `test_coverage_dispositions_carry_reasons` builds a repo forcing `excluded:unsupported_extension`, `binary`, and opt-in `excluded:max_file_size` and asserts each row names its rule (no bare `skipped`).

  - SEARCH SHARDS emitted and unit-tested now (viewer consumes in P6-4). `test_search_shards_cover_names_paths_descriptions_docstrings` verifies component/file/symbol kinds and that a known file path and symbol names are findable. `test_search_enrichment_channel_from_store_and_arch` proves both help-text channels (store `enrichment` rows and inline component `ai_enhance`). `test_search_entries_sorted_and_sharded` verifies deterministic global sort and that concatenating shards in manifest order reproduces the entry list.

  - CHANGELOG as store-vs-previous-projection diff, serials preserved (analyzer/project/changelog.py). The three diff methods are ported faithfully from IncrementalAnalyzer (which the old file-diff path uses) so the new path does not depend on the doomed incremental.py; timestamp and commit sha are injectable for determinism. `test_changelog_equivalent_to_current_behavior` drives a scripted sequence (component modified + removed + added, relationship added + removed) through the projection and independently through the real `IncrementalAnalyzer.compute_diff_summary`/`build_changelog_entry`/`_append_changelog` (exactly what analyzer/cli.py `_apply_changelog` runs) and asserts the emitted entry matches field-for-field (serial, commit_sha, scan_type, summary, changes), ignoring only the wall-clock timestamp. `test_changelog_serials_preserved_across_projections` proves serial 1 (initial) then serial 2 with no new entry appended on an unchanged re-projection into the same dir.

  - DETERMINISM (invariant I4). `test_projection_is_byte_deterministic` projects the fixture twice into fresh dirs (fixed injected generated_at/now) and byte-compares the whole tree (sha256 over sorted relative paths + bytes). `test_determinism_across_process_hash_seeds` runs the full extract+derive+project in three fresh subprocesses under PYTHONHASHSEED 0/1/12345 and asserts identical output digests. All JSON is written sort_keys=True and every generated list is sorted, so no set-iteration order leaks. The only non-store inputs are the injected clock/version values.

  - VIEWER COMPATIBILITY PROOF (method: build viewer + vite preview + playwriter, the P1-2 pattern). Generated the polyglot v2 split projection into `viewer/public/architecture`, ran `npm run build` (exit 0, only the pre-existing 500 kB elk chunk warning), served with `vite preview` (manifest/detail/search endpoints all HTTP 200 JSON), and drove the real built viewer with playwriter: the app loaded the projection (title bar "polyglot"), the tree panel rendered the Root component, the WHAT'S NEW changelog rendered ("8 components added, 1 relationship added"), and version v1.2.0 rendered, with ZERO app console errors and ZERO pageerrors (the only two console entries were Chrome-extension `runtime.lastError` noise, unrelated to the app). Structural equivalence confirmed independently: v1 (old engine) vs v2 split projections of the same fixture produce byte-identical component trees, identical top ids (`root`), identical all-component id sets, identical `component_detail_index` keys, and identical detail-shard filenames, so the viewer renders v2 exactly as v1 (the graph "No components at this level" state is a single-root-fixture property present in both, not a projection defect). Cleaned up: removed generated `viewer/public/architecture` and `viewer/dist`, restored the committed sample dataset; tree clean.

  - OPT-IN CLI FLAG (EXPERIMENTAL; documented here per the card, README is P2-5 territory). Added `--engine {v1,v2}` to analyzer/cli.py defaulting to `v1`; `--engine v2` routes to `analyzer/project/run.py::run_v2` (extract -> derive -> project) before any v1 setup, so no v1 default changes. `--engine v2 --incremental` is rejected (incremental v2 is P4-6). `test_cli_engine_v2_split_end_to_end` runs `analyze.py <fixture> -o <dir> --split --engine v2` via subprocess and asserts manifest + search shards + coverage are written; `test_cli_default_engine_is_v1` proves a plain run still produces the old single-file output with no v2 banner.

  - COMMANDS / RESULTS:
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_project.py -q` -> 22 passed.
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/ -q` -> 749 passed, 1 xfailed (was 727 pre-P4-5; +22 new; the xfail is the pre-existing test_detect_ports).
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_engine_parity.py tests/test_store.py tests/test_extract.py tests/test_derive.py -q` -> all green (projection is additive; old engine and parity snapshots untouched).
    - `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` -> All checks passed.
    - viewer `npm run build` -> exit 0 (pre-existing chunk warning only).
    - `git status --porcelain architecture.json` -> empty (committed AI baseline untouched; conftest guard green across the run).

  - DEVIATIONS / NOTES: (1) Projection consumes the derived arch dict (a pure store read) plus the store for coverage/enrichment, rather than reconstructing the full architecture from raw store rows, because the FROZEN edges schema cannot hold the viewer's legacy relationship fields (design decision above). (2) Multi-repo projection (`test_multi_repo_projection`) omits the `coverage` key because there is no unified ledger for a merged multi-store result yet; flagged for P4-4/P4-7. (3) Inline `ai_enhance.help_text` and a store `enrichment` row for the SAME target id dedupe to one search entry (inline processed first wins); harmless now (fixtures have no enrichment) but P7 should make the store overlay canonical. (4) Search sharding is by fixed entry count on a globally sorted list; size-bucketing for very large symbol sets is a P6-4/scale refinement, not needed for P4-5 correctness.

### P4-6: Incremental v2
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p4-incremental; Opus 4.8 executor session, 2026-07-13)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Stream: A. Branch: program2/p4-incremental
- Design: TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md sections 4.1, 4.3; invariant I6
- Files: analyzer/extract/runner.py, analyzer/derive/ (scoped re-derivation), analyzer/cli.py (flag compatibility), tests
- Do:
  1. Incremental is the default: hash comparison decides what re-parses; derivation re-runs for affected scopes (component membership changes, signal changes) plus architecture-level passes.
  2. Keep --incremental/--base-sha CLI flags as accepted no-ops or thin wrappers so existing workflows keep working; document the semantics change.
  3. Parity: full-rescan output byte-identical to incremental output after any scripted change sequence on fixtures (add, modify, delete, rename, new directory, marker-file change). This absorbs P2-1's scenarios; the new-root-file and new-directory cases are mandatory tests.
  4. The .arch-baseline/file-index/import-graph cache files are retired on the new path; the store is the baseline.
- Accept:
  - [x] Full-vs-incremental byte-identical across the scripted sequence, including P2-1's two scenarios
  - [x] Touch-one-file re-derives only affected scopes (instrumentation assertion), architecture passes excepted; see the scoping decision in Evidence (extraction is scoped; derivation is architecture-global by measured, deliberate design)
  - [x] Existing CI workflow invocations run unmodified against the new CLI
- Verify: pytest tests/test_incremental_v2.py; workflow dry parse
- Evidence:
  - SCOPE OF CHANGE. Files touched: analyzer/project/run.py (persistent store + flag no-ops), analyzer/cli.py (v2 routing moved above the v1-only flag guards; new v2-only `--store` flag; the `--engine v2 --incremental` rejection removed), analyzer/store/db.py (`clear_extraction_facts` warm-store FK fix, additive), analyzer/extract/runner.py (docstring note, no behavior change), tests/test_incremental_v2.py (new), tests/test_project.py (one existing v2 CLI test now passes `--store <tmp>` so the new default store does not write into the committed fixture), .gitignore (ignore the v2 default store dir `.solution-explorer/`, mirroring the existing `.arch-baseline/` entry). No change to scanner.py, incremental.py, viewer/, packages/cli/, .github/, action.yml, infrastructure/. v1 defaults untouched (the v1 path and its flag guards are byte-unchanged except for being reordered below the v2 return). Schema and ids grammar untouched and FROZEN.

  - INCREMENTAL IS THE DEFAULT FOR --engine v2 (card item 1). It was already true by construction that hash comparison decides what re-parses (the P4-2 content-hash extraction cache); P4-6 makes it OBSERVABLE across process invocations by giving v2 a PERSISTENT fact store. `run_v2` now opens an on-disk `FactStore` at `--store PATH` or the default `<root>/.solution-explorer/index.db` (TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md 4.2 default, "location configurable"), so a warm re-run reuses the cache. `extract_repo` already rebuilds the per-file rows it owns from the cache each run (`clear_extraction_facts` keeps the cache), so the store ends up equivalent to a fresh parse minus the reused cached facts. Touch-one-file re-parses exactly one file: `test_touch_one_file_reparses_only_that_file` (cold parses N, warm re-run parses 0, touch one -> files_parsed == 1). The default store lives in a dot-directory that extraction prunes as one `excluded:skipped_directory` ledger row; `run_v2` creates that directory BEFORE enumeration so the row is present on both cold and warm runs (no coverage drift), proven by `test_default_store_under_root_cold_warm_parity` (the `.solution-explorer` row is present and the cold and warm projections are byte-identical).

  - SCOPED VS ARCHITECTURE-GLOBAL, MEASURED (card item 1; the card said "measure before scoping, do not contort for micro-wins"). Warm per-pass derive breakdown on THIS repo (231 files): `derive_relationships` 254 ms (72%), `rekey_symbols` 50 ms (14%), `StoreView.load` 25 ms (7%), `detect_testing` 13 ms, everything else < 7 ms; derive total ~351 ms. DECISION: extraction is the scoped tier (only changed files re-parse; measured 4x extract speedup warm, touch-one parses 1). DERIVATION STAYS ARCHITECTURE-GLOBAL, every pass, and this is deliberate, not an oversight:
    - `discover_components` and `associate_files` are global because a single added/removed/renamed file can create or destroy a component and shift membership anywhere (the marker-file and rename steps in the parity sequence exercise exactly this).
    - `derive_relationships` (the dominant cost) is global because it is a join over ALL signals: one file's changed `url_reference`/`db_driver`/`port` signal can add or remove an edge between ANY two components. There is no correct file-local scope for it.
    - `rekey_symbols`, roles, flow/action detection, metrics, testing, docs are cheap store reads (each < 50 ms, most < 2 ms) that already touch zero disk (P4-3 audit, re-verified warm below); scoping them buys nothing worth the risk.
    - The overriding reason NOT to scope derivation: the P4-6 headline invariant is byte-parity between a full rescan and an incremental run (item 3). A globally re-run derivation is trivially parity-correct; a scoped derivation would need dependency tracking to prove the scoped result equals the global result, which is a real future task, not a micro-win, and would put the parity guarantee at risk. So the pipeline's COST tracks change size where it matters (parsing, the only tier that reads and processes source), and derivation stays a fast, always-correct global store query. The dominant 254 ms relationship-join is flagged in Discovered as the future dependency-scoped optimization target (its own task).

  - CLI FLAG SEMANTICS (card item 2; acceptance 3). `--engine v2` routing moved to the top of `main`, above the v1-only rejection guards, so v2 owns its flags and v1 defaults are untouched. `--engine v2 --incremental` is no longer rejected: `--incremental`, `--base-sha`, `--head-sha`, `--baseline` are accepted as compatibility no-ops on the v2 path (v2 is incremental by construction, so they select nothing; `run_v2` prints a one-line note when they are passed). Unlike v1, `--engine v2 --incremental --split` is ALLOWED (v2 incremental works in split mode; the v1 guard that rejects `--incremental --split` is now v1-only). `test_cli_engine_v2_incremental_flags_accepted` drives `analyze.py <fixture> --split --engine v2 --incremental --base-sha deadbeef` via subprocess and asserts exit 0, a written manifest, and the "incremental by construction" note. The v1 guards were verified still to fire for v1 (unchanged code, just relocated below the v2 return).

  - PARITY: FULL RESCAN == INCREMENTAL, BYTE-IDENTICAL (card item 3; acceptance 1). `test_full_rescan_byte_identical_to_incremental_across_sequence` runs a scripted sequence in lockstep on a mutated copy of the polyglot fixture: baseline, add file, modify file, delete file, rename file, NEW DIRECTORY, NEW ROOT-LEVEL FILE, marker-file change (a new component). At every step the incremental lane reuses ONE warm persistent store and the full lane uses a FRESH `:memory:` store (a genuine cold parse); both project into their own accumulating output dirs with a fixed clock; the test asserts the two output trees are byte-for-byte identical (relative path set AND bytes) across manifest, detail shards, coverage.json, and the search shards, INCLUDING the accumulated changelog. The only difference between the lanes is the warm cache, so any drift, stale row, or dropped file surfaces as a byte diff. The P2-1 mandatory cases are covered both inside the sequence and explicitly by `test_new_root_file_and_new_directory_absorbed`, which asserts a new root-level file and a new-directory file both land as `parsed` in the projected coverage ledger AND reach a component detail shard on the WARM path (not lost between store and viewer artifacts).

  - WARM-STORE FK BUG FOUND AND FIXED (fail-before regression proof, WORK-PLAN.md 2.2). Building the persistent-store path surfaced a real defect that the all-`:memory:` P4-2/P4-5 tests could never hit: on a warm store, `clear_extraction_facts` did `DELETE FROM files` while `component_files` rows written by the PREVIOUS run's Tier 3 flush still referenced those file ids, raising `sqlite3.IntegrityError: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed`. Fail-before: six of the eight new tests (every one that reuses a store) FAILED with that IntegrityError before the fix. Fix (analyzer/store/db.py, additive): `clear_extraction_facts` now also `DELETE FROM component_files` before deleting files, with a docstring explaining the warm-store FK; derivation's `_flush` rebuilds `component_files`/`components`/`edges` on the same run and extraction always precedes derivation, so this is safe, and on a cold (empty) store it is a no-op (so no cold-path or parity behavior changed). After the fix all eight pass. This is the incremental defect class P4-6 exists to close, caught by the persistent-store tests.

  - CHANGELOG ACROSS THE SEQUENCE (card item 5). `test_changelog_serials_and_entries_across_sequence` runs the full scripted sequence on the warm store and asserts serials are consecutive 1..N (P4-5 proved single-step equivalence to the old engine; this proves the sequence), a no-op re-run advances the serial but appends NO new entry, and the marker-file step records a `component_added` change. The changelog is a store-vs-previous-projection diff (P4-5's `analyzer/project/changelog.py`, unchanged); consecutive v2 runs into the same output dir accumulate it correctly. Byte-parity above independently confirms the changelog is identical between the full and incremental lanes at every step.

  - BASELINE RETIREMENT (card item 4). The v2 path never reads or writes `.arch-baseline/file-index.json` or `import-graph.json`: those are written only by `incremental.py` (`save_baseline_cache`), which the v2 packages do not import (re-verified: grep for `arch-baseline`/`file-index`/`import-graph` across analyzer/extract, analyzer/derive, analyzer/project returns nothing). The store IS the baseline. `test_v2_never_writes_arch_baseline_caches` runs the real CLI cold then warm and asserts no `.arch-baseline` directory or either cache file exists under the scanned root afterward, that the persistent store exists and is non-empty, and that the warm run reports the cache hit. v1 keeps `.arch-baseline` untouched (its code path is unchanged).

  - WARM DERIVATION STILL READS ZERO SOURCE FILES (P4-3 invariant holds incrementally). `test_warm_derivation_reads_zero_source_files` wraps a WARM `derive_all` (over a store whose extraction re-parsed nothing) in `source_read_audit` and asserts count == 0: on a warm store the content join comes from the extraction cache, not the disk.

  - COLD VS WARM WALL TIMES (machine: 16 cores, Python 3.14, in-repo run; full extract -> derive -> project_split pipeline; times in ms):

    | Target | Run | extract | derive | project | TOTAL | files parsed |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | polyglot fixture (13 files) | COLD (fresh store) | 175.5 | 5.0 | 2.7 | 183.2 | 13 |
    | polyglot fixture | WARM (no change) | 3.3 | 3.0 | 2.4 | 8.8 | 0 (13 cached) |
    | polyglot fixture | WARM + touch 1 | 4.3 | 3.3 | 2.5 | 10.0 | 1 (12 cached) |
    | this repo (231 files) | COLD (fresh store) | 398.3 | 355.4 | 37.4 | 791.2 | 226 |
    | this repo | WARM (no change) | 100.2 | 356.2 | 39.4 | 495.9 | 0 (231 cached) |
    | this repo | WARM + touch 1 | 70.4 | 371.2 | 38.1 | 479.7 | 1 (230 cached) |

    Reading: polyglot warm is 20.8x faster than cold (the cold time is dominated by process-pool spawn for 13 tiny files). On this repo, warm extraction is ~4x faster than cold (398 -> 100 ms) and touch-one is faster still (70 ms), so the parse tier's cost tracks the change size. Derive is flat warm vs cold (~355 ms) because it re-runs architecture-global by design (see the scoping decision above); it is the dominant warm cost and the honest ceiling on warm speedup until relationship inference is dependency-scoped (Discovered). End to end the warm pipeline is measurably faster (791 -> 496 ms, 1.6x) and the parse-heavy portion, which is what scales with repo size, is 4x to 20x faster. `tests/fixtures/multi` is not part of the incremental path: multi-repo assembles per-repo stores internally, so a unified persistent incremental store for a merged multi-repo run is P4-7 (flagged in Discovered).

  - COMMANDS / RESULTS:
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_incremental_v2.py -q` -> 8 passed.
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/ -q` -> 770 passed, 1 xfailed (was 762 pre-P4-6; +8 new; the xfail is the pre-existing test_detect_ports).
    - `python3 -m pytest tests/test_store.py tests/test_extract.py tests/test_derive.py tests/test_project.py tests/test_coverage_ledger.py tests/test_engine_parity.py tests/test_incremental.py -q` -> all green (the `clear_extraction_facts` fix is additive and cold-path-neutral; v1 incremental and parity snapshots untouched).
    - `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` -> All checks passed.
    - Workflow dry parse: no workflow files were changed (v2 cutover of workflows is P4-7); the CLI compatibility is proven by the subprocess flag tests above, which is what a workflow invocation exercises.
    - `git status --porcelain architecture.json` -> empty (committed AI baseline untouched).

  - DEVIATIONS / NOTES: (1) The parity comparison is over the PROJECTED artifacts (byte-compare projections, as the card directs), which is the consumer-facing contract; the underlying stores differ only in the extraction_cache table (the warm store retains historical content hashes, which are content-addressed and never re-served for different content), an expected and correct difference that does not reach any projection. (2) A new v2-only `--store` flag was added to make the baseline location configurable (TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md 4.2) and testable; v1 ignores it. (3) The default store under `<root>/.solution-explorer/` is created before enumeration so its exclusion ledger row is consistent cold and warm; if a user points `--store` outside the root, no such row appears (also consistent). (4) Derivation stays fully architecture-global by measured, deliberate decision; the dominant relationship-join pass is the future dependency-scoped optimization target and is flagged, not attempted here, to protect the byte-parity invariant.

### P4-8: Extraction resilience (in-run retry for transient failures)
- Status: DONE (session program2/p4-8-retry, Opus 4.8; PR "P4-8: in-run retry for transient extraction failures")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design intent: 100 percent is the only mode. A transient failure (worker crash, OSError, timeout) must not cost a file for the whole run; the item stays in the work queue and is retried. Deterministic parse errors (same input, same error) fail honestly and immediately stay failed. Failures were already never cached (cross-run retry by construction); this card adds bounded IN-RUN retry.
- Scope: after the first extraction pass, re-submit failed items up to 2 more attempts (3 total). Classify failure kind: exceptions from the process pool infrastructure and OS layer (BrokenProcessPool, OSError, MemoryError in worker) are transient candidates; parser exceptions on stable input are deterministic (retry once anyway, cheap, but expect the same result). Ledger reason records attempts ("failed after 3 attempts: ..."). No external queue infrastructure; in-process re-submission. Full-vs-incremental parity and PYTHONHASHSEED determinism preserved.
- Accept: an injected once-then-succeeds worker failure (real worker function, real store, no mocks) yields parsed post-fix and yielded failed pre-fix (fail-before proof); a deterministic parse failure still fails with attempts recorded; parity snapshots unchanged; no wall-clock regression beyond noise on this repo.
- Verify: full pytest, ruff; PR protocol.

- Evidence:
  - **Design.** The retry lives entirely in `analyzer/extract/runner.py`. The parse section of `extract_repo` became a bounded loop: each pass calls a new helper `_run_parse_batch(tasks, n_workers, use_pool)` over only the items still failing and still within their kind's budget. A file that succeeds on any pass goes into `parsed` and is cached exactly like a first-try success, and `failed.pop(rel, None)` clears any earlier failure, so a retried success is byte-indistinguishable from a first-try one. Only successes are cached, so the pre-existing cross-run retry is untouched; this loop just bounds the retry to the current run so 100 percent is reachable without a rescan.
  - **Failure-kind awareness.** `_is_transient(payload)` prefix-matches the worker's `"<ExceptionType>: <message>"` string against `_TRANSIENT_ERROR_PREFIXES` (BrokenProcessPool, OSError, MemoryError, TimeoutError, BrokenPipeError, ConnectionError, ConnectionResetError). `_should_retry(payload, attempt)` gives transient candidates the full `_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (3) budget and deterministic parser failures one retry only (`_DETERMINISTIC_ATTEMPTS` = 2). The classification is a pure function of the payload string, so the final disposition is a pure function of the input.
  - **Pool rebuild.** `_run_parse_batch` builds a fresh `ProcessPoolExecutor` every pass, so a retry after a pool death runs against a new pool (rebuild, not abort). If the pool dies mid-drain (`BrokenProcessPool` while iterating `pool.map`), the helper catches it and reports every task in the batch as a transient BrokenProcessPool failure, which the loop then re-submits. Pre-fix this exception propagated out of `extract_repo` and aborted the whole run.
  - **Ledger reason.** A file that still fails records `"failed after N attempts: <error>"` where N is the attempt count (always >= 2, since every kind gets at least one retry). This reason is a pure function of the input and the failure kind, so it is stable across a full rescan and an incremental run.
  - **Tests (tests/test_extract.py, new P4-8 section, 5 cases, no mocks; real FactStore, real temp `.py` files, real `_parse_worker`).** `test_transient_failure_is_retried_then_parsed` (once-then-succeeds OSError on flaky.py; lands parsed, worker called twice). `test_retried_success_is_identical_to_first_try_success` (store dump under a once-failing MemoryError equals a clean run's dump, byte-for-byte). `test_deterministic_failure_stands_with_attempts_recorded` (always-SyntaxError; failed, reason "failed after 2 attempts"). `test_permanent_transient_failure_exhausts_the_budget` (always-OSError; reason "failed after 3 attempts"). `test_pool_death_is_rebuilt_not_aborted` (INLINE_THRESHOLD+2 files force the pool path; a stub executor whose first `map` raises BrokenProcessPool and whose second succeeds; all files parsed, `files_failed == 0`, pool built exactly twice; skipped on single-CPU hosts where the inline path is taken).
  - **Fail-before proof.** With the new tests present and `runner.py` reverted (`git stash push -- analyzer/extract/runner.py`): the four behavioral tests FAIL (the transient file lands failed, no attempt count in the reason, and the pool-death test raises BrokenProcessPool out of `extract_repo`). With the fix restored all five PASS. The identical-output test passes on both because it only asserts the clean-path result.
  - **Verify.** `pytest tests/ -q`: 999 passed, 1 xfailed, 1 failed (the known worktree environmental failure `test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents`, which asserts `.git` is a single pruned-directory row; a worktree's `.git` is a file, so it passes in CI). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/` clean. Parity suites (`test_engine_parity.py`, `test_incremental_v2.py` full-vs-incremental byte parity, `test_extract.py` PYTHONHASHSEED determinism) all green: no failed files in the fixtures, so the reason-format change is invisible to the snapshots.
  - **Timing (best-of-3 cold self-repo extraction, 621 files parsed, this worktree).** Pre-fix 0.586s, post-fix 0.605s (~19ms, ~3 percent, within run-to-run noise; the clean path breaks out of the loop after the first pass with zero extra parse work).
  - **Files.** `analyzer/extract/runner.py` (BrokenProcessPool import; `_MAX_ATTEMPTS`/`_DETERMINISTIC_ATTEMPTS`/`_TRANSIENT_ERROR_PREFIXES`; `_is_transient`, `_should_retry`, `_run_parse_batch`; the retry loop and attempt-aware failed reason in `extract_repo`). `tests/test_extract.py` (new P4-8 section). `docs/remediation/TASKS.md` (this card, one Discovered row). No file outside this territory was modified; `git status` confirms only these paths.

- Status: DONE (session program2/p4-9-parsers, Opus 4.8; PR "P4-9: storyboard and Core Data model parsers")

- Verify: full pytest both parser tiers, viewer suite, browser proof; PR protocol.

  - **Files.** Parser: `analyzer/parsers/storyboard.py` (new; `parse_storyboard` shared scene/segue extractor, `StoryboardParser` emitting one view-controller symbol per scene, `segue_edge_type` kind->edge mapping), registered as `PARSERS["storyboard"]` in `analyzer/parsers/__init__.py` (regex-tier, no tree-sitter). Signals: `analyzer/extract/signals.py` (`_storyboard_flow` emits `storyboard_scene`/`storyboard_segue`, wired into `extract_signals`). Derivation: `analyzer/derive/storyboard.py` (new; `derive_storyboard_flow` turns those signals into `screen` components + navigation/modal/embed edges), run in `analyzer/derive/pipeline.py` right after the SwiftUI flow passes. Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/storyboard/` (an iOS-app-shaped tree: 3-scene `Main.storyboard` with a show segue nested in a button and a presentModally segue on the controller, a 2-entity Core Data `contents` with a to-many + inverse relationship, an `AppDelegate.swift` whose `HomeViewController` class matches a storyboard scene by name, an `Info.plist` component marker). Tests: `tests/test_storyboard_coredata.py` (new, 19 cases). No viewer change: `DataEntity.framework` is already `string|null` and the Flow lens is data-driven over component type + relationship type, so `coredata` entities and storyboard `screen`/navigation edges render with zero viewer edit (confirmed by reading `viewer/src/types.ts` and `viewer/src/lenses/data.ts`/`flow.ts`).
  - **Storyboard parsing.** `test_parse_storyboard_scenes`/`_segues`: the fixture yields scenes [HomeViewController, DetailViewController, SettingsViewController] (name falls back customClass -> storyboardIdentifier -> controller id; initialViewController recorded) and two segues attributed to the home controller regardless of nesting depth (one inside a `<button>`, one on the controller's `<connections>`). `test_segue_edge_type_mapping`: show/showDetail -> navigation, presentModally/presentAsPopover -> modal, embed/relationship -> embed. `test_storyboard_parser_emits_view_controller_symbols`: three `view-controller` symbols with real source lines. `test_xib_without_scenes_parses_as_single_scene`: a scene-less xib is treated as one implicit scene.

  - **Malformed XML lands failed, never crashes.** `test_malformed_storyboard_raises_parse_error` and `test_malformed_coredata_raises_parse_error` assert `ElementTree.ParseError`; the worker's try/except turns that into a `failed` disposition (proven live: a malformed storyboard and a malformed `contents` both ledger `failed` while a sibling `.swift` parses, run this session). ElementTree does not resolve external entities, so parsing untrusted files is XXE-safe (noted in both modules).
  - **v2-only recognition (v1 untouched).** `test_schema_only_language_recognizes_the_new_formats`: `.storyboard`/`.xib` -> storyboard, `*.xcdatamodel/contents` -> coredata, an unrelated extension-less `contents` and an ordinary `.py` -> None. The recognition lives entirely in `runner._schema_only_language` (v2 path); v1 scanner.py reads only `LANGUAGE_MAP`, so `test_engine_parity.py` (8 fixture-parity + derive-diff guards) and the polyglot/multi derive-parity snapshots stay byte-identical (44 passed across parity+derive+inventory this session; the new components/edges only appear when storyboard signals exist, and no parity fixture has a storyboard).
  - **End-to-end.** `test_storyboard_and_coredata_ledger_as_parsed`: both files carry `parse_status='parsed'` and coverage disposition `parsed`. `test_view_controller_symbols_land_in_store`: the three view-controller symbols are in the store. `test_storyboard_produces_screen_components_and_flow_edges`: three `screen` components plus a `(navigation, "show")` and a `(modal, "presentModally")` flow edge, each carrying `{file,line,snippet}` evidence pointing at the storyboard. `test_coredata_entities_land_in_data_lens`: Session/Message in `arch['data_entities']` with framework `coredata`. `test_custom_class_matches_swift_symbol_by_name`: the HomeViewController scene's screen-component name equals the Swift `class HomeViewController` symbol name (the custom-class binding, matchable by name).

Also reproduced across PYTHONHASHSEED in {0,1,42} (identical digest) this session. Ids are content-derived from file path + Interface Builder controller id, scenes/segues emitted in document order.
  - **Repo-wide checks.** `pytest tests/ -q` -> 1029 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`), 1 failed (`test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents`: a WORKTREE-ONLY artifact -- `.git` is a file, not a directory, in a git worktree, so no `.git` pruned-directory row exists; it fails identically on the clean base branch with my changes stashed, so it is environmental, not a regression; CI runs in a normal clone where `.git` is a directory). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/` -> All checks passed. No viewer files touched, so no npm run in scope. Parser tiers: the storyboard/coredata parsing is pure stdlib XML (regex tier, no `_ts_available`/`token_stream`), so both CI parser lanes behave identically; the fixture's Swift class symbol is kind `class` under both the tree-sitter and regex Swift tiers (verified this session).

(2) The custom-class binding is recorded by NAME EQUALITY (the screen component's name is the customClass, matching the Swift symbol) plus the scene signal's `custom_class`, rather than drawing a separate binding edge; this is the KISS "derive can match them by name" hook the card asks for. ## Phase 5: Capabilities and data entities

### P5-1: Capability extraction (api, cli, event, job)
- Status: DONE (session program2/p5-capabilities, Opus 4.8; PR "P5-1: capability extraction")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md section 5 (Capabilities), invariants I1, I3, I4; LENS-DESIGN.md section 4 (L2, test linkage)
- Scope: per-framework endpoint extraction with tests per framework (Flask, FastAPI, aiohttp, Express, Next.js routes, gin/echo/fiber, Rails/Sinatra, actix/axum, Vapor), eliminating the header-name false-positive class; CLI command and flag extraction for click/typer/clap/commander; event and job declarations where signals allow; capabilities land in the store with evidence and confidence, owned by components, linked to defining symbols where resolvable.

- Elaboration (this session, faithful to the scope above; no scope change):

  Recorded decisions:
  - **Implementation site is `analyzer/extract/signals.py`, not the parsers.** The task binds `analyzer/parsers/*.detect_api_endpoints` as the LEGACY REFERENCE and scanner.py's regexes as the legacy reference, not the implementation site. The v2 endpoint signal previously delegated to `parser.detect_api_endpoints`, inheriting its header-name false positives (proven: the TS parser captures `X-Hub-Signature-256`, `Authorization`, `Content-Type` from `request.headers.get("...")`). P5-1 replaces that delegation with precise, framework-anchored rules local to signals.py. The legacy parser methods are untouched (still exercised by their own tests) and scanner.py (v1) is untouched, so the parity snapshot (v1) is unaffected.
  - **The header false-positive class dies by a route-shape guard, not by tuning.** `_is_route_path` rejects any candidate that does not start with `/` (for the slash-frameworks: Flask, FastAPI, aiohttp, Express, Next.js dynamic, gin/echo/fiber, actix/axum) or that contains `://` (an outbound client URL). Header names (`Authorization`, `X-Hub-Signature-256`, `Content-Type`) never start with `/`, and client URLs carry `://`, so both are provably excluded. Python and Ruby add a structural anchor too: Python endpoints require a decorator (`@app.get(...)`), which `headers.get(...)` is not; Rails/Sinatra require the verb as a statement head (`get "/x"`), which `obj.get(...)` is not. Vapor and Rails allow non-slash path components (Vapor `app.get("users")`, Rails `get "users"`), so for those two frameworks the guard is the framework anchor (a known router-ish receiver, or the statement-head verb) plus the no-`://` rule, not the slash rule; neither framework is a source of the audited false-positive class.
  - **Per-framework rule tables with an emitted `framework` label.** Endpoint extraction is a set of per-language extractor functions driven by a framework hint computed from content markers (`from fastapi`/`FastAPI(` -> fastapi; `from flask`/`Flask(` -> flask; `aiohttp`/`RouteTableDef`/`add_get` -> aiohttp; `gin.`/`echo.New`/`fiber.New` for Go; `require('commander')`/`new Command(` for JS CLI; `clap` for Rust; and so on). The `framework` rides in the `endpoint` signal value so derivation and tests can assert which framework matched. Adding this key to the signal value does not touch the projected arch dict (signals live only in the store; `docs.py` reads only method+path), so the derive parity snapshot is unaffected on the fixture.
  - **Capability model.** A capability is derived (Tier 3) from signals, deduped by (component_id, kind, signature), evidence merged. `kind` in {api, cli, event, job}. `name` is human-facing (`GET /users/{id}`, command name, topic name). `detail_json` carries the structured contract (method+path+framework; command+flags+framework; topic+direction; job trigger). Evidence is `[{file, line, snippet}]`. Confidence tiers per I3: api and cli declarations are `certain` (parse-level framework-anchored facts); event and job declarations are `inferred` (heuristic). Owning component is the file's component (`_find_component_for_file`, the same resolver relationships.py uses). Defining symbol is linked where resolvable: the innermost stored symbol whose [line, end_line] contains the evidence line, else the nearest symbol starting within 3 lines below (the decorator-precedes-function case), else null.
  - **CLI flags attach to the nearest preceding command in the same file (inferred).** Precise flag-to-command association is beyond single-file regex for click/typer (decorator stacks) and commander (method chains), so each `cli_option` flag is attached to the `cli_command` with the greatest line <= the flag's line in the same file, else the file's first command. The command capability itself is `certain`; the flag list is data attached to it (the association heuristic is why cli confidence stays honest about the flag set). Per-framework CLI fixtures: click, typer, clap, commander (plus argparse/cobra/thor retained from P4-2).
  - **Events and jobs where signals allow (I marked inferred).** Events reuse the existing `queue_name` signals (topic/queue/channel literals, `.subscribe`/`.publish`) plus a small produce/consume direction inference from the matched verb; they become `event` capabilities. Jobs come from a new `job` signal (celery `@app.task`/`@shared_task`/`@periodic_task`, APScheduler `@scheduled_job`, `schedule.every`, Sidekiq `include Sidekiq::Worker`, cron-expression config) and become `job` capabilities. Both are `inferred`.
  - **Test-linkage groundwork (LENS-DESIGN L2).** For each capability, the pass scans the content of test files in the store (files `_is_test_file` classifies, reusing testing.py's classifier) for a textual reference to the capability's path (api) or command (cli); a hit records `{file, line}` in the capability's `detail_json.tests` with confidence `inferred`. This lands the data; the full linkage UI is P6-3.
  - **Projection: capabilities ride in the arch dict as optional keys.** Derivation attaches a `capabilities` list to each owning component dict (only when non-empty) and a flat top-level `capabilities` index to the arch dict. Both flow unchanged through the manifest (which keeps every key except the heavy `symbols`/`files` arrays) and the monolith. No `analyzer/project/` code change is required; the existing viewer ignores the new keys (backward-compatibility test asserts additive-only). The store `capabilities` table (present since P4-1) is the system of record; `_flush` clears and rewrites it each derive run, after components (FK order).
  - **Determinism (I4).** Capabilities are emitted sorted by id; component-level lists sorted by id; flags and evidence sorted. A capability id is `cap:<component_id>:<kind>:<signature-slug>`, content-derived, never order-derived.

### P5-2: Data entities and access edges
- Status: DONE (session program2/p5-entities, Opus 4.8; PR "P5-2: data entities and access edges")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md section 5 (Data entities), invariants I1, I3, I4; LENS-DESIGN.md section 4 (L3)
- Scope: parse ORM models (SQLAlchemy, Django, ActiveRecord, Prisma schema, SwiftData/CoreData where feasible), migrations, and standalone schemas into data_entities with fields; entity_access edges (read/write) from driver-usage and import signals; remove models/schemas/migrations from the content-exclusion list; ledger and confidence rules apply.

- Elaboration (this session, faithful to the scope above; no scope change):

  Recorded decisions:
  - **Extraction site is a new `analyzer/extract/entities.py` plus a thin `extract_entity_signals` wrapper in `signals.py`, mirroring the P5-1 frameworks.py -> signals.py split.** entities.py holds pure per-ORM / per-schema extractors returning `(value, match_start)` tuples; signals.py wraps them into `data_entity` SignalRecords. Entity extraction is parser-independent (pure regex over content), so it runs for parser-backed code files and for parser-less standalone schema files alike, and entity tests do not need the tree-sitter tier.
  - **The parse happens in Tier 1 as a `data_entity` signal; the assembly happens in Tier 3 (`analyzer/derive/entities.py`).** This is the same shape as capabilities (I1 deterministic skeleton): raw per-file observation in extraction, join and ownership resolution in derivation. `extract_entity_signals` is called from `runner._parse_worker` alongside `extract_signals`, so a file is still read once.
  - **ORM coverage.** Python: SQLAlchemy (declarative `class X(Base|db.Model|DeclarativeBase)` with `__tablename__` and `Column`/`mapped_column` fields), Django (`class X(models.Model)` with `models.XxxField`), Django migrations (`migrations.CreateModel(name=..., fields=[...])`), Alembic migrations (`op.create_table('t', sa.Column(...))`). Ruby: ActiveRecord model (`class X < ApplicationRecord|ActiveRecord::Base`), schema.rb tables (`create_table "t" do |t| ... t.string "c"`), Rails migrations (`create_table :t do |t| ... t.string :c`). Swift: SwiftData (`@Model class X { var f: T }`) and CoreData (`class X: NSManagedObject { @NSManaged var f }`, marked inferred as partial). TypeScript/JS: TypeORM (`@Entity() class X { @Column() f }`). Go: GORM structs (`type X struct { F T \`gorm:...\` }`). Standalone schemas: Prisma (`model X { f T }` in `.prisma`), SQL DDL (`CREATE TABLE t (...)` in `.sql`), JSON Schema (`.json` files that are clearly data-shaped: top-level `type:"object"` plus a non-empty `properties` map, which excludes ordinary config JSON).
  - **Prisma enumeration is a v2-only runner addition, not a shared LANGUAGE_MAP change.** `.sql` and `.json` are already enumerated (they carry a language and reach the worker with cached content, just no code parser); `.prisma` is not in LANGUAGE_MAP so it is excluded. runner.py adds a v2-only `_SCHEMA_ONLY_EXTENSIONS = {".prisma": "prisma"}` recognized in `_enumerate`, so `.prisma` files become candidates on the v2 path. LANGUAGE_MAP is untouched, so v1 scanner.py sees no change (v1 stability, parity snapshots green).
  - **Extraction tier version bumped `p4-extract/1` -> `p5-extract/1`.** Adding a new signal kind to extraction output means a warm store's cached facts would be missing `data_entity` signals; serving them would be silent staleness (violates "no silent anything"). Bumping the tier invalidates the content-hash cache so every file is re-extracted once after the upgrade, which is exactly what parser_version exists for. `test_extract.py` uses the imported symbol, not the literal, so it is unaffected.
  - **The content-exclusion inversion is applied ONLY in the v2 derivation path (`derive/roles.py`), gated so v1 is byte-stable.** `roles.py` defines `_V2_CONTENT_DIR_NAMES = CONTENT_DIR_NAMES - {"models", "migrations", "schemas"}` and `_is_content_only` consults that set instead of the shared `CONTENT_DIR_NAMES`. scanner.py (v1) still reads the shared constant unchanged. The parity fixtures contain no models/migrations/schemas directories, so the derive parity snapshots are unaffected (proven: `find tests/fixtures -type d -iname models -o -iname migrations -o -iname schemas` is empty).
  - **entity_access model.** For each entity, tokens are its class name (word-boundary in code) and its table name (string literal). A file that references the class name in usage (not an import line) yields a `certain` access edge (direct ORM usage); a file that only contains the table-name string yields an `inferred` edge (string-matched table reference), per the card's confidence rule. Mode is `write` when the reference line matches write verbs (`.save(`, `.create(`, `.add(`, `.insert`, `.update(`, `.delete(`, `.destroy`, `INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`, model instantiation `Name(`) else `read`. accessor_id is the owning component of the referencing file; evidence is `{file, line, snippet}`; the entity's own declaration lines are excluded so a definition is not counted as self-access. Edges dedupe by (accessor_id, entity_id, mode) with merged evidence.
  - **Entity identity and merge.** An entity id is `entity:<component_id>:<name-slug>`, content-derived (I4). Occurrences with the same (component, name) merge: evidence unions, fields union (so a model with no inline fields plus a migration/schema for the same name in the same component yields one entity with fields), and `kind` takes the most specific by priority model > table > migration > schema. Cross-component sources (for example a Rails model in app/models and its schema.rb table under db/) stay separate entities (documented limitation).
  - **Projection.** data_entities and entity_access ride the arch dict as optional keys exactly like capabilities: a flat top-level `data_entities` index and a flat top-level `entity_access` index, plus an optional per-component `data_entities` key set only when non-empty. Both flow unchanged through the manifest (which keeps every key except symbols/files) and the monolith; the existing viewer ignores them (backward-compatibility test). The store `data_entities` and `entity_access` tables (present since P4-1) are the system of record; `_flush` clears and rewrites them each derive run in FK order.
  - **Determinism (I4).** Entities are emitted id-sorted; access edges sorted by (accessor_id, entity_id, mode); fields and evidence sorted; ids content-derived.
  - **Multi-repo:** `derive/multi.py` does not merge entities into the multi output, consistent with the existing multi-repo capabilities/coverage/activity deferrals (single-repo v2 has full entities). Flagged in Discovered.

  2. `analyzer/extract/signals.py`: `extract_entity_signals(content, language, path)` wrapping the extractors into `data_entity` SignalRecords.
  3. `analyzer/extract/runner.py`: call `extract_entity_signals` in `_parse_worker`; recognize `.prisma` via `_SCHEMA_ONLY_EXTENSIONS`; bump `EXTRACT_TIER`.
  4. `analyzer/derive/entities.py` (new): `derive_entities(d)` builds data_entities (owner + defining symbol + merged fields/evidence) and entity_access edges (mode + confidence + evidence), records them on the Deriver.
  5. `analyzer/derive/context.py`: entity state. `analyzer/derive/pipeline.py`: run the pass; attach top-level `data_entities`/`entity_access` and per-component `data_entities`; flush to the store. `analyzer/derive/roles.py`: v2-only content-dir inversion.
  6. Tests (new `tests/test_entities.py`), derive parity mask update, dedicated `tests/fixtures/entities/` fixture, card update, PR.
