  - **Prisma/SQL/JSON enumeration.** `test_prisma_sql_json_files_are_parsed_not_excluded`: `.prisma` (a v2-only schema extension) plus `.sql` and `.json` land as `parsed` in the coverage ledger. `test_config_json_is_not_a_data_entity`: `config/package_like.json` (no top-level `properties`) is not an entity.
  - **Store landing.** `test_entities_land_in_store_with_fields_and_evidence`: the `User` row carries `fields_json` = the entity detail (fields + framework + table; the frozen-schema additive landing, the entity analog of capabilities' detail_json) and `evidence_json`. `test_defining_symbol_linked_where_resolvable`: the SQLAlchemy `User` model resolves to a defining symbol.
  - **Backward compatibility.** `test_entities_are_additive_optional_keys`: a repo with no ORM code emits `data_entities == []`, `entity_access == []`, and no component carries the `data_entities` key. `test_projection_carries_entities_and_old_viewer_ignores`: the projected monolith carries `data_entities`/`entity_access` additively while every pre-existing key stays readable; the manifest projection was verified to carry both top-level indexes (16 entities, 3 access edges) and the per-component key. The derive parity test still passes with `data_entities`/`entity_access` masked as enumerated difference D7.
  - **Determinism (I4).** `test_entity_emission_is_deterministic`: two extract+derive runs over the same fixture produce byte-identical `data_entities` and `entity_access` JSON. Ids are content-derived (`entity:<component>:<slug>`); entities id-sorted; access edges sorted by (accessor, entity, mode); fields and evidence sorted.
  - **Counts.** Dedicated fixture (`tests/fixtures/entities/`): 16 entities across 11 frameworks (sqlalchemy 2, django model+migration, alembic, activerecord model+table, rails, swiftdata, typeorm, gorm, prisma 2, sql 2, json-schema), 3 access edges (User read+write certain, invoices read inferred). This repo (engine=v2, in-memory store, whole tree incl. test fixtures): 22 entities (model 9, table 7, migration 5, schema 1; frameworks sql 6, django 3, sqlalchemy 2, activerecord 2, prisma 2, alembic 2, gorm/typeorm/swiftdata/rails/json-schema 1 each), 21 with fields, 12 with a defining-symbol link; 72 access edges (55 inferred table-string references across fixtures, 17 certain ORM-class usages; 42 read, 30 write). architecture.json (repo root) untouched.
  - **Repo-wide checks.** `pytest tests/ -q` -> 850 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`, unrelated). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` -> All checks passed. `pytest tests/test_entities.py -q` -> 29 passed. No viewer changes in scope (P5-3 lands the viewer types/tabs), so no npm checks.
  - Deviations: (1) The entity detail (fields + framework + table + symbol + inferred) rides in `data_entities.fields_json` because the frozen P4-1 schema gives that table no `detail` column; this is the additive, migration-free landing mirroring P5-1's use of capabilities' detail_json (I3/I4 preserved). (2) `analyzer/derive/multi.py` (multi-repo v2) does not merge entities into the merged output, consistent with the existing multi-repo capabilities/coverage/activity deferrals (single-repo v2 has full entities); flagged in Discovered below. (3) The content-exclusion inversion inverts only the dir-NAME rule; a data-lens directory dominated by content-extension files below the 80 percent content ratio is architectural on v2, but component discovery still requires a marker or two code files to form a component, so a pure low-code schema directory (like the fixture's `models/`) surfaces its entities under the nearest owning component rather than as its own component. The inversion's decision point is proven directly at `_is_content_only`. (4) `EXTRACT_TIER` bumped to invalidate the content-hash cache so warm stores re-extract once and never silently serve pre-P5-2 facts (no-silent-anything).

### P5-3: Projection and type extensions for capabilities and entities
- Status: DONE (session program2/p5-tabs, Opus 4.8; PR "P5-3: capability and data tabs")
- Model: Opus 4.8 (Sonnet 5 acceptable for the types/plumbing half)
- Scope: optional keys in manifest and detail shards; viewer/src/types.ts additions; DetailPanel gains Capabilities and Data tabs (list-level, lens views come in Phase 6); backward-compatibility test that old datasets render unchanged.

- Elaboration (this session, faithful to the scope above; no scope change):

  Recorded decisions:
  - **The projection half was already delivered by P5-1 and P5-2; this card is the viewer consumption side.** Capabilities, data entities, and entity access already ride the arch dict as optional keys (flat top-level `capabilities` / `data_entities` / `entity_access` indexes plus per-component `capabilities` / `data_entities` keys) and flow unchanged through the manifest and monolith. No analyzer or projection change was needed here; P5-3 lands the `viewer/src/types.ts` additions and the two DetailPanel tabs that consume them. Territory was strictly viewer-side and disjoint from Stream B (no `viewer/src/lenses/`, `store.ts` lens/graph state, `ArchitectureGraph.tsx`, `App.tsx`, or `utils/search.ts` touched).
  - **Types match the emitted `to_dict` shapes exactly.** `Capability` (id, component_id nullable, kind, name, `detail`, evidence, confidence) with a `CapabilityDetail` covering all four kinds (api method/path, cli command/flags, event topic/direction, job name/trigger; shared framework/symbol; `tests` for the L2 linkage). `DataEntity` (id, component_id, name, kind, framework, fields, evidence; optional table/symbol/inferred). `EntityAccess` (accessor_id, entity_id, mode, confidence, evidence). Shared `Evidence` (file, line nullable, snippet). Note: the projected key is `detail` (the store column is `detail_json`); test linkage rides at `detail.tests`. All additive: `capabilities?` / `data_entities?` on `Component`, and `capabilities?` / `data_entities?` / `entity_access?` on `Architecture`.
  - **Tabs gate on presence, so old datasets render identically (I2 spirit, backward compatibility).** The Capabilities tab appears only when `component.capabilities` is non-empty; the Data tab appears when the component owns entities or has any outgoing access edge in `architecture.entity_access`. A dataset carrying neither key has no owned entities and no access edges, so neither tab appears and every pre-existing tab is unchanged (proven by two backward-compat tests plus the full unchanged suite).
  - **Ranked, not just rendered (I11).** Capabilities group by kind in the fixed order api, cli, event, job, then by name within a kind. Entities sort by kind (model > table > migration > schema) then name. Access lists sort certain-before-inferred then by counterparty name then mode. The ranking is load-bearing (fail-before proof below).
  - **Evidence and test linkage per L2.** Each capability, entity, and access edge renders its evidence as `file:line` with a clickable GitHub source link reusing the existing `SourceLink` / `getSourceUrl` pattern. Confidence is surfaced only when not certain (an "inferred" badge; certain facts carry none, per the card). Where `detail.tests` is present, a capability shows a "Tested by" block linking each exercising test file (the L2 proof bridge; the full linkage lens is P6-3).

- Evidence:
  - **Files.** `viewer/src/types.ts` (additive optional types: `Evidence`, `CapabilityTestLink`, `CapabilityDetail`, `Capability`, `EntityField`, `DataEntity`, `EntityAccess`; optional `capabilities?` / `data_entities?` on `Component`; optional `capabilities?` / `data_entities?` / `entity_access?` on `Architecture`). `viewer/src/components/DetailPanel.tsx` (Tab union + `TAB_KEYS` gains `capabilities`/`data`; tabs array gates both new tabs; render switch; new `CapabilitiesTab`, `DataTab`, and helpers `hasDataTabContent`, `buildComponentNameMap`, `ConfidenceBadge`, `EvidenceLinks`, `MethodBadge`, `AccessRow`). Tests: `viewer/src/__tests__/DetailPanel.capdata.test.tsx` (new, 8 cases). No file outside the P5-3 viewer territory was touched (git status: only types.ts, DetailPanel.tsx, the new test, and this card); architecture.json untouched.
  - **Backward compatibility (construction proof).** `shows no Capabilities or Data tab when the dataset carries neither key`: an architecture with no capability/entity keys renders Overview and Files unchanged and neither new tab appears. `shows no Data tab when only an unrelated component owns entities`: an `entity_access` edge whose accessor is a different component leaves the selected component with no Data tab. The full suite (157 tests, 19 files) stays green, so no existing render changed.
  - **Capabilities tab (construction proof).** `groups capabilities by kind in api, cli, event, job order`: four capabilities supplied out of order (job, api, api, cli) render grouped with headers ordered API before CLI before Jobs, and GET /users/{id} before POST /users within the API group. `renders method/path for api, flags for cli, and marks inferred confidence`: the GET method badge and path, the `--env`/`--force` flags, and the job's inferred badge all render (certain api/cli carry none). `renders evidence as a well-formed GitHub source link and shows test linkage`: evidence `src/api/routes.py:20` links to `https://github.com/acme/demo/blob/main/src/api/routes.py#L20`, and the L2 "Tested by (1)" block links `tests/test_users.py:12`.
  - **Data tab (construction proof).** `lists owned entities ranked by kind with kind badges, fields, and evidence links`: model badge before table badge, User before invoices, fields `email: String` / `id: Integer` / bare `amount`, and a well-formed models.py source link. `shows who else touches an owned entity, both directions`: the Worker's read of the API-owned User surfaces under "Accessed by (1)", and the API's own inferred write of invoices surfaces under "Reads / Writes (1)". `appears for a component that only accesses entities it does not own`: the Worker (owns nothing, reads User) still gets a Data tab with the outgoing edge and no owned-entities header.
  - **Fail-before proof (WORK-PLAN principle 2, for the I11 ranking).** Reversing `CAP_KIND_ORDER` to `["job", "event", "cli", "api"]` makes `groups capabilities by kind...` FAIL (the `API (2)` header no longer precedes `CLI (1)` / `Jobs (1)`); restored to `["api", "cli", "event", "job"]` it PASSES. Command and both outcomes reproduced this session.
  - **Viewer verification.** `npm test -- --run` -> 157 passed (19 files), including the 8 new cases. `npm run lint` -> 0 warnings. `npx tsc -b` -> clean. `npm run build` -> built (the >500 kB chunk-size advisory is pre-existing and unrelated).
  - **Analyzer untouched.** `pytest tests/ -q` -> 849 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`), 1 failed: `test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents`. That single failure is the expected git-worktree environmental artifact: in a linked worktree `.git` is a gitlink file, not a directory, so the `.git`-directory ledger-row assertion finds no directory row. It is unrelated to this viewer-only change (no analyzer file was modified) and does not reproduce on a normal checkout.
  - Deviations: none. Projection/manifest optional keys were already delivered by P5-1/P5-2, so this card added no analyzer code, consistent with its scope (the viewer types/tabs half).

### P5-4: Git activity extraction (Activity lens data)
- Status: DONE (session program2/p5-activity, Opus 4.8; PR "P5-4: git activity extraction")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 4 (L5) and 6; TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md invariants I2, I4, I6, I7
- Scope: per-file churn series, author contribution shares, and co-change pairs from git log into the store (additive schema_version migration); cached by commit range; ledger-accounted; wholly language-agnostic. No dependency on Phase 5 semantics; may be scheduled immediately after the Phase 4 gate as the first NEW lens data source.

- Elaboration (this session, faithful to the scope above; no scope change):

  Recorded decisions:
  - **Schema (SCHEMA_VERSION 1 -> 2, additive migration).** Four new tables, all with deterministic natural sort keys, keyed by repo-relative git path (not files.id, because git history references deleted and renamed paths that are not in the current files table, and files.id is reassigned every extraction run):
    - `file_activity(path PK, commit_count, lines_added, lines_removed, first_seen, last_modified)` per-file churn totals plus first-seen and last-modified author dates.
    - `file_activity_period(path, period, commit_count, lines_added, lines_removed, PK(path, period))` where `period` is a calendar `YYYY-MM` bucket. Calendar buckets (not "last N days") keep the by-period data byte-deterministic across runs: they depend only on git history, never on wall-clock now().
    - `file_author(path, author_key, author_name, commit_count, lines_added, lines_removed, PK(path, author_key))` author contribution per file. `author_key` is the lowercased email (identity), `author_name` the display name.
    - `cochange_pair(path_a, path_b, cochange_count, PK(path_a, path_b))` with `path_a < path_b`, co-commit counts.
    - Activity provenance in `meta`: `activity_git` (0/1), `activity_shallow` (0/1), `activity_head` (HEAD sha last processed), `activity_commits` (total commits processed), `activity_first_commit` / `activity_last_commit` (author dates).
    - Migration mechanism added to `analyzer/store/db.py`: a `MIGRATIONS` registry in `schema.py` and version-gap application in `_create_schema` (was: raise on any mismatch). A cold store gets all tables from `schema_statements` in one shot; a warm v1 store gets the activity tables via the migration and its `schema_version` bumped to 2. `clear_extraction_facts` does NOT clear the activity tables (like `extraction_cache`, they are the incremental baseline; the activity pass owns their lifecycle).
  - **Extraction unit: one commit = one logical change unit.** Chosen over per-day because it is git-native, deterministic, and unambiguous (no timezone or day-boundary decision). Merge commits are excluded (`git log --no-merges`): `git log --numstat` emits no diff for merges by default, so counting them would only add noise. Co-change pairs are generated only for commits touching at most `MAX_FILES_PER_COMMIT_FOR_COUPLING` (30) files; a commit touching more is a bulk or mechanical change and still counts toward churn and authorship but does not manufacture O(n^2) spurious coupling (the CodeScene cap). Recorded as module constants.
  - **Placement: `analyzer/extract/activity.py` (extraction-adjacent).** It reads `git log`, not source files, so it does not belong in the derive tier (whose zero-source-reads instrumentation guards file opens) and does not violate that rule: git history is environment/provenance, read once at extraction time, exactly as the projection tier is allowed to read `.git` for the repo URL (P4-3 precedent). It runs in `run_v2` after `extract_repo` (it needs the `files` table to know which current paths exist) and before `derive_all`.
  - **Aggregation + projection shape: dedicated `activity.json` shard.** `analyzer/project/activity.py` aggregates per-file activity to component granularity using `store.component_files()` (populated by `derive_all`'s flush, which runs before projection). It emits a dedicated `activity.json` shard (parallel to `coverage.json`) carrying the provenance block, ranked component hotspots with knowledge (author shares, knowledge islands at top-author-share >= 0.95, bus factor as the fewest authors covering >= 50% of commits), ranked component-pair and file-pair coupling above a support floor (`MIN_COCHANGE_SUPPORT` = 2), and per-file detail. A lightweight `activity` summary (top hotspots plus provenance) rides in the manifest like the coverage summary. Monolith mode embeds the full activity dict under an `activity` key. All optional; the viewer ignores them until P6-5 (backward-compatibility test).
  - **Cache by commit range (I6).** `meta.activity_head` records the HEAD sha last processed. A warm run whose HEAD is unchanged processes zero commits (early return, proven by a test). When HEAD advanced and the old head is an ancestor of the new (fast-forward / normal history growth), only `<old_head>..HEAD` is processed and merged additively (all counts are additive over commits, so an incremental merge equals a full reprocess). When the old head is not an ancestor (rebase, force-push, or a shallow boundary shift), the activity tables are cleared and history is fully reprocessed (never a silent partial). Recorded.
  - **Graceful absence.** A non-git root populates no activity tables, records `activity_git=0`, and emits no `activity` projection key (no error). A shallow clone produces truthful partial data with `activity_shallow=1` surfaced in the provenance block, never a pretend-full history.
  - **Ledger accounting.** The pass reads git, not source files, so it adds no `coverage` (file-disposition) rows. It accounts for itself in the `meta` provenance block (git availability, shallow marker, commit count, processed range), which the projection surfaces. This is the honest reading of "ledger-account the pass": the coverage ledger stays a per-source-file ledger; the activity pass states exactly what git history it consumed.

- Do:
  1. `analyzer/store/schema.py`: bump `SCHEMA_VERSION` to 2, add the activity DDL (tables above, `IF NOT EXISTS`), add a `MIGRATIONS` registry. `analyzer/store/db.py`: apply version-gap migrations in `_create_schema`; add writer/reader helpers for the activity tables (deterministic ordering) and activity-meta accessors; leave activity tables out of `clear_extraction_facts`.
  2. `analyzer/extract/activity.py`: detect git, detect shallow, read `git log --no-merges -M --numstat` with a NUL-delimited header format, parse renames from numstat's `=>` encoding and canonicalize historical paths to current names, cache by commit range, write the four tables plus provenance. `extract_activity(root, store)` returns a small result object.
  3. `analyzer/project/activity.py`: `build_activity(store)` -> the full activity dict (provenance, ranked component hotspots + knowledge, coupling, per-file detail) or `None` when no activity data; `activity_manifest_summary(activity)` -> the lightweight manifest slice.
  4. `analyzer/project/pipeline.py`: emit `activity.json`, the manifest `activity` summary, and the monolith `activity` key; `analyzer/project/manifest.py` and `monolith.py` carry the key. `analyzer/project/run.py` (v2 path only): call `extract_activity` after `extract_repo`, print a one-line activity summary.
  5. Tests + card update + PR.

- Accept:
  - Schema migrates a v1 store to v2 additively (existing rows intact, new tables present, version bumped); a cold store opens at v2 directly.
  - On a synthetic git repo with scripted commits by two authors, the extracted churn, author shares, knowledge islands, bus factor, and co-change pairs match hand-computed expectations exactly.
  - Renames are followed: activity for a renamed file accrues to its current path.
  - A shallow clone is marked `activity_shallow=1` and its data is not presented as full.
  - A second run over an unchanged HEAD processes zero new commits (cache-by-range).
  - A non-git root yields no activity data and no error; the viewer-facing projection omits the key and old datasets are unaffected (backward-compatibility).
  - Projection is byte-deterministic given the same store.

- Verify:
  - `pytest tests/test_activity.py -q` (new); the fail-before/passes-after proof for the core extraction test recorded in Evidence.
  - `pytest tests/ -q` green (no regressions); `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` clean.
  - A cold vs warm activity extraction over the same repo yields identical tables (determinism).

- Evidence:
  - **Files.** Schema/migration: `analyzer/store/schema.py` (SCHEMA_VERSION 1->2, `_ACTIVITY_DDL`, `MIGRATIONS`), `analyzer/store/db.py` (version-gap migration in `_create_schema`; activity writers/readers `merge_file_activity`/`merge_file_activity_periods`/`merge_file_authors`/`merge_cochange_pairs`/`clear_activity`/`apply_activity_renames`/`file_activity`/`file_authors`/`cochange_pairs`/`has_activity`; activity tables left out of `clear_extraction_facts`). Extraction: `analyzer/extract/activity.py` (new), `analyzer/extract/__init__.py`. Projection: `analyzer/project/activity.py` (new), `analyzer/project/pipeline.py`, `manifest.py`, `monolith.py`, `__init__.py`. CLI wiring (v2 path): `analyzer/project/run.py`. Tests: `tests/test_activity.py` (new, 19 cases), `tests/test_store.py` (migration + activity-helper cases). No file outside the P5-4 territory was touched (verified `git status`: store/, extract/activity.py, project/, tests/, the P5-4 card only).
  - **Recorded decisions**: SCHEMA_VERSION 1->2 additive migration (four activity tables, path-keyed); one commit = one logical change unit (`--no-merges`, coupling capped at 30 files/commit); placement in `analyzer/extract/activity.py` (extraction-adjacent, reads git not source); dedicated `activity.json` shard plus a manifest `activity` summary and monolith `activity` key; cache by `meta.activity_head` (fast-forward incremental with rename migration, else full reprocess); shallow marker in provenance; ledger-accounted via the meta provenance block (the pass reads git, so it adds no per-file coverage rows). Full rationale in the Elaboration block above.
  - **Synthetic-fixture proof** (`test_extraction_matches_scripted_history`, `test_projection_knowledge_island_and_bus_factor`): a tmp git repo with 5 scripted commits by Alice and Bob at fixed dates, `core.py` renamed to `engine.py` at the end. Hand-computed and asserted exactly: `engine.py` commit_count 4 / lines_added 16 (core.py's whole history canonicalized onto it, no stray `core.py` row); `util.py` 2 / 6; `solo.py` 1; author split engine.py Alice 2 / Bob 2, util.py Alice-only; co-change `(engine.py, util.py)=2` (the only pair, from c1+c2; the rename+edit c5 touches one file so makes no pair); calendar periods 2020-01/02/03 exact. Component aggregation: Core (engine.py+solo.py) commit_count 5, top_author_share 3/5, knowledge_island False, bus_factor 1; Util single-author island True, share 1.0; hotspot ranking puts Core first with score 4*16+1*4. `_bus_factor` unit-tested across 6 vectors including a bus_factor-2 case; `_split_rename` across the 5 numstat rename encodings.
  - **Fail-before proof** (WORK-PLAN principle 2, for the new rename-canonicalization logic): disabling `_split_rename` (return `(path, path)` always) makes `test_extraction_matches_scripted_history` FAIL (`assert 'core.py' not in act` -> core.py splits from engine.py: core.py commit_count 3, engine.py 2); restored, it PASSES. Command and both outcomes reproduced this session. As a whole-feature guard, the module did not exist on the parent commit, so the entire suite fails to import there.
  - **Rename handling**: covered by the fixture proof above and by `test_incremental_fast_forward_equals_full_reprocess_across_rename`, which advances a clone from the pre-rename commit to HEAD and asserts the incremental store snapshot (activity, authors, periods, co-change) equals a single full run byte-for-byte, exercising `apply_activity_renames` migrating stored history onto the current name.
  - **Cache-by-range proof** (`test_second_run_over_unchanged_head_processes_zero_commits`): a second `extract_activity` over an unchanged HEAD returns `mode="unchanged"`, `commits_processed=0`, and leaves the tables byte-identical. Reproduced on THIS repo through the real CLI: cold run `Activity: 115 commits processed (full)`; warm re-run against the same `--store` `Activity: 0 commits processed (unchanged)`, and the two `activity.json` outputs are byte-identical (determinism).
  - **Shallow-clone marker** (`test_shallow_clone_is_marked`): `git clone --depth 1 file://...` yields `result.shallow True`, `meta.activity_shallow="1"`, `provenance.shallow True`, and only the tip commit processed; partial history is never presented as full.
  - **Graceful absence / backward compatibility** (`test_non_git_root_produces_no_activity_and_no_error`, `test_projection_omits_activity_key_without_git`): a non-git root writes nothing (`activity_git="0"`, `has_activity()` False, no error), `build_activity` returns None, and a full v2 projection over a non-git tree omits both `activity.json` and the manifest `activity` key, so old datasets and the current viewer are unaffected.
  - **Real-repo end-to-end** (`python analyze.py . -o <scratch> --split --store <scratch>`): engine=v2 cold run produced `activity.json` (357 KB) with 115 commits, 38 components ranked by hotspot (data, components, src, analyzer leading), 54 cross-component coupling pairs (viewer/src <-> viewer/src/components 115, analyzer <-> tests 46, App.tsx <-> store.ts 12), knowledge islands and bus factors surfaced truthfully on this mostly single-author repo. `architecture.json` untouched throughout.
  - **Repo-wide checks**: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 794 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`, unrelated). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` -> All checks passed. No viewer changes (P6-5 consumes this data later), so no npm checks in scope.
  - **Migration verified** (`test_schema_migration_v1_to_v2_is_additive`): a simulated v1 store (activity tables dropped, `schema_version="1"`, a pre-existing `files` row) opened by current code gains the activity tables, keeps its row, and bumps to `schema_version="2"`. The prior `test_schema_version_mismatch_raises` was updated to `test_schema_version_newer_than_code_raises`: a store NEWER than the code still hard-errors (no silent truncation); an OLDER store now migrates. This is a direct consequence of adding the migration path this task requires.
  - Deviations: (1) CLI wiring landed in `analyzer/project/run.py` (the v2 entry point, inside the project/ territory) rather than `analyzer/cli.py`; cli.py only routes to `run_v2` and has no per-tier hook, so run.py is the correct and minimal insertion point. No arg-parser change was needed (activity always runs on the v2 single-repo path; it is a graceful no-op off git). (2) Multi-repo v2 (`--config`) does not extract activity, consistent with its existing coverage/incremental deferrals (see Discovered row below).

### P5-5: Rule extraction (Rules lens data)
- Status: DONE (session program2/p5-rules, Opus 4.8, Stream A; PR "P5-5: rule extraction")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 4 (L6) and 6; TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md invariants I1, I3, I4
- Scope: deterministic detection of rule-bearing code (validation clusters, domain conditionals, calculations with domain constants, decision-shaped branching) into typed rule entities (validation, calculation, policy, io) with inputs/outputs, trigger context, evidence, and confidence. Plain-language statements are Phase 7 enrichment work, never extraction (I1).

- Elaboration (this session, faithful to the scope above; no scope change):

  Recorded decisions:
  - **Detection site is a new `analyzer/extract/rules.py` plus a thin `extract_rule_signals` wrapper in `signals.py`, mirroring the P5-1 frameworks.py -> signals.py and P5-2 entities.py splits.** rules.py holds pure per-language, per-kind detectors returning `(value, match_start)` tuples over a single file's content; signals.py wraps them into `rule` SignalRecords; runner calls `extract_rule_signals` in `_parse_worker` alongside `extract_signals`/`extract_entity_signals`, so a file is still read exactly once. Detection is per-file (the whole file content is in hand at extraction time), so guard-clause clusters, multi-branch conditionals, and formulae are all detectable at Tier 1 as raw observations.
  - **The parse happens in Tier 1 as a `rule` signal; the assembly happens in Tier 3 (`analyzer/derive/rules.py`).** Same shape as capabilities and entities (I1 deterministic skeleton): raw per-file observation in extraction, owner/symbol/trigger/entity resolution and cross-linking in derivation. `derive_rules(d)` runs after `derive_capabilities` and `derive_entities` so it can link a rule's trigger context to the enclosing capability (when the enclosing symbol defines one) and an IO rule to the P5-2 entity whose field it constrains.
  - **The four rule kinds and their detection anchors (precision over recall; thresholds recorded):**
    - **validation.** (a) declared validators, confidence `certain`: pydantic `@validator`/`@field_validator`/`@root_validator`/`@model_validator`, Django `clean_<field>`/`clean` methods, Rails `validates`/`validate`, and zod/joi field chains (`name: z.string().email()`, `name: Joi.string().required()`) that carry at least one constraint method. (b) guard-clause validation, confidence `inferred`: an `if <condition>` (one-line or two-line body) that raises/throws, accepted ONLY when the raised type is in a validation allowlist (ValueError, ValidationError, TypeError, AssertionError, HTTPException/HTTPError, BadRequest*, InvalidArgument*, ArgumentError, or any name ending `ValidationError`/`ValidationException`/`ValidationError!`) AND the condition is validation-shaped (a comparison, membership, negation, `is [not] None`, `len(`, or a regex/`.match`). A bare re-raise, a raise of a non-validation error, or an `if` with no raise is not a rule (kills the noise class).
    - **calculation**, confidence `inferred`: an assignment or `return` whose expression contains an arithmetic operator (`+ - * / %`) over at least one named identifier, accepted ONLY when a domain-named target or operand anchors it (fee, rate, tax, total, price, amount, discount, cost, threshold, limit, interest, balance, salary, wage, score, commission, penalty, charge, subtotal, vat, margin, bonus, quantity, duration, ...). Index/counter math (`i = i + 1`, `count += 1`) has no domain anchor and is rejected.
    - **policy**, confidence `certain` for declared permission gates / `inferred` for shape-matched branching: (a) permission/authorization anchors: functions named `can_*`/`is_allowed*`/`has_permission*`/`authorize*`/`check_*_permission`, decorators `@permission_required`/`@login_required`/`@requires_permission`/`@requires_role`, Rails `before_action :authorize`, and `authorize(`/`.authorize!`/`can?(`/`has_perm*` calls; (b) decision-table-shaped branching: a `match`/`switch` over a domain subject with >= 2 `case` labels, or an `if/elif`(Python)/`if/elsif`(Ruby) chain with >= 2 `elif`/`elsif` arms. A plain if/else (one branch) is not a policy rule.
    - **io**, confidence `certain` (schema/field-declared): declarative field bounds and format constraints on model/schema fields: Django field `max_length=`/`MaxValueValidator`/`MinValueValidator`/`RegexValidator`/`null=False`, SQLAlchemy `Column(..., nullable=False)`/`CheckConstraint`, SQL DDL `NOT NULL`/`CHECK (...)`/`VARCHAR(n)`, and Prisma `@db.VarChar(n)`/`@default`/`@unique`. Each carries the constrained field name so derivation can link it to the P5-2 entity that owns the field.
  - **Rule value (extraction).** `{kind, summary, inputs, outputs, anchor, framework, confidence, field}`. `summary` is a MECHANICAL rendering of the code (the condition text and outcome, e.g. `raise ValueError when not email`, `total = subtotal + tax`, `switch(status) -> [active, closed, pending]`, `max_length=200 on title`), never a natural-language explanation (that is P7, invariant I1). `inputs`/`outputs` are extracted identifier/field names where locatable. `field` is set for io rules (the constrained field). Every signal carries the match line.
  - **Rule identity and derivation (Tier 3).** id is `rule:<component_id>:<kind>:<slug>-<hash8>` where the hash is over `(file, line, summary)`, so ids are content-and-path-derived and stable across runs, never discovery-order-derived (I4). Owner is the file's component (`_find_component_for_file`, the resolver capabilities/entities use). The defining/enclosing symbol is resolved via the shared `_resolve_symbol` (innermost containing symbol, else nearest following, the decorator case). Trigger context: when the enclosing symbol id equals a capability's `detail.symbol`, the rule records `trigger.capability = <cap id>` (the route/command this rule fires under); otherwise `trigger.symbol = <symbol id>` where resolvable. Entity link: an io rule whose `field` matches a field of an entity declared in the same file records `entity = <entity id>` (Data-lens L3 cross-reference). Evidence is `{file, line, snippet}`. Emission is deterministic: rules id-sorted, inputs/outputs/evidence sorted.
  - **Store: rules table via the established schema_version migration (SCHEMA_VERSION 2 -> 3), following the P5-4 precedent.** The frozen P4-1 schema had no rules table; `MIGRATIONS[3] = _RULES_DDL` adds `rules(id, component_id, kind, summary, detail_json, evidence_json, confidence)` (the inputs/outputs/anchor/framework/trigger/symbol/entity/field detail rides in `detail_json`, the additive payload-column pattern P5-1/P5-2 use for capabilities/entities). A warm v2 store migrates additively and bumps to 3; a cold store opens at 3. `add_rule` writer and `rules()` deterministic reader added; `_flush` clears+rewrites rules (FK to components, dropped before components). Symbol/entity/capability links are soft references in `detail_json`, not FKs, so flush ordering stays simple.
  - **Extraction tier bumped `p5-extract/1` -> `p5-extract/2`.** A new `rule` signal kind means a warm store's cached facts would be missing rule signals; serving them would be silent staleness (violates "no silent anything", I2). Bumping the tier invalidates the content-hash cache so every file is re-extracted once after the upgrade.
  - **Projection.** rules ride the arch dict as optional keys exactly like capabilities/entities: a flat top-level `rules` index plus an optional per-component `rules` key (set only when non-empty). Both flow unchanged through the manifest (keeps every key except symbols/files), the detail shards (per-component key rides in the component dict), and the monolith; the existing viewer ignores them (backward-compatibility test). The store `rules` table is the system of record.
  - **Determinism (I4).** rules emitted id-sorted; inputs/outputs/evidence sorted; ids content-derived; every extractor is position-ordered (no set iteration).

- Do:

  2. `analyzer/extract/signals.py`: `extract_rule_signals(content, language, path)` wrapping detectors into `rule` SignalRecords.
  3. `analyzer/extract/runner.py`: call `extract_rule_signals` in `_parse_worker`; bump `EXTRACT_TIER`.

  5. `analyzer/derive/rules.py` (new): `derive_rules(d)` builds rule entities (owner + enclosing symbol + trigger/capability + entity link + merged evidence), records them on the Deriver.
  6. `analyzer/derive/context.py`: rules state. `analyzer/derive/pipeline.py`: run the pass after capabilities+entities; attach top-level `rules` and per-component `rules`; flush to the store. `analyzer/derive/__init__` unaffected.
  7. Tests (`tests/test_rules.py`), parity mask update (D8), `tests/fixtures/rules/` fixture, card update, PR.

  - A noise fixture of ordinary control flow (loop guards, internal null checks/early returns, bare error-handling re-raises, index/counter math, single if/else) yields ZERO rules.
  - Rules carry trigger context (enclosing symbol, linked to the capability where the enclosing symbol defines one) and io rules link to the P5-2 entity whose field they constrain, where resolvable.
  - Rules land in the store (migration v2 -> v3 additive; cold store opens at v3) with detail and evidence.
  - Projections carry `rules` as optional keys; an old dataset (no rules) projects/renders unchanged (backward-compatibility test).
  - Emission is deterministic (two derives over the same store byte-identical).

    | Kind | Detection anchors | Confidence | Languages proven |
    |---|---|---|---|

    | io | Django field `max_length=`/`null=False`/`MaxValueValidator`/`RegexValidator`; SQLAlchemy `Column(nullable=False)`/`CheckConstraint`; SQL DDL `NOT NULL`/`CHECK`/`VARCHAR(n)`/`DEFAULT`; Prisma `@db.VarChar`/`@default`/`@unique`; TS class-validator `@MaxLength`/`@IsEmail`/...; Rails schema `t.<type> :col, limit:/null:/default:` | certain (schema/field-declared) | Python, TypeScript, Ruby, SQL |

  - **Thresholds recorded (precision over recall).** validation guards require a raise/throw of an allowlisted validation exception (a bare re-raise or a non-validation error is not a rule); calculation requires an explicit arithmetic operator in the RHS with a domain-named anchor (augmented `+=` on a non-domain target, index/counter math, and `x = y * 2` are rejected); policy branching requires >=2 elif/elsif arms or >=2 case labels (a single if/else is never a rule); io links to an entity only when the entity is declared in the SAME file (a same-component field-name-collision fallback was rejected).
  - **Noise-test proof (the precision guarantee).** `test_noise_python_yields_zero_rules`, `test_noise_typescript_yields_zero_rules`, `test_noise_ruby_yields_zero_rules` each feed a file of ordinary control flow (loop guards `if i >= len: break`, internal null checks `if obj is None: continue/return`, index/counter math `count += 1` / `idx = idx + 1` / `x = y * 2`, bare re-raise and `raise RuntimeError('internal')` error handling, and single if/else) and assert `extract_rules(...) == []`. All three return exactly 0. `test_internal_error_raise_is_not_validation` proves a non-validation raise (`raise RuntimeError('upstream failed')` on `if not resp.ok`) is NOT a rule.
  - **Fail-before proof (WORK-PLAN principle 2, for the load-bearing raise allowlist).** In a throwaway subprocess, adding `RuntimeError` to `analyzer.extract.rules._PY_VALIDATION_EXC` makes the internal-error guard `if not resp.ok: raise RuntimeError('upstream failed')` LEAK as a validation rule (`raise RuntimeError when not resp.ok`, 1 rule where 0 is expected); with the shipped allowlist it is 0. The allowlist is therefore the load-bearing filter that separates input validation from internal error handling. Command and both outcomes reproduced this session (the real module is untouched; the monkeypatch lived only in the subprocess).

The derive parity guard `test_polyglot_diff_vs_snapshot_is_only_enumerated_differences` (and its multi twin) still passes with `rules` masked as enumerated difference D8.

Ids are content-and-path-derived (`rule:<component>:<kind>:<anchor-slug>-<hash8(file,line,summary)>`); rules id-sorted; inputs/outputs/evidence sorted; every extractor is position-ordered (no set iteration).

(2) The io->entity cross-link is same-file-only by deliberate choice (a same-component fallback mis-linked a DTO field to an unrelated table sharing the field name); precision over recall, proven by `test_io_link_prefers_same_file_entity_not_field_collision`. (3) `analyzer/derive/multi.py` (multi-repo v2) does not merge rules into the merged output, consistent with the existing multi-repo capabilities/entities/coverage/activity deferrals (single-repo v2 has full rules); flagged in Discovered. (4) `EXTRACT_TIER` bumped to `p5-extract/2` so warm stores re-extract once and never silently serve pre-P5-5 facts (no-silent-anything).

### P5-6: Correlation extraction (clones, orphans, concerns)
- Status: DONE (session program2/p5-correlations, Opus 4.8, Stream A; PR "P5-6: correlation extraction")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md section 9; invariants I1 (deterministic skeleton, no AI invention), I3 (evidence-bearing), I11/I15 (findings ranked, evidenced, verification-gated); WORK-PLAN-2 section 6 (elaborate before implementing).
- Scope: near-duplicate clone clusters via token-stream fingerprinting over tree-sitter tokens; orphan detection (components and concern members unreachable from entry points); concern membership from signals (imports, API usage, naming) and clone clusters. Findings land as typed store entities with members, evidence, confidence, and verification status. Concern naming and intent conformance are P7-4.
- Elaboration (2026-07-13, no scope change):
  1. **Token-stream hook (parser tier, additive).** `TreeSitterParser.token_stream(content)` returns leaf tokens as `(line, norm, raw)` in source order, or `None` when tree-sitter is unavailable (the fragment tier then skips the file; regex-only files never participate, recorded). Normalization for type-2: comments dropped, string/number/boolean literals collapse to a `LIT` placeholder (so renamed constants match), identifiers collapse to an `ID` placeholder, and punctuation/operators/keywords keep their literal text (anonymous tree-sitter leaves, so structure is preserved). Type-1 (exact) is the special case where the raw tokens also match. No change to any existing parser method; the hook is only called by P5-6 extraction.
  2. **Clone-fragment extraction (Tier 1, `extract/clones.py`).** For each function/method symbol whose normalized token count is at least MIN_FRAGMENT_TOKENS (50), emit one `clone_fragment` signal carrying: start line, end line, ntokens, `norm_hash` (blake2b over the normalized token sequence), `raw_hash` (over the raw sequence), and `fps` (winnowing fingerprints over normalized K-grams). Winnowing (K=5-gram, W=4 window) guarantees any shared run of at least K+W-1=8 tokens shares a fingerprint, at roughly 2/(W+1) of the k-grams kept. Fingerprints are blake2b-derived (stable across processes; never Python `hash`, which is PYTHONHASHSEED-salted, I4). Wired into `_parse_worker` after the rule signals so a file is still read once; `EXTRACT_TIER` bumps p5-extract/2 -> p5-extract/3 (invalidates the cache so warm stores re-extract once, no silent stale facts, I2). Class/container symbols are skipped (methods are fragmented individually), so parent/child spans do not double-count.
  3. **Correlation derivation (Tier 3, `derive/correlations.py`, reads only the store).** (a) CLONE CLUSTERS: read `clone_fragment` signals, resolve each fragment's owning component. Build an inverted fingerprint index; drop stop-fingerprints whose document frequency exceeds MAX_DF (appear in > 40 fragments OR > 20% of fragments) so idioms do not create giant candidate buckets. Union-find over candidate pairs (fragments sharing a surviving fingerprint) whose fingerprint-set Jaccard is at least SIM_THRESHOLD (0.80), plus a direct union of all fragments sharing a `norm_hash` (whole-fragment type-1/2 clones). Emit clusters of at least 2 fragments spanning at least 2 files. Each cluster: members (file, symbol, line range, component), similarity class exact (all `raw_hash` equal) | renamed (all `norm_hash` equal, raw differs) | similar (type-3 near-dup), and size (member count + representative token length). (b) ORPHANS: leaf components (no child components) with no incoming edge, no `entry_points` and a non-entry type, no owned capability, no testing data, not root, not vendored. Symbol-level orphans are deferred: the store has no symbol-granularity cross-reference graph, so "cheaply resolvable" is not satisfied at symbol level (recorded deviation). (c) CONCERNS: deterministic membership sets over existing facts, at component granularity, with mechanical slug ids: `concern:logging` (logger imports), `concern:auth` (auth-library imports plus `policy` rules from P5-5), `concern:persistence` (`db_driver` signals, `data_entities`, `entity_access`), `concern:http-client` (`http_client` signals), `concern:caching` (cache-library imports, redis driver), `concern:configuration` (`env_var` signals), plus clone-derived `concern:clone-<hash>` for any cluster spanning 3+ components. Each concern: slug id, member list (component + file evidence), mechanical detection basis. AI naming is P7-4. (d) FINDINGS as typed store entities: duplication (each cross-file cluster), orphan (each orphan component), and inconsistency candidates (a concern whose members use visibly divergent implementations, initially the logging concern with 2+ distinct logger libraries; conservative, marked inferred). Every finding carries kind, members, evidence, confidence (inferred), `verification_status='unverified'` (P7-4 flips it), and a deterministic `rank_score` (I11/I15 input): duplication ranks by member count, component span, token size, and class weight; inconsistency by distinct-implementation count; orphan by component size. Findings are emitted sorted by descending rank then id.
  4. **Store (schema_version 3 -> 4 via MIGRATIONS, recorded decision).** The frozen P4-1 layout has no correlation tables; following the P5-4/P5-5 precedent, `MIGRATIONS[4]` adds two tables additively: `concerns(id, kind, title, basis, members_json, detail_json)` and `findings(id, kind, summary, members_json, evidence_json, confidence, verification_status, rank_score, detail_json)`. Members ride in `*_json` payload columns (the additive pattern capabilities/rules use; component references are soft ids, not FKs, so `_flush` ordering stays trivial). A cold store opens at 4; a warm v1/v2/v3 store migrates additively and bumps to 4. `add_concern`/`add_finding` writers and `concerns()`/`findings()` deterministic readers (findings ordered by rank_score desc then id, I11). Correlation tables are cleared and rewritten by the derivation `_flush` (like edges/rules), NOT by `clear_extraction_facts` (they are derived, not extraction facts; the `clone_fragment` signals they derive from live in `signals` and ARE rebuilt by extraction).
  5. **Projection.** `_assemble` attaches optional top-level `concerns` and `findings` flat indexes and per-component `concerns`/`findings` id-reference lists (the ai_enhance optional-key precedent; set only when non-empty). The manifest and monolith carry all top-level arch keys, so both split and monolith modes ship them and old viewers ignore them (backward-compat test). `_flush` persists them to the store.
  6. Determinism (I4): fragment fingerprints sorted; ids content/path-derived (blake2b), never discovery-order-derived; clusters, concerns, and findings id-sorted (findings additionally rank-ordered); union-find iteration over sorted keys. Performance recorded on this repo; algorithmic complexity documented in `correlations.py`.
- Accept:
  - [ ] Token hook returns normalized `(line, norm, raw)` leaf tokens for a tree-sitter file and None for a regex-only parser; no existing parser behavior changes
  - [ ] A copied-then-renamed function across two components forms one cluster classified `renamed`; an identical copy classifies `exact`; the cluster carries per-member file + line-range evidence
  - [ ] Noise test: a file of trivial idioms (imports, one-line getters, boilerplate) below the token/df thresholds produces no cluster
  - [ ] An orphan component (no incoming edge, no capability, no test, non-entry type) surfaces as a `finding` of kind orphan with confidence inferred and evidence
  - [ ] Two components importing distinct logger libraries both join `concern:logging`, and an inconsistency finding is emitted listing the divergent libraries, marked unverified
  - [ ] Every finding carries kind, members, evidence, confidence, `verification_status='unverified'`, and a deterministic `rank_score`; findings read back rank-ordered
  - [ ] Schema migrates v3 -> v4 additively; a v1 store migrates through v2/v3/v4; cold store opens at 4
  - [ ] Projection carries optional `concerns`/`findings` top-level and per-component keys; a non-correlated dataset and an old viewer are unaffected (backward-compat test)
  - [ ] Determinism: two derives over the same store produce identical concerns/findings; self-repo run completes in seconds (number recorded)
  - [ ] Repo-wide green: pytest tests/ -q; ruff check analyzer/ tests/
- Verify: pytest tests/ -q; ruff check analyzer/ tests/; self-repo v2 run timing recorded; migration + determinism + noise + fixture proofs in Evidence.
- Evidence:
  - **TOKEN HOOK (parser tier, additive).** `TreeSitterParser.token_stream(content)` (analyzer/parsers/tree_sitter_base.py) returns leaf tokens as `(line, norm, raw)` in source order, or None when tree-sitter is unavailable. Comments dropped; string/number/boolean literals collapse to `LIT` (raw keeps the actual literal text so type-1 stays distinct from type-2); identifiers collapse to `ID`; operators/punctuation/keywords keep their literal text (anonymous leaves). Iterative stack walk (children pushed reversed) so no RecursionError on deep trees. Verified live: `def f(x): y = x + 1; return "hi"` tokenizes to `def ID ( ID ) : ID = ID + LIT return LIT` with raw `1`/`"hi"` preserved. No existing parser method touched; called only by P5-6 extraction.
  - **CLONE-FRAGMENT EXTRACTION (Tier 1).** `analyzer/extract/clones.py`: per function/method symbol with >= MIN_FRAGMENT_TOKENS(50) normalized tokens, one `clone_fragment` signal carries start/end line, ntokens, `norm_hash`/`raw_hash` (blake2b), and winnowing fingerprints (K=5-gram, W=4 window; ~2/(W+1) kept, guarantees a shared fingerprint for any run >= K+W-1=8 tokens). All hashing blake2b (never PYTHONHASHSEED-salted `hash`, I4). Wired into `_parse_worker` after rule signals; `EXTRACT_TIER` bumped p5-extract/2 -> p5-extract/3 (warm-store re-extract once, no silent stale facts, I2). Class/container kinds skipped so bodies do not double-count methods.
  - **CORRELATION DERIVATION (Tier 3, reads only the store).** `analyzer/derive/correlations.py`: clone clustering (norm_hash union + df-filtered fingerprint-inverted-index candidate pairs, Jaccard >= SIM_THRESHOLD(0.80), union-find; stop-fingerprints in > MAX_DF_ABS(40) or > MAX_DF_FRAC(20%) of fragments dropped); orphan detection (leaf component, no incoming edge, non-entry type, no `entry_points`, no owned capability, not test/content, code present); concerns (logging/auth/persistence/http-client/caching/configuration from imports+signals+policy rules+entity access, plus clone-derived `concern:clone-<hash>` for 3+ component clusters); findings (duplication per cross-file cluster, orphan per orphan component, inconsistency for a logging concern with 2+ distinct logger libs). Findings carry kind, members, evidence, confidence=inferred, `verification_status='unverified'` (P7-4 flips it, I15), deterministic `rank_score` (I11). Auth policy-rule contribution gated by access-control keywords (authoriz/permission/jwt/oauth/...) so generic multi-branch decision rules do NOT flood auth (calibrated against the 104 self-repo policy rules, only access-control ones counted). Runs last in `derive/pipeline.py::derive_all` (reads components/edges/capabilities/entities/rules/clone-fragment signals).
  - **STORE (schema_version 3 -> 4).** `MIGRATIONS[4] = _CORRELATIONS_DDL` adds `concerns(id, kind, title, basis, members_json, detail_json)` and `findings(id, kind, summary, members_json, evidence_json, confidence, verification_status, rank_score, detail_json)`, members in payload columns (soft component refs, no FK). `add_concern`/`add_finding` writers; `concerns()` (id-sorted) and `findings()` (rank_score DESC, id, I11) readers. Cleared+rewritten by derivation `_flush` (before rules, like edges); NOT by `clear_extraction_facts` (they are derived; the `clone_fragment` signals they derive from live in `signals` and ARE rebuilt by extraction). Cold store opens at 4; a warm v1 store migrates through v2 activity, v3 rules, v4 correlations (`test_store.py::test_schema_migration_v1_to_current_is_additive`, asserts each new table usable and finding round-trips unverified).
  - **PROJECTION.** `_assemble` attaches optional top-level `concerns`/`findings` flat indexes and per-component `concerns`/`findings` id-reference lists (`_attach_id_refs`, set only when non-empty). Manifest and monolith carry all top-level keys, so both modes ship them; `test_projection_carries_optional_correlation_keys` proves the monolith carries them and that a component with no membership carries neither key (old viewers unaffected).
  - **FIXTURE PROOFS (`tests/test_correlations.py`, 12 cases, real extract->derive path).** Renamed clone across alpha/ and beta/ forms exactly one cluster classified `renamed` with 2 components and per-member line-range evidence; a byte-identical function across calc_one/ and calc_two/ classifies `exact`; the orphanage/ leaf surfaces as a `finding:orphan:orphanage` (confidence inferred, unverified); logx/ (structlog) and logy/ (loguru) both join `concern:logging` and produce one inconsistency finding listing both libs, unverified; concern slugs are mechanical; findings are rank-ordered and all unverified/inferred with members+evidence; derivation is deterministic across two runs; findings round-trip through the store rank-ordered.
  - **NOISE + THRESHOLD PROOF.** `test_noise_idioms_produce_no_cluster`: two files of ten one-line getters each yield zero duplication findings. `test_min_fragment_threshold_is_enforced`: a getters-only file emits zero `clone_fragment` signals. Load-bearing proof: at MIN_FRAGMENT_TOKENS=50 the idiom repo produces 0 duplication findings; forcing the threshold to 3 makes the same idioms cluster (1 finding), so the threshold is what suppresses idiom noise.
  - **PARITY MASK.** `test_derive.py` D9: `_strip_capabilities` also pops `concerns`/`findings` (top-level and per-component) before normalization, so the frozen polyglot/multi snapshots still match; the correlations are asserted directly in `test_correlations.py`.
  - **PERFORMANCE (self-repo, engine v2).** 560 files, 1228 `clone_fragment` fragments. Extract 0.99s; full derivation 4.67s of which the correlation pass is 0.267s (mean of 5); total wall ~5.7s. Seconds, not minutes. Complexity documented in `correlations.py`: fingerprinting linear in tokens (Tier 1); clustering near-linear in fragment count, candidate pairs bounded by the df filter to O(MAX_DF_ABS * total_fingerprints); union-find near-linear. Self-repo counts: 6 concerns (auth 6, caching 4, configuration 2, http-client 7, logging 2, persistence 24), 45 findings (32 duplication, 13 orphan). The broad persistence/http-client counts are honest self-analysis artifacts (the analyzer's own driver/pattern definitions match its detectors); P7-4 verification prunes.
  - **COMMANDS / RESULTS.** `pytest tests/ -q` -> 900 passed, 1 xfailed (was 898+1 before this task; +12 test_correlations, +2 updated store/rules migration assertions for the v4 bump). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/` -> All checks passed. `architecture.json` (repo root) untouched throughout (not committed).
  - **DEVIATIONS / NOTES:** (1) Symbol-level orphans are deferred: the store has no symbol-granularity cross-reference graph, so exported-symbol reachability is not "cheaply resolvable"; orphans are component-level (recorded, matches the card's "where cheaply resolvable" latitude). (2) Duplication findings are emitted for every cross-FILE cluster, a superset of the card's "clusters spanning components", because within-repo cross-file duplication in one component is still worth surfacing; component span feeds the rank and gates the clone-derived concern (3+ components). (3) Inconsistency findings are enabled for the logging concern only (`_INCONSISTENCY_CONCERN_KINDS`), the conservative canonical case LENS-DESIGN names; the mechanism is generic and other kinds can be enabled with evidence. (4) Auth policy-rule contribution is keyword-gated to access-control rules (see above), a precision choice proven necessary by the self-repo run where 104 generic policy rules would otherwise have flooded auth. (5) Multi-repo v2 (`derive_multi_from_config`) does not derive correlations, consistent with the existing multi-repo capabilities/entities/rules/activity/coverage deferrals (single-repo v2 has full correlations); flagged in Discovered. (6) `EXTRACT_TIER` bumped so warm stores re-extract once and never serve pre-P5-6 facts.

## Phase 6: Perspectives

### P6-1: Lens framework
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-lens; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-13)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Scope: a lens abstraction in store.ts and App (Structure, Flow, Capability, Data, and the LENS-DESIGN additions), URL state (?lens=), per-lens node/edge selection feeding the existing graph pipeline; Structure remains the default and pixel-identical for old data. Implements invariants I11 (ranked landing views), I12 (same element every lens), I13 (rationale strip), and I14 (each lens ships its question list with the gesture that answers each, tested).
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 3 (I11 to I14), 4 (L1 Structure), 8 (question grammar). Structure is the only lens implemented now; Flow/Capability/Data and the LENS-DESIGN additions register in their own cards (P6-2, P6-3, P6-5 to P6-9).
- Files (viewer only): src/lenses/registry.ts (new), src/lenses/structure.ts (new), src/lenses/index.ts (new), src/store.ts, src/utils/urlState.ts, src/hooks/useUrlSync.ts, src/components/LensSwitcher.tsx (new), src/components/RationaleStrip.tsx (new), src/components/DetailPanel.tsx, src/App.tsx, src/types.ts, tests.
- Do:
  1. Lens registry and switcher. A `LensDefinition` interface (id, label, short description, availability predicate, node/edge selection, question list) with `registerLens`/`getLens`/`listAvailableLenses`. Structure registers now and is the default; the registry is extensible so the later lenses self-register. A `LensSwitcher` control in the header lets the user change lens; it lists the available lenses for the loaded dataset.
  2. URL state. Add `?lens=` to urlState and useUrlSync: restore on load, write on change, apply on popstate. Absent or unknown lens falls back to Structure. Compose with the existing component/drill/tab params.
  3. Per-lens node/edge selection feeding the existing graph pipeline. The graph asks the active lens for its nodes and edges; the Structure lens returns exactly today's `getVisibleComponents`/`getAggregateNodes`/`getComponentRelationships`, so with Structure selected the render is pixel-identical for old data (test this).
  4. I12 (same element, every lens). Switching lens preserves the selected element, breadcrumbs, drill level, and URL state; the selection rides stable identity (I4). Test with a second (test-registered) lens that switching does not lose selection/drill/breadcrumbs and that the URL carries lens plus the preserved params.
  5. I13 (rationale strip). A compact `RationaleStrip` on the component detail panel showing whatever of author, last change, churn, originating commit/PR, and AI intent (architectural role, help-text presence, last AI-enhancement time) is present in the dataset, degrading to render nothing when none is present. Forward-compatible with the git-activity fields P5-4 will add (read from an optional `rationale` shape), and populated today from `ai_enhance`.
  6. I14 (question list per lens, tested). The Structure lens ships a documented question list, each item pairing a human question with the exact gesture that answers it (per LENS-DESIGN section 8, restricted to what Structure supports today). A test exercises each gesture against the real store and asserts the answer.
  7. I11 for Structure. Assert (do not redesign) that the drill landing preserves the existing ranked child ordering.
- Accept:
  - [ ] Lens registry with Structure registered and default; switcher renders and lists available lenses; `?lens=` round-trips through the URL and composes with component/drill/tab
  - [ ] Structure lens node/edge selection equals today's store selectors, so the graph is pixel-identical for old data (equality test)
  - [ ] I12: switching lens preserves selectedComponentId, drillLevel, breadcrumbs, and the URL params (test with a second registered lens)
  - [ ] I13: the rationale strip renders present fields and renders nothing when the dataset carries none (test both)
  - [ ] I14: the Structure question list is encoded and every gesture is exercised in a test that asserts the answer
  - [ ] I11: a test asserts the drill landing keeps the existing ranked ordering
  - [x] Full viewer verification green; existing tests unchanged and green
- Verify: npm test -- --run; npm run lint (0 warnings); npx tsc -b; npm run build
- Evidence:
  - LENS REGISTRY + SWITCHER (card items 1, 2, 3). New `src/lenses/`: `registry.ts` (`LensDefinition` = id/label/description/`isAvailable`/`getGraph`/`questions`; `registerLens`/`getLens`/`listLenses`/`listAvailableLenses`/`resolveLensId`; `DEFAULT_LENS_ID = "structure"`), `structure.ts` (the Structure lens, `isAvailable: () => true`, `getGraph` returns exactly `getVisibleComponents`/`getAggregateNodes`/`getComponentRelationships`, plus the I14 `STRUCTURE_QUESTIONS`), `index.ts` (side-effect registration + re-exports). Store gains `lens` state (default structure), `setLens` (resolves unknown/unavailable ids to Structure, and does NOT touch selection/breadcrumbs/drill), and `getLensGraph()` (dispatches to the active lens, falls back to Structure). `ArchitectureGraph` now builds its nodes/edges from `getLensGraph()` instead of the three selectors directly, keyed on `lens`; for Structure this returns the identical arrays, so old data renders pixel-identically. `LensSwitcher.tsx` (a header `<select>` listing `listAvailableLenses`) is mounted in App. URL: `urlState` gains `lens` (omitted from the URL when default, so old/Structure links are byte-unchanged); `useUrlSync` restores it on load (before drill/selection), writes it on change (lens-only change uses replaceState), and applies it on popstate.

The test wires one gesture per question against the REAL store and asserts the answer (select -> identity card + panel; drill -> ranked children; select -> relationships; expand aggregate -> member revealed; search -> located by name via the index; ai_enhance -> rationale present), plus a coverage assertion that every documented question id has a tested gesture and vice-versa.

SHARD SEARCH E2E: searching "windowed list" (words from the VirtualList docstring, not its name) returned the `VirtualList` symbol (viewer/src/components/VirtualList.tsx) and `computeVisibleRange` (viewer/src/utils/virtual.ts), proving docstring-backed shard search finds symbols without opening their component (this validates P6-4 too). The I12 test therefore registers a lightweight second lens to prove cross-lens identity, which is legitimate framework-contract testing (the switch preserves stable identity, I4). (2) The switcher renders even with one available lens (a single-option control listing Structure), so it is visible and future-ready; it hides only when no dataset is loaded. ### P6-2: Flow lens
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-flow; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-13)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Scope: render navigation/tab/modal/embed edges and UIAction target_view links as a screen-flow diagram grouped by tab container; reachable from a screen node's context; works on the unamentis dataset today (the data already exists).
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 3 (I11 to I14), 4 (L4: flows walkable as scenarios; runtime variant deferred), 8 (question grammar). Follows the Activity lens (P6-5) as the pattern for how a lens registers, gates availability, docks a ranked panel, and encodes per-node visuals in ArchitectureGraph without touching other lenses.
- Elaboration (2026-07-13, no scope change): (1) Register a `flow` lens available when `hasFlowData(arch)` (a navigation/tab/modal/embed edge, a screen/tab/tab-container component, or a UIAction with target_view); absent hides it. (2) getGraph returns a global (not drill-scoped) screen-flow selection: all flow-typed components as nodes, real flow relationships (cloned, kind-labeled) plus synthetic target_view action edges as edges; aggregates empty. Layout direction is a new optional `LensDefinition.layoutDirection`, "RIGHT" for Flow (left-to-right layered walk), default "DOWN"; ArchitectureGraph reads it. (3) The lens lands on a ranked list of entry flows (tab containers, tabs, and top-level screens with no incoming flow edge) ordered by reachable node count (I11), in a left-docked FlowPanel. Selecting one enters follow mode: a DFS pre-order walk stepped by Next/Previous, each hop selected and breadcrumbed, with the path list clickable. (4) Non-UI datasets never show the lens; http edges are never fabricated into flows (recorded as a future scenario variant). (5) I12: `?lens=flow` composes with new `flow`/`step` params, all gated on the Flow lens; selection survives lens switches. I13: following selects each screen so the rationale strip renders on it. I14: five-question Flow list tested in flowQuestions.test.
- Files (viewer only): src/lenses/flow.ts (new), src/lenses/registry.ts (layoutDirection), src/lenses/index.ts, src/store.ts (flow follow state + selectors), src/components/FlowPanel.tsx (new), src/components/ArchitectureGraph.tsx (per-lens layout direction + flow rings), src/App.tsx (dock FlowPanel), src/utils/urlState.ts (flow/step params), src/hooks/useUrlSync.ts (restore/write/popstate), src/utils/layout.ts (action/embed edge styles), src/components/DetailPanel.tsx (partial-testing guard), tests.
- Accept:
  - [x] Flow lens registers and is available exactly when the dataset carries flow-bearing data; a non-UI dataset (this repo, a backend-only fixture) never shows it and http edges are never turned into flows (flowLens.test availability block)
  - [x] getGraph selects only screen/tab/tab-container nodes and draws navigation/modal/tab edges kind-labeled ("push", "modal (sheet)", "tab: X") plus target_view action edges; the Structure graph does NOT draw the action edge (contrast proof in flowLens.test)
  - [x] Entry flows are ranked by reachable screen count, widest first (I11); intermediate screens are not entries (construction proof)
  - [x] Follow steps forward/backward selecting each hop and clamps at both ends; selection is the shared identity and survives a lens switch (I12)
  - [x] ?lens=flow plus flow/step compose with existing params and restore on load and back/forward (I12); verified in the real browser
  - [x] Every documented Flow question has a tested gesture and the coverage assertion holds (I14, flowQuestions.test)
  - [x] Full viewer verification green; real-browser walk over the committed unamentis dataset is console-error-free
- Verify: npm test -- --run; npm run lint (0 warnings); npx tsc -b; npm run build; real-browser smoke over the committed unamentis sample (walk one flow end to end); pytest tests/ -q once from the worktree root (the worktree .git ledger-test environmental failure is expected).
- Evidence:
  - FLOW LENS (card items 1, 2). `src/lenses/flow.ts` defines the lens: `hasFlowData` gates availability on a flow edge OR a flow-typed component OR a target_view action; `collectFlowComponents` gathers all screen/tab/tab-container nodes in pre-order; `buildFlowEdges` clones the real navigation/tab/modal/embed relationships with a kind-labeled `label` ("push: <dest>", "modal (sheet)", "tab: <name>") and appends `collectActionEdges`, which resolves each UIAction `target_view` (by id, then name, then id-suffix) to a flow node and emits a synthetic `type: "action"` edge (the data LENS-DESIGN flagged as modeled-but-never-drawn). getGraph is global (walks `ctx.architecture.components`, not the drill level) so the whole journey stays visible; `layoutDirection: "RIGHT"` was added to `LensDefinition` (default "DOWN") and read by ArchitectureGraph. `utils/layout.ts` gained `action` (teal dotted) and `embed` (slate dotted) edge styles. Structure is untouched: getGraph, layout, and node visuals are all lens-gated.
  - RANKED ENTRIES + WALK (card item 3, I11). `rankEntryFlows` lists tab containers, tabs, and root screens (no incoming flow edge), ranked by reachable node count desc, ties by name; `walkFlow` is a DFS pre-order over outgoing flow edges (unique nodes) = the ordered journey. Store follow state (`flowEntryId`, `flowStep`) with `getFlowEntries`/`getFlowPath`/`setFlowEntry`/`flowStepNext`/`flowStepPrev`/`flowGoToStep`/`clearFlow`; each step calls `selectComponent` so the graph centers/highlights and the detail+rationale strip render (I13). `FlowPanel.tsx` docks left when `lens === "flow"` (App.tsx), showing the ranked landing then the follow view (Following header, Step X of N, Prev/Next, clickable path breadcrumb). ArchitectureGraph rings the entry (indigo) and current step (teal) under the Flow lens only.
  - URL + IDENTITY (I12). `urlState.ts` adds `flow`/`step` (strict-integer step, emitted only under the Flow lens); `useUrlSync.ts` restores follow on load and popstate and writes it on change, composing with component/drill/tab/lens. flowLens.test asserts selection survives a flow<->structure switch.
  - QUESTION LIST (I14). `FLOW_QUESTIONS` = where-start (ranked entries), what-next (Next), how-here (Previous), modal-or-push (edge kind label), which-tab (trace incoming tab edge). `flowQuestions.test.tsx` exercises one gesture per id against the real store and asserts the coverage set equals the documented set.
  - REGRESSION SURFACED + FIXED. The real-browser walk crashed the DetailPanel with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')" the moment a tab-container was selected: the SwiftUI detector emits `testing: {}` (truthy, no `test_frameworks`) and the tab computation read `component.testing.test_frameworks.length` unguarded. Guarded with optional chaining + `?? 0` (DetailPanel.tsx). FAIL-BEFORE: reverted the guard, ran `npx vitest run src/__tests__/detailPanelTestingGuard.test.tsx` -> 1 failed (render threw); restored the guard -> passes. The Structure lens rarely selects these UI nodes, so this latent crash only surfaced because the Flow lens follows them by design.
  - COMMANDS / RESULTS (from viewer/): `npx vitest run` -> 19 files, 151 passed (was 133; +17 flow, +1 testing guard); `npm run lint` -> 0 warnings; `npx tsc -b` -> clean; `npm run build` -> exit 0 (only the pre-existing 500 kB elk-chunk warning). REAL BROWSER (Playwright over `vite preview`/`dev` on the committed unamentis dataset): lens switcher offers Structure + Flow; Flow lands on 37 ranked entries (Tab Bar reaches 24 screens, first); following Tab Bar shows "Step 1 of 30", Next advances to Step 3 with URL `...&flow=...&step=2` and the teal path highlight on "Chat", Previous returns to Step 2; a cold load of `?lens=flow&flow=...&step=4` restores "Step 5 of 30" following Tab Bar; switching back to Structure removes the panel and drops the flow params. Console error-free throughout (only after the DetailPanel fix). `pytest tests/ -q` from the worktree root -> 820 passed, 1 xfailed, 1 failed (`test_coverage_ledger.py::test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents`, the known worktree `.git`-is-a-file environmental failure). `git status --porcelain architecture.json` empty (committed baseline untouched).
  - DEVIATIONS / NOTES: (1) The follow walk is a DFS pre-order of the entry's reachable subgraph (documented in flow.ts): a branching flow is not a single line, so consecutive steps are usually one hop deeper and the prefix is the breadcrumb; a strict linear-path model would drop branches. (2) getGraph is intentionally global rather than drill-scoped so the whole journey renders while following; drill navigation does not reshape the flow diagram. (3) The committed unamentis dataset carries 0 target_view actions, so the synthetic action edge is exercised by fixtures/tests, not the live walk; it renders when present. (4) DetailPanel partial-testing guard is a bug fix outside the lens files but inside viewer territory, made because the lens surfaced the crash (principle 4: no silent breakage).

### P6-3: Capability and Data lenses
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-capdata; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-13; PR "P6-3: Capability and Data lenses")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Scope: Capability lens groups capabilities by component with contract detail and AI business meaning; Data lens shows entities with read/write edges from components; both integrate search, selection, detail panel, and deep links.
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 4 (L2 Capability: the test linkage is the proof bridge; L3 Data: entities, fields, and read/write access edges), 3 (I11 to I13), 8 (I14). Follows the Flow (P6-2) and Activity (P6-5) lenses as the pattern for how a lens registers, gates availability, docks a ranked panel, and encodes per-node visuals in ArchitectureGraph without touching other lenses. Consumes the P5-1 capabilities, P5-2 data_entities, and P5-2 entity_access data (flat top-level indexes on the arch dict plus per-component keys, already delivered and consumed by the P5-3 DetailPanel Capabilities/Data tabs).
- Elaboration (2026-07-13, no scope change):
  - **Availability gates.** A `capability` lens registers unconditionally but `isAvailable(arch)` returns true only when `arch.capabilities` is non-empty; a `data` lens is available only when `arch.data_entities` is non-empty. A dataset carrying neither (a bare architecture, old data) shows neither lens in the switcher and degrades identically to a dataset without coverage or activity: no error, no empty lens. Tested both ways with a bare architecture.
  - **CAPABILITY LENS.** Landing (I11): a left-docked `CapabilityPanel` groups the whole-system `arch.capabilities` by kind in fixed order (api, cli, event, job) with per-group counts, each group expandable to its capabilities. Ranking within a group: api by path then name, others by name (deterministic). Each entry names its owning component (resolved from `component_id`) and shows a tested/untested badge computed from the L2 test linkage (`detail.tests` non-empty = tested; the proof bridge). Selecting a capability calls `selectCapability(id)`: it records `selectedCapabilityId` (highlighting the entry), routes through `navigateToComponent(component_id)` so the owning component is selected in the graph (I12 stable identity), and opens the Capabilities tab in the detail panel (via a `pendingDetailTab` store field the panel consumes). The graph under this lens (getGraph) shows the capability-owning components globally (walked across the whole tree, like Flow's global selection) plus the structural relationships among them, so "what can it do" reads spatially; each owner node carries a badge cluster showing its capability counts by kind (api/cli/event/job), passed through node data and rendered by ComponentNode only when present. Non-owner components are not drawn (a stronger form of the card's "or dim"); the ranked panel is the entry point, never a hairball.
  - **DATA LENS.** Landing (I11): a left-docked `DataPanel` ranks entities by access-edge count (most-touched data first) within kind groups (model, table, migration, schema in fixed order). Each entry: name, kind badge, owning component, and read/write access counts. Selecting an entity calls `selectEntity(id)`: it records `selectedEntityId`, focuses the entity's owning component (`navigateToComponent`), and opens the Data tab. The graph switches to an EGO VIEW of the selected entity: the owner component as the hub, its accessor components as spokes, connected by read (green) and write (amber) styled edges, one edge per access row, with an "inferred" marker (dashed, reduced opacity) on inferred-confidence edges (I3). The panel's focused view is the lens's heart, answering "who touches this data": a reads list (green) and a writes list (amber) of accessor components, each with its confidence. A back control returns to the ranked landing. Before an entity is selected the graph shows the entity-owning components globally plus their structural relationships (parallel to the Capability landing graph).
  - **I12 cross-lens identity and URL state.** `?lens=capability` composes with a `capability=<id>` param; `?lens=data` composes with an `entity=<id>` param; both are emitted only under their own lens (like `flow`/`step`), compose with the existing component/drill/tab params, restore on load, write on change, and apply on popstate. Selecting a capability/entity drives a real component selection, so switching lens preserves the selected element (I4).
  - **I13 rationale strip** continues to work: selecting a capability or entity selects its owning component, so the rationale strip renders on that component exactly as under other lenses (activity/AI data flows in where present).
  - **I14 question lists.** Capability: what can it do (grouped kinds), which kind (group labels), who owns it (entry names + selection), is it proven by tests (tested/untested badge), where is the contract (evidence file:line in the Capabilities tab).
