### P6-4: Scale UX: aggregation, shard search, prefetch
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-lens; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-13)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Scope: replace hero-filter hiding with expandable aggregation nodes so every child is visible or visibly aggregated (closes the silent-hiding gap per I2 spirit); consume search shards so search covers descriptions, docstrings, and AI help text without visiting components; predictive prefetch of children and breadcrumb ancestors of the selection; virtualized long lists in panels.
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md section 4 (L1 refinement: aggregation nodes instead of hidden internals); TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md section 4.4 (search shards); invariant I2 spirit (no silent hiding). Search-shard format is analyzer/project/search_shards.py (read-only), emitted since P4-5 (search/manifest.json + search-NNNN.json).
- Files (viewer only): src/store.ts, src/types.ts, src/utils/search.ts, src/utils/virtual.ts (new), src/components/AggregateNode.tsx (new), src/components/VirtualList.tsx (new), src/components/ArchitectureGraph.tsx, src/components/SearchOverlay.tsx, src/components/DetailPanel.tsx, src/App.tsx, tests.
- Do:
  1. Aggregation nodes. At a drill level the store's hero filter currently DROPS small internal modules (non-hero, non-library/infra, no children, fewer than 10 files) with only a `console.warn` fallback when it removes everything. Replace the drop with aggregation: components that would be hidden are grouped (by type) into labeled aggregate nodes carrying a member count. Every child at a drill level is therefore either a real node or a member of a visible aggregate. The aggregate is expandable in place (click the node or its expand control): expanding adds the members as real nodes at the same level and keeps the aggregate marker visible as a labeled group with a collapse control. Aggregate expansion state resets on architecture reload; it is keyed by drill level so it never leaks across levels. Content-type children remain a deliberate exclusion (assets/data blobs, not code), documented, not aggregated.
  2. Shard search. Consume the prebuilt search shards (search/manifest.json + search/search-NNNN.json) lazily (fetch the manifest, then the shards, on first search). Merge the shard entries into the existing Fuse index so search covers component descriptions, file module docs, symbol docstrings, and AI enrichment help text, and so split-mode symbols are searchable BEFORE their owning component is opened. Shard entries merge onto the in-browser base index by stable key (they enrich, they do not duplicate); component type/language badges are preserved from the base entry. Selecting a shard-only symbol (not present in the monolith) navigates to its owning component. Degrades silently when no shards exist (monolith or old data).
  3. Predictive prefetch. When a component is selected or drilled into, predictively prefetch the detail shards most likely to be opened next: the children of the selection plus the breadcrumb ancestors, bounded (default 8), scheduled at idle time (requestIdleCallback with a setTimeout fallback). Reuses loadComponentDetail so it is a no-op in monolith mode and rides the existing per-component cache/loading/negative-cache guards. Bounded so a wide fan-out never floods the network.
  4. Virtualized long lists. Long detail-panel lists (symbols especially, which the analyzer can emit in the thousands and which the panel currently hard-caps at 100 with a "showing 100 of N" message) render through a windowed VirtualList so all rows are reachable without rendering thousands of DOM nodes. Fixed-and-variable row heights are supported (an inline-expanded symbol row is taller). The hard 100-row cap is removed. Small lists keep their current rendering (virtualization engages past a threshold), so common cases stay pixel-identical.
  5. Optionally surface "kept X of Y symbols" when `stats.total_symbols_detected` differs from `stats.total_symbols` (Discovered row from P1-2). Optional; header display.
- Accept:
  - [ ] At every drill level, no component is silently hidden: a component that the old hero filter would drop appears as a member of a visible, counted aggregate node, and expanding the aggregate reveals it as a real node (store test with fail-before against the old drop path)
  - [ ] Aggregate expansion is in place and reversible; expansion state resets on architecture reload and does not leak across drill levels
  - [ ] Search finds a component by its description, a file by its module doc, a symbol by its docstring, and any target by its AI help text, from shard data, without opening the component (search test)
  - [ ] Shard entries merge (not duplicate) onto base entries and preserve the component type badge; split-mode symbols are findable before their component is opened
  - [ ] Prefetch targets are exactly the selection's children plus breadcrumb ancestors, bounded to the limit, deduplicated (pure-function construction proof) and are scheduled through loadComponentDetail
  - [ ] Long lists virtualize (windowed range math has a construction proof); the 100-symbol cap is gone; small lists are unchanged
  - [x] Full viewer verification green
- Verify: npm test -- --run; npm run lint (0 warnings); npx tsc -b; npm run build; exercise shard search against a v2 projection generated into a scratch dir with the analyzer CLI
- Evidence:
  - AGGREGATION NODES (card item 1). The hero filter lived in `store.ts` `getVisibleComponents`, which DROPPED small internal modules (non-hero, non-library/infra, no children, fewer than 10 files) when a hero was present, warning only if it emptied the level. Refactored into one source of truth: `computeDrillLevelView(promoted, drillLevel)` diverts the would-be-dropped set into typed `AggregateNode`s (`types.ts`) instead of discarding them, and `drillView(arch, drillLevel)` returns `{ shown, aggregates }` for both top level and drill levels. `getVisibleComponents` now returns `shown` plus the members of any EXPANDED aggregate; `getAggregateNodes` returns the level's aggregates. New store state `expandedAggregates` with `toggleAggregate`/`expandAggregate`/`collapseAggregate`; ids embed the drill level (`__agg__<level>__<type>`) so expansion never leaks across levels, and reset on `setArchitecture`. `AggregateNode.tsx` renders the collapsed/expanded group (counted label, member preview, +/- control, expands in place while keeping the marker visible); `ArchitectureGraph.tsx` registers the `aggregate` node type, emits aggregate nodes, and skips them in `onNodeClick` (they own their toggle). FAIL-BEFORE: `src/__tests__/aggregation.test.ts` asserts a small helper module is aggregated (not in `getVisibleComponents`, present as an aggregate member) and revealed on expand; on the pre-fix store there is no `getAggregateNodes` and the module is dropped, so those assertions cannot pass. Existing store tests (the two hero-filter/tab-container cases) stay green because heroes and substantial children remain shown.
  - SHARD SEARCH (card item 2). `utils/search.ts` gains `loadSearchShards()` (lazy: fetches `./architecture/search/manifest.json` then each `search-NNNN.json`, content-type-guarded so a Vite SPA HTML fallback is ignored, silent when absent) and `addShardEntries()`/`shardEntryToResult()`. `rebuildFuse` now merges base + detail + shard entries by `${type}:${id}`: shard free text (descriptions, module docs, docstrings, AI help text) folds onto the base entry while the base component type/language badge is preserved; enrichment entries (`ref_id` `component:<id>`) merge onto the owning element; split-only symbols/files insert fresh carrying `componentId`. Added a low-weight `text` Fuse key and `ignoreLocation: true` (without it Fuse's location/distance buries a word deep in a long description or docstring, defeating the feature). `SearchOverlay.tsx` calls `loadSearchShards()` on first open and, for a shard-only symbol/file, navigates to its owning component (split-mode symbols are findable before their component is opened). FAIL-BEFORE / construction proof: `src/__tests__/searchShards.test.ts` first asserts a help-text word, a docstring word, and a split-only symbol are UNFINDABLE from the base index, then that `addShardEntries` makes each resolve, that the merged component keeps its `service` badge, and that there is one merged entry (no duplicate). REAL-PROJECTION CHECK (card Verify): `analyze.py viewer/src -o <scratch> --split --engine v2 --store <scratch>` emitted `search/manifest.json` (by_kind component 6, file 57, symbol 342) + `search-0000.json` with fields exactly `[ref_kind, ref_id, name, path, component, text]`; real symbol entries carry docstrings in `text` and their owning `component`, matching what `shardEntryToResult` consumes.
  - PREDICTIVE PREFETCH (card item 3). `store.ts` exports pure `collectPrefetchTargets(arch, componentId, breadcrumbs, limit=8)` (selection's children first, then breadcrumb ancestors, self-excluded, deduped, bounded) and `prefetchDetails(componentId)`, which is wired into `selectComponent` and `drillInto` and schedules each target through `scheduleIdle` (requestIdleCallback, setTimeout fallback) -> `loadComponentDetail` (a no-op in monolith mode, guarded by the existing cache/in-flight/negative-cache). Tests: construction proof for `collectPrefetchTargets` (order, bound, dedup, null arch) plus a wiring test that a split-mode `prefetchDetails("sel")` fetches `./architecture/data/detail-child-1.json` and is a no-op in monolith mode.
  - VIRTUALIZED LISTS (card item 4). New `utils/virtual.ts` (`computeOffsets`, `computeVisibleRange`, pure, construction-proven in `src/__tests__/virtual.test.ts`: prefix sums, windowed range + overscan, whole-list fallback when the viewport height is unknown, variable heights, bounds clamping) and `components/VirtualList.tsx` (measures its viewport via ResizeObserver, renders only the visible window; renders all rows when height is unknown so nothing is hidden by a missing measurement). `DetailPanel.tsx` SymbolsTab virtualizes past 150 symbols (fixed-height rows; clicking opens the symbol in the existing SymbolDetail view, since inline expansion is incompatible with fixed-height windowing) and keeps the inline-expand list unchanged below the threshold. The old hard `slice(0, 100)` cap and its "Showing 100 of N" message are GONE: every symbol is reachable in both paths.
  - COMMANDS / RESULTS (from viewer/): `npx vitest run` -> 13 files, 110 passed (was 89; +21 across virtual/aggregation/searchShards); `npm run lint` -> 0 warnings; `npx tsc -b` -> clean; `npm run build` -> exit 0 (only the pre-existing 500 kB elk-chunk size warning). `git status --porcelain architecture.json` empty (committed baseline untouched).
  - DEVIATIONS / NOTES: (1) Content-type children remain a deliberate exclusion (assets/data blobs, not code), documented in `computeDrillLevelView`; they are not aggregated. (2) Item 5 ("kept X of Y symbols" using `total_symbols_detected`) NOT implemented: the v2 engine (default since P4-7) uses the coverage ledger, not the v1 symbol cap, so `total_symbols_detected` is a v1-only field and v2 projections do not emit it; surfacing it would only affect legacy `--engine v1` single-file output. Left to the header/docs owner; recorded in Discovered. (3) In the virtualized symbols path (>150) clicking a row opens the symbol in the detail view rather than expanding inline; this only affects very large symbol lists, and small lists keep the pixel-identical inline-expand UX. (4) `SearchOverlay` component selection now routes through `navigateToComponent` (drills to the owner then selects) instead of a bare `selectComponent`, so a nested search hit becomes visible in the graph; consistent with the file/symbol paths.

### P6-5: Activity lens
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-activity; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-13; PR "P6-5: Activity lens")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 4 (L5), 3 (I11 to I13), 8 (I14). Consumes the P5-4 activity data (activity.json shard plus the manifest `activity` summary, or the monolith inline `activity` key).
- Scope: hotspot map (change frequency times complexity or health) as the ranked landing view; knowledge map (authorship distribution, knowledge islands, bus factor at component granularity); change coupling reached from hotspots, never standalone. Every element cross-links per I12 and carries the rationale strip per I13.

- Elaboration (this session, faithful to the scope above; no scope change):

  Recorded decisions:
  - **Availability gate mirrors coverage.** The lens registers unconditionally but its `isAvailable(arch)` returns true only when `arch.activity` is present. P5-4 emits a manifest `activity` summary (split mode) or an inline `activity` key (monolith) exactly when git history was read, so presence == availability. A dataset without git activity never shows the lens in the switcher and degrades identically to a dataset without coverage. No error, no empty lens.
  - **Two-tier data load (matches the P5-4 shard split).** The manifest `activity` summary (provenance + top 10 hotspots + counts) gates the lens and needs no fetch. The full ranked component list, per-component knowledge (author shares, island, bus factor), coupling, and per-file detail live in `activity.json` (split) or inline under `architecture.activity` (monolith). The store fetches `activity.json` lazily on first Activity-lens use OR first rationale-strip render (so author/last-change/churn flow into the strip for every element per I13, not only inside the lens), guarded like `loadCoverageRows` (in-flight, error, and architecture-swap guards; reset on `setArchitecture`). Monolith mode reads the inline full dict, no fetch.
  - **Landing view is the ranked hotspot list (I11), graph is the spatial complement.** The primary entry is a bounded, ranked `ActivityPanel` docked left of the graph: components ordered by hotspot score (change frequency times size, the P5-4 score), each row a heat bar plus commit count, churn, and knowledge badges. The graph renders the current drill level's nodes (the Activity lens `getGraph` returns exactly Structure's node/edge selection, so the spatial view stays coherent) with a heat ring encoding each node's hotspot score. Never an unranked hairball as the first screen.
  - **Selecting a hotspot drills into its per-file detail.** Clicking a ranked row routes through `navigateToComponent` (drills to the owner, selects it: I12 stable identity), and the panel switches to a focused view showing that component's per-file hotspot breakdown (ranked, lazy from `activity.json`), its knowledge map, and its change coupling. A "back to hotspots" control returns to the ranking.
  - **Knowledge map at component and team granularity.** Focused view shows author contribution shares (team granularity: per-author commit share, top author first), a knowledge-island badge (top author >= 95%, the P5-4 flag) and the bus factor. The overview also surfaces the island/bus-factor risk as badges on the ranked rows so "what is at risk" is legible before drilling.
  - **Coupling is reached FROM a hotspot, never standalone.** The coupling list only renders in the focused view ("what changes together with this component"): the component-coupling partners touching the focused id, ranked by co-change count, cross-component pairs by construction (the P5-4 `component_coupling` already excludes same-component pairs). There is no standalone coupling hairball anywhere.
  - **Rationale strip (I13).** When activity data is loaded, the strip merges git-derived author (lead author), last change (relative time of last modification), and churn into the existing rationale view for every component, alongside AI intent where present. It still renders nothing when neither activity nor AI data exists.

- Do:
  1. `types.ts`: add `ActivityData` (provenance, ranked `components` with knowledge, `component_coupling`, `file_coupling`, `files` detail map), `ActivityComponent`, `ActivityAuthor`, `ActivityCoupling`, `ActivityFile`, `ActivityProvenance`, and `ActivityManifestSummary`; add optional `activity?: ActivityManifestSummary | ActivityData` to `Architecture`.

### P6-6: Rules lens
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-rules; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-13; PR "P6-6: Rules lens")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md section 4 (L6), 3 (I11 to I13), 8 (I14). Follows the Capability (P6-3) and Data (P6-3) lenses as the pattern for how a lens registers, gates availability, docks a ranked panel, cross-links, and encodes per-node visuals in ArchitectureGraph without touching other lenses. Consumes the P5-5 rules data (flat top-level `rules` index plus per-component `rules` keys, already delivered).
- Scope: typed rule browser ranked per I11; decision-table rendering where branching warrants; links into Data (rule inputs/outputs) and Capability (rules behind a contract) lenses; every rule drills to its lines; stale statements marked per I5.
- Elaboration (2026-07-13, no scope change):
  - **Availability gate (degrades like coverage/activity/capdata).** A `rules` lens registers unconditionally but `isAvailable(arch)` = `hasRules` (arch.rules non-empty). A dataset carrying no rules (a bare architecture, old data) never shows the lens in the switcher and degrades identically to a dataset without coverage: no error, no empty lens. Tested both ways (absent key, present-but-empty key).
  - **LANDING (I11).** A left-docked `RulesPanel` groups the whole-system `arch.rules` by kind in fixed order (policy, validation, calculation, io: the decision-bearing kinds lead) with per-group counts, each group expandable. Within a kind, rules group by owning component ranked by rule count (most-enforcing component first, ties by id), so "where do decisions live" (the kind ranking) and "what does this component enforce" (the component subgrouping) both read directly. Each rule row shows the mechanical summary, an `inferred` marker when shape-matched, small `data`/`contract` tags when the rule carries a cross-link, and evidence file:line links (a GitHub SourceLink reusing `getSourceUrl`). Rules within a component sort by evidence file:line then id (deterministic).
  - **FOCUSED VIEW.** Selecting a rule opens a focused detail (parallel to the Data lens ego view): the kind badge + confidence, "enforced in <owner>", then either a DECISION TABLE (for decision-shaped policy rules) or the MECHANICAL SUMMARY; a clearly-shaped IN PLAIN LANGUAGE slot (Phase 7); the two cross-lens jump buttons when the rule carries links; inputs/outputs; and evidence file:line. A "‹ Rules" control returns to the ranked landing.
  - **DECISION TABLE (L6).** The P5-5 extractor emits decision-shaped policy rules (anchors switch/match/case_when) as a subject (`detail.inputs`) plus the branch case labels (`detail.outputs`); it does NOT capture each branch's body. `decisionTableFromRule` returns a table only for those anchors with a subject and >= 2 branch labels; every other rule (guard clauses, if/elif chains with no captured labels, permission gates, all non-policy kinds) renders its mechanical summary. The table is a small two-column render (Case | Matches when: `<subject> == <label>`) so branching reads as a scannable table rather than a run-on string. When Phase 7 supplies the branch action, the second column upgrades in place. Recorded as a Discovered row (the card assumed condition-action pairs; the extractor emits subject + labels).
  - **SELECTION + I12.** `selectRule(id)` records `selectedRuleId` (highlighting the entry) and routes through `navigateToComponent(component_id)` so the owning component is selected in the graph (I12 stable identity), which also drives the rationale strip (I13) on that component. There is no Rules detail tab, so no `pendingDetailTab` is requested. Switching lens preserves the selected element.
  - **CROSS-LENS JUMPS (L6).** `viewRuleInDataLens(id)` reads the rule's `detail.entity` (io -> L3), `setLens("data")` + `selectEntity(entityId)`; `viewRuleInCapabilityLens(id)` reads `detail.trigger.capability` (L2), `setLens("capability")` + `selectCapability(capId)`. Both preserve identity (the linked element's owner is selected) and URL state (the entity/capability param under its lens; the rule param drops), and are no-ops returning false when the link is absent, so the UI gates the affordance.
  - **GRAPH (P6-6 item 5).** The Rules lens `getGraph` returns the rule-owning components (globally) plus the structural edges among them (parallel to the Capability lens), and ArchitectureGraph attaches per-kind rule-count badges to each owner node via `ruleCountsByComponent`, rendered by ComponentNode as an indigo badge cluster (the Capability lens badge pattern). Non-owners are not drawn; the ranked panel is the entry point, never a hairball.
  - **I12 URL STATE.** `?lens=rules` composes with a `rule=<id>` param, emitted only under the Rules lens (like flow/capability/entity), composing with the existing component/drill/tab params, restoring on load, writing on change, and applying on popstate.
  - **STATEMENT SLOT + STALENESS (I5).** The additive optional `Rule.ai_enhance` ({statement?, stale?}) is rendered by the IN PLAIN LANGUAGE slot: when Phase 7 lands a statement it shows; a `stale` flag renders a staleness marker per I5; until then the slot shows a muted placeholder so it is visibly present and lights up when enrichment lands.
- Do:
  1. `types.ts`: additive optional `Rule`, `RuleDetail`, `RuleTrigger`, `RuleAIEnhance` matching the projection exactly; optional `Architecture.rules` and `Component.rules`.
  2. `lenses/rules.ts`: register the `rules` lens (`isAvailable` = `hasRules`, `getGraph` = owner components + structural edges, `RULE_QUESTIONS`), plus pure helpers `RULE_KIND_ORDER`, `hasRules`, `ruleEntityId`, `ruleCapabilityId`, `collectRuleOwnerIds`, `ruleCountsByComponent`, `groupRulesByKind`, `decisionTableFromRule`, `buildRulesGraph`.
  3. `lenses/registry.ts` (LensContext optional `selectedRuleId`), `lenses/index.ts` (register + re-export).
  4. `store.ts`: `selectedRuleId` state (reset on `setArchitecture`), `selectRule`/`clearRule`/`getRules`, `viewRuleInDataLens`/`viewRuleInCapabilityLens`; pass `selectedRuleId` into `getLensGraph` context.
  5. `components/RulesPanel.tsx`: the ranked grouped landing plus the focused rule view (decision table, statement slot, cross-lens jumps, evidence); mounted in `App.tsx` left of the graph when the lens is active.
  6. `components/ArchitectureGraph.tsx` (per-kind rule-count badges per owner node), `components/ComponentNode.tsx` (render the `ruleBadges` cluster).
  7. `utils/urlState.ts` + `hooks/useUrlSync.ts` (`rule` param, gated per lens, restore/write/popstate).
  8. Tests + card update + PR.
- Accept:
  - [x] The Rules lens appears only when the dataset carries rules; a bare dataset and a present-but-empty key show neither (isAvailable tests both ways, no error, no empty lens).
  - [x] Rules group by kind (policy, validation, calculation, io) with counts; within a kind, by owning component ranked by rule count (construction proof).
  - [x] Selecting a rule selects its owning component (I12), records `selectedRuleId`; the graph shows rule-owning components with per-kind rule badges.
  - [x] A decision-shaped policy rule renders as a two-column decision table; every other rule renders its mechanical summary (construction proof: switch/match -> table, if/elif and non-policy -> summary, < 2 branches -> summary).
  - [x] A rule with an entity link jumps to the Data lens with that entity focused; a rule with a capability trigger jumps to the Capability lens with that capability selected; both preserve identity and URL state, and are no-ops when the link is absent (store tests + real browser).
  - [x] I12: `?lens=rules&rule=` round-trips through the URL, composes with component/drill/tab, and preserves the selected element across a lens switch (tests).
  - [x] I14: every documented Rules question has a tested gesture and every tested gesture maps to a documented question (coverage test).
  - [x] Full viewer verification green; existing tests unchanged and green.
- Verify: npm test -- --run; npm run lint (0 warnings); npx tsc -b; npm run build; a real v2 projection of this repo, then a real-browser smoke over the 198 rules (walk the lens, exercise both cross-lens jumps, console-error-free); pytest tests/ -q once from the worktree root (the worktree .git ledger-test environmental failure is expected and recorded).
- Evidence:
  - **Files (viewer only).** New: `src/lenses/rules.ts` (Rules lens + `RULE_QUESTIONS` + pure helpers), `src/components/RulesPanel.tsx` (ranked grouped landing + focused rule view), `src/__tests__/rulesLens.test.ts` (25 cases), `src/__tests__/rulesQuestions.test.ts` (6 cases). Modified: `src/types.ts` (`Rule`/`RuleDetail`/`RuleTrigger`/`RuleAIEnhance`; optional `Architecture.rules` and `Component.rules`), `src/lenses/registry.ts` (LensContext optional `selectedRuleId`), `src/lenses/index.ts` (register + re-export), `src/store.ts` (`selectedRuleId` reset on `setArchitecture`; `selectRule`/`clearRule`/`getRules`; `viewRuleInDataLens`/`viewRuleInCapabilityLens`; `selectedRuleId` in `getLensGraph` context), `src/components/ArchitectureGraph.tsx` (per-kind rule-count badges per owner node; memo dep), `src/components/ComponentNode.tsx` (render the `ruleBadges` cluster), `src/utils/urlState.ts` + `src/hooks/useUrlSync.ts` (`rule` param, gated per lens, restore/write/popstate), `src/App.tsx` (mount `RulesPanel` when the lens is active).   - **AVAILABILITY GATE.** `rulesLens.isAvailable = hasRules` (arch.rules non-empty). `rulesLens.test` asserts the lens is in `listAvailableLenses` for a dataset carrying rules and absent for a bare dataset AND for a present-but-empty key. Real browser: the switcher on this repo's projection lists Rules and the panel renders.

Tests assert the kind order, the policy count (3 = 2 billing + 1 web), the component ranking (`billing:2` before `web:1`), the within-component order, first-seen owner order, and `ruleCountsByComponent` per-kind badge counts.

Tests: switch -> table (`zone` + 3 branches), match -> table, if/elif -> null, non-policy -> null, one-branch -> null. Tests assert the Data jump lands on `lens=data` with `selectedEntityId=e:invoice` and the owner selected; the Capability jump lands on `lens=capability` with `selectedCapabilityId=cap:billing:charge` and the owner selected; both are no-ops (false, lens unchanged) for a rule without the link.
  - **I12 URL STATE.** `?lens=rules&rule=` composes with component/drill/tab, is emitted only under the Rules lens, restores on load, writes on change, applies on popstate. Test asserts the round-trip and that the `rule` param is dropped under a different lens. ### P6-7: Tours
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-tours; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-14; PR "P6-7 Tours and findings-to-sets integration")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md section 4 (L7), section 3 (I5 staleness, I11 ranked, I12 identity), section 8 (I14 question grammar).
- Scope: tour player in the viewer (ordered steps, highlighted locations, narration, progress); tour artifacts stored as first-class store entities keyed to stable IDs; staleness markers per I5. Tour authoring/generation is Phase 7 enrichment work.
- Elaboration (2026-07-14, no scope change):
  - **Data-first landing (the projection contract).** Tours ride the projection as an optional `Architecture.tours?: Tour[]` (types.ts), mirroring how activity (P5-4) and rules (P5-5) landed data-first before their viewers. `Tour {id, title, description, steps: TourStep[{target, title, narration, evidence?{file,line}}], provenance?{derived_from_commit?, stale?}}`. `target` is a stable id (component id, file path, or symbol id, I12). The store table and the enrichment-side generation are the analyzer follow-up (see the Discovered row); scope here is the PLAYER, per the card. Old datasets omit the key and degrade like coverage/activity/findings (no entry point, byte-identical legacy render).
  - **The player is not a lens.** Like the findings surface, it is a globally reachable feature available whenever `hasTours(arch)`. `ToursEntry` is a slim bar below `FindingsEntry`; it opens the tour LIST. `TourPlayer` renders the list overlay (tours in AUTHORED order, I11, with step-count badges and a stale marker per I5) and, when a tour is active, a docked STEP PANEL.
  - **Playing.** `startTour` lands on step 0 and selects its target on stable identity (I12) via `navigateToTourTarget` (a component id -> `navigateToComponent`; otherwise the step's evidence file, or a bare file-path target, opens via `openFileDeepLink`, resolving the symbol at the line). The step panel shows the step's narration with next/previous/exit, breadcrumbs progress ("Step X of N", the Flow-lens follow pattern), links the evidence file:line ("show me the code"), and marks the tour stale when `provenance.stale` (I5). Transient player state (`toursOpen`/`activeTourId`/`tourStep`) resets on `setArchitecture`, exactly like the Flow walk; NO store table of tour artifacts (deferred).

### P6-8: Concerns and Findings surfaces
- Status: DONE (branch program2/p6-findings; Opus 4.8 executor session, Stream B, 2026-07-13; PR "P6-8: Concerns and Findings surfaces")
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 8 (I14 question grammar), 9 (findings and concerns), 10 context (set-level actions are P6-9; finding rows expose members as a selectable-set hook, a stub that records the intent). Consumes the P5-6 projection: top-level `concerns`/`findings` flat indexes plus per-component `concerns`/`findings` id-reference lists (already delivered). No analyzer/python change.
- Scope: the ranked findings surface (duplication clusters, convergence violations, orphans, inconsistencies) with verification status; concern browser with membership drill-in; contextual finding badges on affected elements in the detail panel; every finding actionable per I15 (open members, annotate the set, export directive).
- Elaboration (2026-07-13, no scope change):
  - **The surface is not a lens.** It is a globally reachable ranked surface, available whenever the dataset carries findings or concerns. A header-adjacent entry point (`FindingsEntry`, a slim bar directly under the coverage badge) opens an overlay panel (`FindingsSurface`, sibling to SearchOverlay/AdminDashboard, which are also overlay UI and not URL-synced). Availability gate: `hasFindingsSurface(arch)` = `(arch.findings?.length ?? 0) > 0 || (arch.concerns?.length ?? 0) > 0`. No keys means no entry point and byte-identical legacy rendering (both the entry and the overlay render null). The overlay is store-driven transient UI, not URL state, matching the existing overlays; deep-linking the surface is a Discovered follow-up.
  - **FINDINGS TAB (I11, I15).** `getFindings()` returns the findings sorted by `rank_score` desc then id (I11; the getter re-sorts rather than trusting projection order). A kind filter (`all` / the kinds present, e.g. duplication, orphan, inconsistency) narrows the list. Each row shows: a kind badge, a verification-status badge (an `unverified` finding is VISIBLY marked "unverified, not confirmed" and never presented as established fact, the DeepWiki lesson, I15/section 9), the confidence, the summary, and an expandable member list with `file:line` evidence links. Evidence links navigate: a member with a file navigates via `openFileDeepLink(file, line)` (drills to the owning component, marks the symbol); a component member navigates via `navigateToComponent(component_id)`. Every row carries the three I15 action affordances: (a) OPEN MEMBERS: navigate to each member component/file (per-member links plus an "open first" control); (b) ANNOTATE THE SET: wired to review mode as far as single-element annotation allows today, it stages the finding's members as a selection set (the P6-9 seam, `stageFindingSet`) and opens the existing annotation input on the finding's representative component, so the reviewer can attach feedback now; the staged set records the full intended membership for true set annotation in P6-9; (c) EXPORT DIRECTIVE: a disabled button with a tooltip naming P6-9 (the structured work-order export is section 10 / P6-9). The member list is the selectable-set hook section 10 calls for.
  - **CONCERNS TAB (I12).** `getConcerns()` lists concerns with member counts and detection basis, each expandable to its members with evidence links; selecting a member navigates to it (`navigateToComponent`, I12). Concern slugs render as-is with their mechanical `title`/`kind`; a shaped but empty "plain-language name" slot (Phase 7 P7-4) sits beside the mechanical name, exactly like the Rules lens plain-language slot, rendering nothing until P7-4 fills it.
  - **CONTEXTUAL BADGES (detail panel only).** `getFindingsForComponent(id)` returns the findings whose members reference that component id (robust: derived from member `component_id`, independent of whether the projection set the per-component ref). A small findings badge renders in the DetailPanel next to the rationale strip when the count is non-zero; clicking opens the surface filtered to that element's findings (`openFindingsSurface({ elementFilter: id })`). Kept to the DetailPanel to stay off other streams' graph turf; a graph badge is a Discovered follow-up.
  - **I14 QUESTION LIST (tested).** `FINDINGS_QUESTIONS` documents the five section-8 questions this surface answers, each paired with the gesture: noticed (ranked findings), confirmed (verification status), where (evidence links), shares-trait (concern members), what-can-i-do (the I15 affordances). Every id is exercised against the real store with a coverage assertion.
- Files (viewer only): `src/findings/model.ts` (new: `hasFindingsSurface`, `sortFindings`, `filterFindingsByKind`, `findingsForComponent`, `findingKinds`, `evidenceLabel`, `FINDINGS_QUESTIONS`), `src/components/FindingsSurface.tsx` (new), `src/components/FindingsEntry.tsx` (new), `src/types.ts` (`Finding`/`FindingMember`/`FindingEvidence`/`Concern`/`ConcernMember`/`ConcernEvidence`; `Architecture.findings`/`concerns`; `Component.findings`/`concerns`), `src/store.ts` (surface state + getters + `stageFindingSet`), `src/App.tsx` (mount entry + overlay), `src/components/DetailPanel.tsx` (contextual badge), tests.
- Do:
  1. `types.ts`: add the Finding/Concern data model (optional keys on Architecture and Component); old datasets omit them.
  2. `findings/model.ts`: pure helpers and the question list, unit-testable without the store.
  3. `store.ts`: `findingsSurface` state (`open`, `tab`, `kindFilter`, `elementFilter`), `openFindingsSurface`/`closeFindingsSurface`/`setFindingsSurfaceTab`/`setFindingsKindFilter`; getters `getFindings`/`getConcerns`/`getFindingsForComponent`/`getConcernById`; `stagedFindingSet` + `stageFindingSet` (the P6-9 seam); reset on `setArchitecture`.
  4. `FindingsSurface.tsx`: the overlay with Findings and Concerns tabs, the I15 affordances, verification-status marking.
  5. `FindingsEntry.tsx`: the entry bar under the coverage badge; renders null when the surface is unavailable.
  6. `DetailPanel.tsx`: the contextual findings badge.
  7. `App.tsx`: mount `FindingsEntry` after `CoverageBadge` and `FindingsSurface` among the overlays.
  8. Tests + card update + PR.
- Accept:
  - [ ] The surface entry appears only when the dataset carries findings or concerns; a bare dataset shows no entry and the overlay renders null (degrade test both ways; legacy rendering byte-identical).
  - [ ] Findings are ranked by `rank_score` desc (I11); `getFindings()` is non-increasing in rank_score and the top item is the maximum.
  - [ ] The kind filter narrows the list to one kind and back to all; `findingKinds` lists exactly the kinds present.
  - [ ] Every finding row shows kind, a VISIBLE verification-status marker (an unverified finding is labeled unverified and not presented as fact), confidence, members, and file:line evidence; evidence links navigate (openFileDeepLink / navigateToComponent).
  - [ ] I15 affordances present on every row: open members (navigate), annotate the set (stages the member set via `stageFindingSet` and opens the annotation input on the representative component), export directive (disabled, tooltip names P6-9).
  - [ ] Concerns list with member counts and basis, expandable to members with evidence; selecting a member navigates to it (I12); the mechanical slug renders with a shaped empty plain-language slot.
  - [ ] Contextual badge: `getFindingsForComponent(id)` returns the component's findings; the DetailPanel badge count matches and opening it filters the surface to that element.
  - [x] I14: every documented FINDINGS question has a tested gesture and every tested gesture maps to a documented question (coverage test).
  - [x] Full viewer verification green; existing tests unchanged and green.
- Verify: npm test -- --run; npm run lint (0 warnings); npx tsc -b; npm run build; a real v2 projection of this repo (45 findings, 6 concerns) into a scratch dir, then a real-browser smoke over the surface (console-error-free); pytest tests/ -q once from the worktree root (the worktree .git ledger-test environmental failure is expected and recorded).

Modified: `src/types.ts` (`Finding`/`FindingMember`/`FindingEvidence`/`Concern`/`ConcernMember`/`ConcernEvidence`; optional `Architecture.findings`/`concerns` and `Component.findings`/`concerns`), `src/store.ts` (findings surface state + getters + the P6-9 seam), `src/App.tsx` (mount `FindingsEntry` after `CoverageBadge`, `FindingsSurface` among the overlays), `src/components/DetailPanel.tsx` (contextual findings badge). No analyzer/python file touched (P5-6 data consumed read-only); `architecture.json` and the committed `viewer/public/architecture` sample untouched throughout.
  - **THE SURFACE IS NOT A LENS.** A globally reachable ranked overlay opened from a header-adjacent entry bar (`FindingsEntry`, a slim bar directly under `CoverageBadge`) or the contextual detail-panel badge. `hasFindingsSurface(arch)` = findings or concerns present. The overlay is store-driven transient UI (`findingsSurface` state), sibling to SearchOverlay/AdminDashboard which are likewise not URL-synced; deep-linking the surface is a Discovered follow-up. Escape and backdrop close it.

A legacy dataset (no keys) shows no entry point and renders exactly as before.
  - **FINDINGS TAB (I11, I15).** `getFindings()` = `sortFindings(arch.findings)`, ranked by `rank_score` desc, ties by id (the getter re-sorts, not trusting projection order). `findingKinds` lists the kinds present; `filterFindingsByKind` narrows. Each `FindingRow`: kind badge, a VISIBLE `VerificationBadge` (an unverified finding reads "⚠ unverified · not confirmed" with a "candidate, not a confirmed fact" tooltip, never asserted as fact, the DeepWiki lesson / I15), confidence, rank, summary, an expandable member list with `file:line` evidence links, and the three I15 affordances: OPEN MEMBERS (navigates via `openFileDeepLink`/`navigateToComponent`), ANNOTATE THE SET (`annotateFindingSet`: stages the members via `stageFindingSet` = the P6-9 seam, then opens the annotation input on the representative component), EXPORT DIRECTIVE (disabled, tooltip names P6-9). The mechanical `title` + slug `id` render as-is; a shaped-but-empty plain-language name slot (P7-4) sits beside them, like the Rules lens plain-language slot.
  - **CONTEXTUAL BADGE (DetailPanel only).** `getFindingsForComponent(id)` derives from finding members' `component_id` (robust, independent of the projection's per-component ref). The DetailPanel renders a "N findings here" badge below the rationale strip when non-zero; it opens the surface filtered to that element (`openFindingsSurface({ elementFilter: id })`). Kept to the DetailPanel to stay off graph turf; a graph badge is a Discovered follow-up.

(2) The current worktree projects 48 findings / 6 concerns (the P5-6 baseline recorded 45; the +3 is my own added viewer files, findings/model.ts + FindingsSurface.tsx + the two test fixtures, creating a few more cross-file clone clusters).
