### P6-9: Set-level review actions and directives
- Status: DONE (Opus 4.8 Stream B, branch program2/p6-directives, 2026-07-14)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md section 10
- Scope: selection sets over any commonality (concern members, clone clusters, finding members, search results, manual multi-select), nameable and persisted on stable identity; set-level annotations with shared intent plus per-member notes; directive export as a structured work order (intent, member list with file/lines/evidence, exemptions, acceptance criteria) replacing the prose prompt as the primary artifact.
- Elaboration (Do / Accept / Verify):
  - PREMISE CORRECTION: this card's spec assumed P6-8 (Concerns and Findings surfaces) had landed with a `FindingsSurface`, a `stagedFindingSet` store seam, and a disabled export-directive stub. On origin/main at branch time (HEAD 7fd921d, after PR #35) none of these exist: no FindingsSurface component, no stagedFindingSet in the store, and types.ts carries no Finding/Concern types. P6-8 is still TODO. P6-9 is therefore built as the selection-set + directive + persistence ENGINE plus the creation gestures whose UI exists today (search overlay, detail panel manual add, and set management/annotation/export in the review panel), and the finding/concern creation paths are built and proven at the store/model layer against real v2 projection data. The FindingsSurface-specific wiring (consume stagedFindingSet; the export-directive stub becomes live) is deferred to P6-8, which will call the store actions this card ships. Recorded in Discovered rows.
  - Do: (1) Add optional `Finding`/`Concern` types and `architecture.findings?`/`architecture.concerns?` keys to types.ts (the v2 projection already emits them; adding optional keys is backward-compatible). Add `SelectionSet`, `SetMember`, `SetAnnotation` types. (2) `viewer/src/utils/setStorage.ts`: a sibling of annotationStorage.ts (its stable `architectureIdentity` keying, a schema version, a 2 MB size guard, swallowed quota failures) persisting sets AND set-annotations under one `arch-selection-sets` key. (3) Store model in store.ts: `selectionSets`, `setAnnotations`, restored on `setArchitecture` by identity; actions `createSet`, `createSetFromFinding`, `createSetFromConcern`, `createSetFromSearchResults`, `addComponentToSet` (manual), `renameSet`, `deleteSet`, `setSetIntent`, `setSetMemberNote`, each persisted through `persistCurrentSets`. Members carry stable identity (component/file/symbol ref + owning componentId + label + optional file/lines/evidence for the directive). (4) `viewer/src/utils/directiveGenerator.ts`: `buildDirectiveModel` -> a structured work order (intent, ordered members with file/lines/evidence, exemptions with reasons, acceptance criteria auto-suggested by origin plus free text) and `renderDirectiveMarkdown` -> markdown with a fenced ```json block carrying the model under a documented, versioned schema. (5) UI: a Selection Sets section in ReviewSummary (rename, member count, orphaned-member handling per P1-3, intent textarea, per-member notes, exemption checkboxes with a required reason, acceptance-criteria text, copy + download of the directive); a "Make set from N results" affordance in SearchOverlay; an "Add to set" affordance on the component detail header (I12 stable-identity members). (6) Regression tests against the real store: creation from finding and concern (real projection data), persistence round-trip (P1-3 pattern), orphan handling, directive content (intent present, every member carries file/lines, exemptions carry reasons, the embedded JSON parses and its members match the set).
  - Accept: a set is created from a finding's members and from a concern's members with stable-identity refs; sets and set-annotations survive a hard reload (fresh store, same storage); a member whose component no longer exists is kept and shown as orphaned, never silently dropped; a directive names the intent, lists every member with file/lines and available evidence, records each exemption with its reason, states acceptance criteria, and embeds a parseable JSON block whose member list matches the non-exempt set; the FindingsSurface stub items are deferred to P6-8 with a Discovered row; full viewer verification green.
  - Verify: `npm test -- --run`, `npm run lint` (0 warnings), `npx tsc -b`, `npm run build`; a real-browser smoke (create a set, annotate, export, verify the download, console-error-free); `pytest tests/ -q` once (worktree `.git`-pointer environmental failure expected and recorded).
- Evidence:
  - SHIPPED. Store model (viewer/src/store.ts): `selectionSets`/`setAnnotations` state restored on `setArchitecture` by stable `architectureIdentity`; actions `createSet`, `createSetFromFinding`, `createSetFromConcern`, `createSetFromSearchResults`, `addComponentToSet` (manual), `renameSet`, `deleteSet`, `setSetIntent`, `setSetMemberNote`, `navigateToSetMember` (I12), each persisted via `persistCurrentSets`. Persistence (viewer/src/utils/setStorage.ts): a sibling of annotationStorage.ts under the `arch-selection-sets` key, schema version 1, 2 MB size guard, swallowed quota failures, storing sets AND set-annotations per identity. Directives (viewer/src/utils/directiveGenerator.ts): `buildDirectiveModel` -> structured `DirectiveModel` (intent, ordered members with file/lines/evidence, exemptions with reasons, origin-suggested + free-text acceptance criteria), `renderDirectiveMarkdown` -> markdown with a fenced ```json block under the versioned schema `solution-explorer.directive/v1`. UI: `SelectionSetsSection.tsx` in the review panel (rename, member count, orphaned-member badge, intent textarea, per-member notes, exemption checkboxes with a required-reason field, acceptance-criteria text, Copy + Download); a "Make set from N results" affordance in SearchOverlay; an "+ Set" affordance on the component detail header. Types (viewer/src/types.ts): optional `Finding`/`Concern` + `architecture.findings?`/`concerns?`, and `SelectionSet`/`SetMember`/`SetAnnotation`.
  - REGRESSION TESTS (real store, no store mock; finding/concern fixtures are real rows lifted from a v2 projection of this repo). `viewer/src/__tests__/selectionSets.test.tsx` (9): create-from-finding (2 members with stable fragment refs `analyzer/derive/docs.py:149` / `analyzer/scanner.py:2509` carrying file + line spans 149-202 / 2509-2582), create-from-concern (Logging concern -> 2 component members with `import` signal evidence), unknown-id returns null, persistence round-trip (set + intent + member note survive a `simulateReload` against the same jsdom localStorage), orphan retention after a changed architecture, ReviewSummary renders the set card with a visible "1 orphaned" badge (never silently dropped, P1-3 pattern), manual add-to-set create/append/de-dupe, quota-write safety, delete persists and drops the annotation. `viewer/src/__tests__/directiveGenerator.test.ts` (6): intent present and every member carries file + numeric line_start/line_end; origin auto-suggestion merged with free text; exemptions move members out of scope AND a blank reason does NOT exempt (no unjustified exemption); the fenced JSON parses, its `schema` matches, and its member/exemption refs match the model exactly; orphaned member kept and marked; concern-kind-specific + finding-kind acceptance suggestions.
  - FAIL-BEFORE PROOF: disabling the sets restore in `setArchitecture` (`selectionSets: []`, `setAnnotations: []`) made exactly the 3 persistence/orphan-visibility tests fail (`survives a hard reload`, `keeps orphaned members after a changed architecture`, `renders a selection set in ReviewSummary with an orphaned badge`) while the 6 creation/dedupe/quota/delete tests still passed; restoring the fix -> 9 passed. The directive JSON-parse and exemption-reason assertions are the directive spec's own guards.
  - REAL-BROWSER SMOKE (vite dev on :5199 over a fresh v2 split projection of this repo with 50 findings / 6 concerns; Chrome via playwriter): dismissed the onboarding modal, opened search (Cmd+K), typed "logger", clicked "Make set from 50 results" -> a real set "Search: logger" (50 members) rendered in the review panel; filled the shared-intent field ("All logging must route through the shared structured logger, level from config."); expanded "Export directive", added an acceptance line, clicked "Copy directive" (button flipped to "Copied directive", proving the clipboard write path ran), then clicked "Download .md" and captured the exact 50195-byte blob the real `downloadText` helper produced. The captured markdown carries the Intent / Members / Acceptance criteria / Machine-readable work order sections, the intent text, and a fenced JSON block that parses to `schema: solution-explorer.directive/v1` with 50 members (each member evidence-bearing: search match plus resolved component path, capabilities, and rules from the architecture) and the free-text acceptance criterion. Zero console errors and zero page errors across the whole flow (`state.errors` empty at every step). Clipboard READ-BACK and the OS download event / CDP session are not observable through the playwriter extension (they hang or error); verified via the Blob-URL capture hook instead, which exercises the identical generator + download path.
  - VERIFICATION: viewer `npm test -- --run` -> 260 passed (30 files, +15 new P6-9 tests over the 245 baseline after prior merges); `npm run lint` -> 0 warnings; `npx tsc -b` -> exit 0; `npm run build` -> clean (only the pre-existing 500 kB elk-chunk advisory). `pytest tests/ -q` (run once) -> 916 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`), 1 failed = `test_coverage_ledger.py::test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents`, the known git-WORKTREE `.git`-pointer environmental failure already recorded twice in Discovered rows; this stream is viewer-only (zero analyzer/python edits), so it passes on a normal checkout. Committed root `architecture.json` untouched; the committed `viewer/public/architecture` demo dataset was regenerated only for the smoke and restored (`git checkout` + `git clean`), leaving `git status` carrying only viewer/src source and this card.
  - DEVIATIONS: (1) PREMISE: P6-8 (Concerns/Findings surfaces, its `stagedFindingSet` seam, and the disabled export-directive stub) has NOT landed on origin/main, contrary to the card's spec. P6-9 shipped the full engine plus the creation gestures whose UI exists today (search "make set", detail "+ Set", and set management/annotation/export in the review panel); creation-from-finding and creation-from-concern are real store actions proven against real projection rows, ready for P6-8's surface to call. The FindingsSurface-specific wiring ("consume stagedFindingSet, replacing the fallback"; "the export-directive stub becomes live") is deferred to P6-8. Recorded in Discovered rows. (2) Search/manual members legitimately carry a file path but no line number (search results have no line span); finding/concern members carry full file + line spans (proven in the unit tests). The directive renders "no file location" / omits the line where the origin has none, which is honest rather than fabricating a line.
  - INTEGRATION LANDED (2026-07-14, branch program2/p6-tours Task A): the deferred FindingsSurface wiring this card named is now built. P6-8's finding and concern rows call the P6-9 engine directly: `annotateFindingSet`/`annotateConcernSet` build real sets via `createSetFromFinding`/`createSetFromConcern` and open the set annotation flow; `exportDirectiveForFinding`/`exportDirectiveForConcern` build (or reuse) the set and render the directive via this card's `generateDirective`. `findingsToSets.test.ts` proves the full finding-to-directive flow (set -> annotation -> parseable `solution-explorer.directive/v1` JSON whose members match the finding). The P6-9 store actions and `directiveGenerator` were the seam, exactly as this card's Discovered row anticipated.

### P6-10: Non-source inventory (repo totality)
- Status: DONE (Opus 4.8 executor, branch program2/p6-inventory, 2026-07-14)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design authority: this card. Owner directive: the unit of totality is the REPO, not the source subset. Everything that reaches the central repo is a first-class citizen; for every non-source file the tool must offer, deterministically and without AI, what it is, why it is probably there, and a high-level recommendation. Context decides meaning (a .md under docs/ is documentation; a .md under .claude/ or a prompts/ directory is agent/system-prompt content that is part of the product). AI may later elaborate but must not be required (I1, I9). Ground truth that motivated the card: the unamentis-ios demo showed "6,046 non-source files accounted for", and 5,970 of them (98.7%) are one accidentally committed TestResults.xcresult bundle plus a .DS_Store; the tool counted them honestly but explained nothing. The feature must make that discovery unavoidable: gross disproportion between source and non-source is itself a finding.
- Scope:
  - Analyzer (Tier 3/4, zero source reads: paths, sizes, ledger dispositions, and .gitignore only): classify every non-parsed ledger row into a category taxonomy via deterministic context rules (path components + extension, never extension alone). Categories at minimum: documentation; agent/prompt content (.claude/, CLAUDE.md, prompts/, *.instructions.md); product assets (asset catalogs, images/fonts/media under resource roots); localization; build/test output (*.xcresult, .build, DerivedData, coverage artifacts, *.log) with a cleanup recommendation; IDE/workspace metadata (*.xcodeproj internals, .idea, .vscode); OS cruft (.DS_Store, Thumbs.db) with a remove+ignore recommendation; VCS/CI infra (.git*, .github, hooks); config (plists, yml/toml/json the parser did not claim); lockfiles; certificates/keys/secrets-shaped files (flag security_sensitive, recommend verifying intent); archives; data files; vendored; unknown (loud, never silently "other").
  - Per group emit: category id, label, one-line explanation, recommendation string, count, total bytes where cheaply available, extension breakdown, top directories, bounded sample paths, flags (security_sensitive, likely_unwanted, gitignore_candidate).
  - Projection: an `inventory` section (coverage.json or inventory.json, executor's call, additive and versioned); old datasets degrade to the current panel (no fake data).
  - Viewer: from the coverage drill-in's NON-SOURCE ACCOUNTED family, an "Explore inventory" affordance opens the inventory view: groups ranked by count/bytes (I11), explanation + recommendation visible, expandable file lists, tooltips through tooltipCopy, every file path drillable. Disproportion cue: when non-source dwarfs source, say so plainly and point at the dominant group.
  - Shape leaves room for a future ai_enhance overlay per group (do not build the enrichment pass now).
- Accept: on a synthesized xcresult-heavy fixture the build/test-output group carries the cleanup recommendation and dominates the ranking; product assets read as expected-here, not as a problem; a .md under a prompts/ dir classifies as agent content while docs/*.md classifies as documentation; a .pem fixture flags security_sensitive; unknown files land in a loud unknown group; viewer renders groups ranked with recommendations and drillable members; old datasets without `inventory` render exactly as today; fail-before proofs on the classifier tests; both CI parser tiers green.
- Verify: pytest full suite; viewer vitest + lint + tsc + build; real-browser smoke on the regenerated iOS projection (the TestResults.xcresult discovery must be readable in the UI in one drill).
- Future (recorded, not in scope): pattern-based security/prompt-injection scanning over non-source content; per-file git-history context (when added, by whom) for recommendations.
- Evidence:
  - SHIPPED. Analyzer (`analyzer/project/inventory.py`, Tier 3/4, zero source reads): `classify_row(path, disposition, reason)` is a pure deterministic classifier using path components AND extension together, first-match-wins over 15 categories (documentation, agent_content, product_assets, localization, build_test_output, ide_metadata, os_cruft, vcs_ci, config, lockfile, secrets, archive, data, vendored, and a loud unknown). `build_inventory(rows, root)` aggregates the non-source family into ranked groups, each carrying id, label, one-line explanation, recommendation, count, bytes (statted when the tree is available, null otherwise), extension breakdown, top directories, a bounded 200-path sample, directory_rows, and flags (security_sensitive, likely_unwanted, gitignore_candidate). The non-source family mirrors the CoverageBadge `NON_SOURCE_DISPOSITIONS` exactly (binary, unsupported_extension, empty_file, skipped_directory, vendored_repo), so analyzer and viewer agree on scope; source gaps (failed, oversized) stay in the coverage gaps family where they already live loudly. The pruned-directory ruling is honored: a pruned dir is ONE row counted as one and recorded in `directory_rows`, never expanded into fabricated per-file counts, and it contributes no bytes.
  - CONTEXT RULES, NOT EXTENSION ALONE: `docs/guide.md` classifies as documentation while `prompts/system.md`, `.claude/...`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `*.instructions.md` classify as agent_content; a `.pem`, `id_rsa`, or `.env*` flags security_sensitive; a path inside a `.xcresult` bundle or any build/coverage directory is build_test_output regardless of extension; an unrecognized non-source file lands in the loud `unknown` group, never a silent "other". The gitignore_candidate flag reads the repo's top-level `.gitignore` (the only file this module opens beyond a stat) and is suppressed for members already ignored, so the tool never nags to add an entry that exists.
  - PROJECTION: additive and versioned. `build_coverage(store, root)` attaches an `inventory` section (`version`, `non_source_total`, `source_gap_total`, ranked `groups`, and `dominant` when the top group is more than half of all non-source rows) to the coverage payload, which rides in `coverage.json` (split) and inline under `architecture.coverage.inventory` (monolith). The manifest summary slice (`coverage_manifest_summary`) is untouched, so the badge still reads a lightweight summary. The key is omitted entirely when there are no non-source rows (`build_inventory` returns None), so an old dataset degrades to exactly the pre-inventory coverage output. Bytes are environment data like the git remote in `prepare_arch`: present when the tree is available, absent on a re-projection from the store alone, both proven.
  - VIEWER: from the coverage drill-in's NON-SOURCE ACCOUNTED family, an "Explore inventory" affordance (`data-testid=inventory-explore`, gated on a loaded inventory so old datasets never show it) opens `InventoryPanel.tsx`, a modal consistent with FindingsSurface (backdrop, Escape, `role=dialog`, `max-w-2xl`, dark-mode and ~390px friendly with `flex-wrap` headers and `px-3`). Groups render pre-ranked by the analyzer (count then bytes, I11); each shows its explanation, a highlighted recommendation, flag badges (security/likely unwanted/gitignore?), a dominant badge, an extension breakdown, top directories, and a `VirtualList` sample of drillable file paths wired to `openFileDeepLink` (a non-source file with no owning component records an honest missing-owner notice, never a throw). The disproportion cue (`data-testid=inventory-disproportion`) fires when non-source outnumbers analyzed source by more than 3 to 1 and names the dominant group in plain language with its recommendation. All copy routes through `tooltipCopy.ts` (`TOOLTIP_COPY.inventory.*`, added to `SWEPT_SURFACES`), no em or en dashes anywhere.
  - TESTS. Analyzer `tests/test_inventory.py` (16): context rules (docs vs prompts vs .claude vs CLAUDE.md, .pem/id_rsa/.env security, xcresult/.build/.log build output, xcodeproj IDE, node_modules vendored, .DS_Store cruft, assets, localization, config, lockfile), the explicit FAIL-BEFORE proof (`_ext_only` stand-in shows extension-alone collapses docs and prompts into one bucket while `classify_row` separates them), the xcresult-heavy dominant-group + cleanup-recommendation ranking, product assets read as expected-here (likely_unwanted False, "keep"), pruned-directory-counts-as-one, unknown-is-loud, order-independent determinism, bytes-with-tree vs bytes-absent-without-tree, source-only tree emits no inventory key (degradation), on-disk `project_split` coverage.json carries the inventory, and gitignore_candidate suppressed when already ignored. Viewer `viewer/src/__tests__/inventoryPanel.test.tsx` (7, real store + only fetch stubbed exactly as the live viewer fetches coverage.json, inventory object lifted verbatim from a real v2 projection): affordance appears only after the inventory loads and opens the panel; no affordance for an old dataset without inventory (degradation); groups ranked build_test_output first; every group's recommendation visible plus dominant and security badges; the disproportion cue names the dominant group; expand reveals the virtualized file list and extension breakdown; a file path drills through the real store navigation. Plus 4 `SWEPT_SURFACES` inventory entries in the tooltip sweep.
  - VERIFICATION: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 993 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`), 1 failed = `test_coverage_ledger.py::test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents`, the known git-WORKTREE `.git`-pointer environmental failure the card flagged in advance (in a worktree `.git` is a file, not a pruned directory); it passes on a normal checkout and in CI. Viewer `npx vitest run` -> 329 passed (37 files, +10 over the 319 baseline), `npm run lint` -> 0 warnings, `npx tsc -b` -> exit 0, `npm run build` -> clean (only the pre-existing 500 kB elk-chunk advisory). Committed root `architecture.json` untouched (conftest guard green); no regenerated demo dataset committed.
  - DEVIATIONS: (1) SCOPE READING: the inventory classifies the non-source family (the badge's NON-SOURCE ACCOUNTED set), not literally every non-parsed row, because failed and oversized rows are analyzable SOURCE already surfaced loudly in the coverage gaps family; every non-source row is classified and unknown is loud, satisfying every accept criterion (the .pem, the xcresult bundle, docs vs prompts, and the loud unknown are all non-source). Recorded as the coherent reading of "repo totality" (source gaps are shown, just in their own family). (2) The full per-file enumeration of a huge group is not duplicated inline; the group carries a bounded 200-path virtualized sample and the complete list remains one panel away in the coverage ledger rows (which already carry every row), so nothing is hidden and the classifier is never re-implemented in TypeScript (the card's analyzer/viewer-must-agree caution). (3) The `ai_enhance`-per-group overlay is left as shape only (optional future key), not built, per the card.

### P6-11: Coverage language and inventory vocabulary (fast-follow)
- Status: DONE (2026-07-19, Opus 4.8). Branch program2/p6-11-language-vocab.
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Scope: (1) The v2 CLI stdout summary must speak the three-family coverage semantics, matching the badge: replace the raw "Coverage: 651/712 parsed" line with source-analyzed percent plus gap count plus non-source accounted count. v1 output untouched (parity). (2) Inventory vocabulary from the unamentis-ios unknowns: .hooks (vcs_ci), .swiftformat and similar tool dotfiles (config), *.xcframework bundles (vendored, they are third-party binary frameworks), .xcprivacy (config), .storyboard/.xib (classified sensibly as UI resources with an honest explanation, not unknown; parsing them is P4-9), .xcdatamodeld internals (data; parsing is P4-9), and backup droppings (*.backup, *.bak, *.orig, trailing-tilde) flagged likely_unwanted and gitignore_candidate. (3) Acceptance: the unamentis-ios fixture set yields ZERO unknown rows; every new rule has a fail-before test; the CLI line change has a test asserting the new wording and the absence of the old.
- Verify: full pytest, ruff; no viewer changes expected (copy comes from the analyzer only if surfaced); PR protocol.
- Evidence:
  - CLI LANGUAGE (analyzer/project/run.py, analyzer/project/coverage.py). The v2 run summary's `Coverage: {parsed}/{total} parsed` line is replaced by the three-family form: `Coverage: {pct}% of source analyzed ({analyzed} parsed, {N} gap[s]); {M} non-source accounted for`. The math is a new Python mirror of the viewer's CoverageBadge, added to `coverage.py` and reused (single source of truth for the family split): `coverage_families(summary)` classifies each disposition exactly as the viewer's `computeCoverageFamilies` (parsed = analyzed; the shared `NON_SOURCE_DISPOSITIONS` frozenset imported from inventory.py = non-source; everything else that is not parsed = a loud source gap), and the percent denominator is analyzed + gaps only (never the non-source rows), so a repo dominated by assets/vendored code never reads as low coverage. `format_source_percent(fam)` reproduces the viewer's `formatSourcePercent` including the anti-round-up rule (a real gap never rounds up to a bare 100; capped at 99.9) and uses `math.floor(x*10 + 0.5)/10` to match JavaScript `Math.round` (round half up) rather than Python's banker's rounding. The gap noun is singular for exactly one gap. v1 is untouched: run.py is the v2-only entry point (run_v2), so the v1 stdout and all P4/P5 parity snapshots are byte-identical (full suite green, parity tests included).
  - VOCABULARY (analyzer/project/inventory.py). Ordered rules added to `classify_row`, each ahead of the broader rule it must beat: `.hooks` added to `_VCS_DIR_TOKENS` -> vcs_ci; a new early backup rule (before secrets/config) sends `_BACKUP_EXTS` = {.backup, .bak, .orig} and trailing-tilde filenames to a new `backup_files` category (likely_unwanted + gitignore_candidate, "remove and gitignore" recommendation; trailing tilde is matched on the whole filename because splitext keeps the `~` on the extension side); a `.xcframework` path-component check in the vendored section (before assets/config/data) sends every bundle internal to vendored; `.storyboard`/`.xib` -> a new `ui_definition` category ("Interface Builder UI", honest explanation that parsing into screens is not done yet, P4-9); a `.xcdatamodeld`/`.xcdatamodel` path-component check -> data (data's explanation now names Core Data models); `.xcprivacy` added to `_CONFIG_EXTS` and a new `_CONFIG_NAMES` set (`.swiftformat`, `.swiftlint.yml`, and peers) -> config for whole-name tool dotfiles that carry no useful extension. Two new CATEGORY_META entries (ui_definition, backup_files); the viewer is fully data-driven over group.id/label/explanation/recommendation/flags (InventoryPanel.tsx renders whatever groups arrive, no hardcoded id list), so the new categories render with zero viewer change.
  - TESTS (all fail-before by construction: before this card each new path returned "unknown" or the old wording). tests/test_inventory.py: seven per-rule classifier tests (`test_hooks_directory_is_vcs_infrastructure`, `test_tool_config_dotfiles_are_config`, `test_xcframework_bundle_internals_are_vendored`, `test_xcprivacy_is_config`, `test_interface_builder_files_are_ui_definitions_not_unknown`, `test_core_data_model_internals_are_data`, `test_backup_droppings_are_flagged_for_removal`), a table test over the exact unamentis-ios unknown set asserting each intended category and none unknown (`test_unamentis_ios_unknown_set_reaches_zero`), and a real extract+build_inventory end-to-end test on a synthesized tree mirroring those unknowns asserting ZERO unknown rows (`test_ios_unknowns_tree_yields_zero_unknown_rows`, no mocks). tests/test_cli.py TestCoverageLineLanguage: `test_full_coverage_reads_as_100_percent_of_source` (100% wording, "0 gaps", "non-source accounted for" present; old `\d+/\d+ parsed` regex absent) and `test_a_real_gap_is_not_rounded_up_to_100` (one oversized file excluded by an explicit bound reads "1 gap" singular, never "100% of source analyzed", old wording absent).
  - VERIFICATION: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 1005 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing test_detect_ports), 1 failed = `test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents` (the known git-worktree environmental failure: a worktree has a `.git` FILE not a directory, so the pruned-`.git`-directory row does not appear; passes in CI on a normal checkout). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/` clean. Viewer untouched (no shared source string changed; inventory copy flows as data), so the viewer suite was not required by the card's conditional and was not run.
  - DEVIATIONS: (1) chose a dedicated `ui_definition` category for storyboards/xib over broadening product_assets, because a storyboard is a UI layout definition that P4-9 will parse, not a shipped media asset; the honest explanation says so. (2) chose a dedicated `backup_files` category (the card's cleanest option) so the group carries a single "remove and gitignore" recommendation and the likely_unwanted + gitignore_candidate flags in one place. (3) `.xcdatamodeld` internals route to the existing `data` category (per the card's "-> data") with the category explanation extended to name Core Data models, rather than adding a Core-Data-specific category, to keep the taxonomy KISS.

### P6-12: Project knowledge layer (.solution-explorer/rules)
- Status: DONE (elaborated and implemented 2026-07-19, Opus 4.8; branch program2/p6-12-knowledge-layer)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design intent: the product self-matures per project. When an AI pass (enrichment today; merge-time automation later) figures out what an unknown artifact is, that knowledge becomes a permanent, deterministic, project-local rule. Rules are data; no AI at parse or query time (I9). The unknown bucket trends to zero per project without core-product changes.
- Scope:
  - Rule store: `.solution-explorer/rules/inventory.yml` in the analyzed repo. Schema per rule: pattern (gitignore-style glob), category (must be a known category id), optional label/explanation/recommendation overrides, optional flags, source (ai-enrichment | human), added (date), evidence (list of paths that motivated it), contribute (bool, scaffold for the future upstream-contribution path, no behavior yet).
  - Loader: ordered rule sources, defaults then (future org level, scaffolded as an empty slot in the order) then repo. Repo rules win over built-ins for matching paths. Fully deterministic, bad rules rejected loudly with a clear message, never silently skipped.
  - Linguist compatibility: honor `.gitattributes` overrides linguist-vendored, linguist-documentation, linguist-generated as classification input (the existing GitHub-ecosystem standard), mapped to vendored / documentation / build_test_output respectively. Project rules beat gitattributes beat built-ins.
  - Provenance in the projection: inventory groups and rows record which rule id fired (project rule vs built-in vs gitattributes); the viewer shows it (tooltip through tooltipCopy: "classified by a rule this project taught itself").
  - Enrichment writes rules: the enhance pass, when it identifies an unknown, appends a rule with source ai-enrichment and evidence to the repo rule file (dedupe on pattern+category; never overwrite human rules).
  - KISS bound: one YAML file, one loader, glob matching with the same semantics .gitignore users expect. The parser-rules tier (tree-sitter .scm envelope) is documented in the card as the NEXT tier of this folder, not built now.
- Accept: loader precedence proven (project rule overrides built-in for the same path; gitattributes honored; bad rule rejected loudly); enrichment writes a valid rule for a real unknown on a real store (no mocks) and a second run classifies it deterministically with zero AI; projection carries rule provenance; viewer renders it; unamentis-ios dogfood shows unknowns going to zero via generated rules; self-repo dogfood clean.
- Verify: full pytest, viewer suite, browser proof on the regenerated iOS demo; PR protocol.
- SCHEMA AS SHIPPED (`.solution-explorer/rules/inventory.yml`, a mapping with a top-level `rules:` list; per rule): `id` (stable slug, required, unique per file), `pattern` (gitignore-style glob, required), `category` (a known CATEGORY_META id, required; unknown rejected loudly), `source` ("ai-enrichment" | "human", required), `label`/`explanation`/`recommendation` (optional group overrides), `flags` (optional partial override of security_sensitive/likely_unwanted/gitignore_candidate), `added` (optional ISO date), `evidence` (optional list of repo paths), `contribute` (optional bool, schema-only scaffold, no behavior). Glob semantics: `*` within a segment, `**` across segments, `?` one non-slash char, a slash-free pattern matches the basename at any depth, a pattern with a slash is anchored to the repo root, a directory match covers everything beneath it.
- Evidence:
  - LOADER + PRECEDENCE (`analyzer/project/rules.py`). `load_rule_set(root, extra_sources=())` reads ordered sources low-to-high precedence: an org-level slot (scaffolded empty via `extra_sources`; only the repo file is wired) then `<root>/.solution-explorer/rules/inventory.yml`. Higher precedence is placed FIRST in `project_rules` so a first-match evaluation lets the repo rule win; within a file, file order wins. Full classification precedence is project rules > `.gitattributes` Linguist overrides > built-in `classify_row`, implemented in `inventory._classify_with_provenance`. Proven: `test_project_rule_overrides_builtin_for_the_same_path` (fail-before: the built-in calls a `.log` file build_test_output; a project rule reclassifies that exact path to documentation), `test_repo_rule_beats_org_slot_later_source_wins`, `test_within_a_file_first_matching_rule_wins`, `test_project_rule_beats_gitattributes_beats_builtin`.
  - LOUD REJECTION. Bad rules (unknown category, invalid/empty glob, missing id/pattern, invalid source, duplicate id) are named on stderr and skipped individually; the rest of the file still loads. `test_bad_rule_is_rejected_loudly_and_the_rest_still_load` writes one good rule plus a bad-category, a bad-glob, and a bad-source rule and asserts only `good` survives with each problem named on stderr. A malformed whole file (bad YAML or wrong top-level shape) warns and skips the whole file, never crashing.
  - GITATTRIBUTES (Linguist). `_parse_gitattributes` maps `linguist-vendored`->vendored, `linguist-documentation`->documentation, `linguist-generated`->build_test_output; SET forms `attr` and `attr=true` honored, unset `-attr` and `attr=false` ignored; last matching line wins (gitattributes semantics). `test_gitattributes_linguist_overrides_are_honored` covers all three maps plus the two ignored forms.
  - GRACEFUL DEGRADATION WITHOUT PyYAML. The minimal CI lane (`architecture-viz.yml` generate job) installs no extras, so PyYAML may be absent. The loader's `_yaml` is `None` then; a rules file that exists is skipped with one loud stderr warning, never a crash. `test_loader_degrades_loudly_without_pyyaml` monkeypatches `rules._yaml = None` and asserts zero rules applied, "PyYAML is not installed" on stderr, and `build_inventory` still runs (the `.qzz` stays unknown). PyYAML is present in the tree venv (6.0.3) but is NOT a declared dependency (Required-by empty), so the degradation path is real, not theoretical.
  - PROVENANCE IN THE PROJECTION. `build_inventory` records, per group, a `rule_provenance` breakdown ({source: count} over "builtin" / "gitattributes" / "project:<rule-id>") and a `sample_provenance` list aligned with `samples`. Both are additive and emitted ONLY when a non-built-in source classified at least one row, so old datasets and rule-free repos are byte-identical (the pre-P6-12 26 test_inventory.py cases stay green unchanged). `test_provenance_recorded_per_group_and_per_sample` and `test_no_rules_means_no_provenance_keys_old_dataset_parity` prove both sides. Optional rule overrides land at the group level (`test_rule_overrides_group_label_and_recommendation`).
  - ENRICHMENT WRITES RULES. `analyzer/enrich/rules_writer.py:write_inventory_rules` is a PURE function over disk: it validates each proposed rule (known category, compilable glob), dedupes on (pattern, category), NEVER overwrites a human rule (existing entries re-read raw and preserved; a proposed rule contradicting a human pattern is skipped and reported), assigns a stable id, and writes a file the loader accepts. `analyzer/enrich/identify.py:identify_unknowns` is the AI pass: it reads the store coverage ledger, collects the still-unknown non-source paths (after applying current rules, so already-learned files are not re-proposed), builds `prompts.build_identify_unknowns_prompt`, invokes through the injectable `Invoker` seam, validates, and writes. `test_identify_writes_a_rule_and_second_projection_classifies_with_zero_ai` builds a REAL store from a real repo (store kept OUTSIDE the repo so its sqlite sidecars are not inventory rows), drives a canned invoker (the established P7 seam; no live shell-out), and proves the written rule reclassifies `telemetry.qzz` out of unknown on a SECOND projection with zero AI. Writer unit tests: `test_writer_round_trips_through_the_loader`, `test_writer_dedupes_and_never_overwrites_human_rules`. Idempotence: a re-run writes nothing (all duplicates). Cost controls: `test_identify_dry_run_invokes_nothing` (ExplodingInvoker), `test_identify_no_unknowns_is_a_clean_noop`.
  - CLI. `analyze.py identify unknowns [root] --store <db> [--max-targets N] [--dry-run] [--model M] [--report F]`, dispatched from `analyzer/cli.py:main` on the first token (single entrypoint, same pattern as enhance/verify/name). Real dry-run against a polyglot fixture store printed "2 unknown path(s) ... dry run: no model invoked; prompt is 3402 chars"; the missing-store guard exits 2 with a readable message. No `architecture.json` or `.solution-explorer/` committed (proof artifacts in the scratchpad).
  - VIEWER. `InventoryPanel` shows a "learned" badge on any group a project rule classified (green, tooltip "Classified by a rule this project taught itself ...") and a per-row emerald dot on the exact sample paths a project rule classified. Copy through `tooltipCopy.ts` (`inventory.projectRule`, `inventory.projectRuleRow`), no em/en dashes. Types added additively to `types.ts` (`InventoryGroup.rule_provenance?`, `.sample_provenance?`). Viewer tests use a genuine analyzer snapshot (a learned data group): `marks a group learned ...`, `dots the exact sample row a project rule classified, not the built-in row` (exactly one dot on the project row), `shows no learned marker for a group with only built-in provenance`.
  - VERIFICATION. `pytest tests/ -q` -> 1030 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`), 1 failed = the standing worktree-only `test_pruned_directory_row_stands_in_for_its_contents` (`.git` is a pointer file in a worktree, not a directory; environmental, already recorded, passes on a normal checkout). +20 new tests in `tests/test_project_rules.py`. `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` clean. Viewer: `npx vitest run` -> 333 passed (37 files), `npm run lint` 0 warnings, `npx tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` succeeds.
  - DEVIATIONS: (1) The AI rule-writer ships as a dedicated `identify unknowns` pass, not folded into `enhance` (Discovered row: focused, testable, honestly an enrichment pass; composing into one orchestration run is a one-call follow-up). (2) The parser-rules tier (tree-sitter `.scm` envelope) is documented as the NEXT tier in `rules.py` and deliberately not built (KISS bound). (3) Overrides resolve at the category/group level in rule-id order (Discovered row). (4) The writer preserves human rule VALUES exactly but reformats the whole file (no ruamel; zero-required-dependency bound). (5) Multi-repo v2 does not apply per-repo rules, consistent with the standing multi-repo deferral family. (6) unamentis-ios dogfood (unknowns->zero via a live AI run) and the browser proof on the regenerated iOS demo are owner-run steps: they need a live `claude` invocation plus a rebuild/wrangler redeploy, out of this worktree's territory; the deterministic loop is proven end-to-end on a real store with the canned-invoker seam.

## Phase 7: AI enrichment industrialization

### P7-1: Enrichment provenance and staleness
- Status: DONE (elaborated and implemented 2026-07-13, Opus 4.8 Stream A). Branch program2/p7-provenance.
- Model: Opus 4.8 (no substitution)
- Design: TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md section 6; invariant I5
- Scope: define the component-files digest; enrichment rows carry derived_from_hash and commit_sha; staleness computed and surfaced in projections, viewer (marker on AI content), and later MCP; migration for the existing ai_enhance baseline. Builds on P3-3's shipped drift-tolerant matcher (DONE, PR #6): provenance makes matching unnecessary because identity plus digest travel with the enrichment; retire the merge-script path from CI only after a parallel-run validation period shows parity with the P3-3 matcher on real deploys.
- Elaboration (Do / Accept / Verify):
  - Do: (1) freeze the component-files digest and the symbol/relationship/architecture digests in a new `analyzer/enrich/` package docstring (the P7-1 deferred decision of TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md section 12). (2) Write-path helpers (`analyzer/enrich/provenance.py`) that stamp `derived_from_hash` (from the frozen definition), `commit_sha` (from git, nullable), and `created_at` (injectable clock) via the existing `store.add_enrichment` upsert. (3) Read-time staleness (`analyzer/enrich/staleness.py`): a `DigestIndex` over the store plus a join returning every enrichment row marked fresh/stale/unknown, never dropping stale rows. (4) Export overlay (`analyzer/enrich/overlay.py`, wired into `analyzer/project/pipeline.py`): the store enrichment overlay is canonical, applied onto the arch dict with a `stale`/`derived_from_commit` marker, plus the P4-5 search-shard dedupe reversal (store wins over inline). (5) Import bridge (`analyzer/enrich/importer.py` + `scripts/import-ai-baseline.py`): read an ai_enhance architecture.json baseline into provenance-stamped enrichment rows, stamped with the CURRENT digest and an explicit imported flag. The merge-script path stays untouched (parallel-run period, not this task).
  - Accept: digest is store-only and stable across runs and PYTHONHASHSEED; enrichment rows carry derived_from_hash + commit_sha + created_at; a changed-file scenario flips staleness in both the store query and the projection with the payload still served; the real committed baseline imports 23/23 components with digests; store overlay wins over inline ai_enhance; merge script and all prior tests still green.
  - Verify: `pytest tests/ -q`, `ruff check`, and a live import+project round-trip on the committed baseline.
- Evidence:
  - FROZEN DIGEST DEFINITION (analyzer/enrich/__init__.py, frozen once merged). All digests are blake2b-256 hex, computed store-only so they are stable across runs and PYTHONHASHSEED (blake2b is keyless/unsalted, unlike Python `hash`). (1) COMPONENT: ordered blake2b over the component's member files' `(path, content_hash)` pairs sorted by path, absorbing `path + \x00 + content_hash + \n` per file; members and content_hash come from the store's `component_files` join and `files` table (content_hash = sha256 of the file bytes). An empty component hashes to a fixed digest. (2) SYMBOL: blake2b over `file_content_hash + \x00 + symbol_id`; the store symbol id is span-normalized by construction (no line number). (3) RELATIONSHIP: blake2b over `source_digest + \x00 + target_digest + \x00 + type`, where each endpoint contributes its component digest, or a stable stand-in `blake2b("id:" + id)` if it is not a component. (4) ARCHITECTURE: blake2b over the ordered `(component_id, digest)` list sorted by component id. Target-id conventions: component -> component id; symbol -> symbol id; relationship -> `"<source>|<target>|<type>"`; architecture -> the sentinel `@architecture`. Staleness is computed at read time (current digest != stamped digest), never stored; stale enrichment is served with a marker, never dropped (I5).
  - STALENESS FLIP PROOF: `tests/test_enrich_provenance.py::test_staleness_flip_through_real_extract` is the gate's named scenario end to end: extract+derive the polyglot fixture, enrich a component (fresh), append a line to one member file, re-extract (warm) + re-derive into the same store (enrichment survives clear_extraction_facts and the derive _flush), then assert the row reads `stale: True` in both `enrichment_staleness(store)` and the projection overlay, with the payload still served (`help_text` intact). A unit form (`test_staleness_flips_on_content_hash_change`) mutates a member file's content_hash directly and asserts the same flip; `test_fresh_right_after_stamp` and `test_unstamped_row_has_unknown_staleness` (None verdict for a legacy un-stamped row) bracket it.
  - IMPORT-BRIDGE RESULT ON THE REAL COMMITTED BASELINE: against a v2 store built from this repo (`analyze.py . --split --store <db>`), `import_ai_baseline(store, architecture.json)` imported 23/23 ai-enhanced components (exact-id match, 0 unmatched), 23/23 ai relationships, and the architecture root, for 47 enrichment rows, every row stamped with its current digest and marked fresh (0 stale) immediately after import. Projecting that store (`project_monolith` with the store overlay) emitted ai_enhance on 23 components + arch-level + 23 relationships, each carrying `_provenance = {imported: True, source: ai_enhance-baseline, commit: unknown}` and no stale marker (store matches current code). Why current digest + imported flag: an import asserts the baseline text matches the code as-imported, so the current digest makes it read fresh now and go stale only on later change; stamping an unknown historical digest would make everything read stale immediately. commit_sha is null (unknown) with the imported flag recording that provenance was asserted at import, not observed at enhancement.
  - DEDUPE REVERSAL NOTE (recorded decision): the P4-5 Discovered row noted that an inline component `ai_enhance.help_text` and a store enrichment row for the same target deduped to one search entry with inline winning (components processed first). Reversed in P7-1: the store overlay is canonical. `analyzer/project/search_shards.py` now adds store enrichment BEFORE inline (first-wins dedupe => store wins), and `analyzer/enrich/overlay.py` replaces any inline ai_enhance with the store payload. Fail-before confirmed: with the pre-reversal order the search entry resolves to the inline text ("INLINE help"), which `test_search_shards_store_enrichment_wins_over_inline` rejects; with the reversal it resolves to the store text ("STORE help"). `test_overlay_store_wins_over_inline_ai_enhance` asserts the same for the projection overlay.
  - VERIFICATION: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 917 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing test_detect_ports); the 17 new tests in tests/test_enrich_provenance.py are all green, including `test_digests_stable_across_pythonhashseed` (two subprocess runs with PYTHONHASHSEED=0 and =1 emit identical component/symbol/relationship/architecture digests). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` clean. The overlay is a no-op when the store has no enrichment (`test_overlay_is_noop_without_enrichment`), so parity fixtures and non-enriched projections are byte-unchanged; the P4-5 parity snapshots stayed green in the full run. The merge script (scripts/merge-ai-enhancements.py) and its tests are untouched and green (parallel-run period preserved; retiring it from CI is explicitly NOT this task).
  - DEVIATIONS: none in scope. The viewer is untouched (staleness markers land in projections only; viewer rendering is later work per the card). No schema change was needed: the P4-1 enrichment table already fits (target_kind, target_id, payload_json, derived_from_hash, commit_sha, created_at, UNIQUE(target_kind,target_id)); this task adds only helpers, so SCHEMA_VERSION stays 4.

### P7-2: Headless enrichment CLI
- Status: DONE (elaborated and implemented 2026-07-13, Opus 4.8 Stream A). Branch program2/p7-enhance.
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Scope: `solution-explorer enhance` running DPEA over the store via the Claude CLI headlessly, no hardcoded paths, partition/parallel limits honored, quality scorer enforced as a gate, --update mode re-enhancing only stale/new scopes plus architectural neighbors; the /ai-assist skill becomes a thin wrapper; CI-callable with cost controls (documented flags for partition caps).
- Elaboration (Do / Accept / Verify):
  - Entry shape (RECORDED): `python3 analyze.py enhance [root] --store <path> [options]`. `enhance` is a subcommand word dispatched from `analyzer/cli.py:main()` before the flag-based argparse (the existing CLI is positional-path + flags, so a first-token dispatch keeps analyze.py the single entrypoint and avoids restructuring the whole parser). The engine lives in `analyzer/enrich/` (partition.py, prompts.py, engine.py) and the CLI in `analyzer/enrich/enhance_cli.py`.
  - Invocation mechanism (RECORDED): the Python Agent SDK is NOT installed (`pip show claude-agent-sdk` empty); the `claude` CLI is (v2.1.110). The engine invokes `claude -p --output-format json --model <model>` with the per-partition prompt on stdin, parses the envelope's `result` (model text), `total_cost_usd`, and `usage`. The invoker is an injectable callable so failure-path tests mock it; the real proof shells out for real.
  - Do: (1) `analyzer/enrich/partition.py`: deterministic DPEA phase-2 partitioner over the projected arch tree (line-budget subtree grouping, affinity merge of undersized partitions by relationship count, source-owned relationship assignment), with an `include_ids` filter for --update mini-partitions. (2) `analyzer/enrich/prompts.py`: per-partition prompt built from RESOURCES.md's payload contract (schema, role vocabulary, criticality guidance, few-shot) plus store facts (component rich fields, capabilities, entities, rules, edge evidence) — the store grounds the model MORE than the old file-reading ritual. Plus an architecture-level prompt. (3) `analyzer/enrich/engine.py`: orchestrate derive -> plan -> (bounded-parallel) invoke -> parse -> clean-to-schema + inject metadata -> validate via the scorer's own validators -> retry once with feedback -> stamp valid partitions via P7-1 `stamp_enrichment` (all-or-nothing per partition: a partition failing validation twice is recorded failed, its targets stay unenriched, never junk) -> project + run `scripts/score-ai-enhancement-quality.py` as the gate. (4) `analyzer/enrich/enhance_cli.py` wired into `analyzer/cli.py`. (5) `--update`: staleness-scoped via P7-1 `enrichment_staleness` (stale or missing component targets) expanded to architectural neighbors (undirected, all edge types), architecture narrative regenerated when its digest is stale or any target is in scope (provenance-grounded, not literal always-regen; a no-change update is genuinely zero). (6) Cost controls: `--max-partitions`, `--max-parallel` (default 4), `--model` (default sonnet-class), `--dry-run` (print plan + prompt sizes, no invocation), `--threshold`, `--report`. No hardcoded paths (scorer + store located relative to repo root / store path args). (7) SKILL.md becomes a thin wrapper pointing at the CLI, keeping its deploy/preview flows.
  - Accept: partition planning is deterministic and cap-honoring; prompts embed the schema contract + store facts; a schema-invalid response is rejected and (after one retry) recorded failed with targets unenriched; --update scopes to stale/missing + neighbors and a no-change update enhances zero; --dry-run invokes nothing; a real headless run against the polyglot fixture writes provenance-stamped rows and passes the scorer at threshold; exit code reflects partial failure.
  - Verify: `pytest tests/ -q`, `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/`, and a real bounded headless run against `tests/fixtures/polyglot` (full run passes the scorer; a second no-change --update enhances zero targets).
- Evidence:
  - ENTRY SHAPE + INVOCATION (recorded): `python3 analyze.py enhance [root] --store <path>`, dispatched from `analyzer/cli.py:main` on the first token `enhance` (analyze.py stays the single entrypoint). The Python Agent SDK is not installed (`pip show claude-agent-sdk` empty); the `claude` CLI is (v2.1.110). The engine invokes `claude -p --output-format json --model <model>` with the per-partition prompt on stdin (stdin avoids ARG_MAX on large prompts) and parses the envelope's `result`, `total_cost_usd`, and `usage`. The invoker is an injectable callable (`analyzer/enrich/engine.py:Invoker`) so failure-path tests mock it while the happy path shells out for real.
  - ENGINE (analyzer/enrich/): `partition.py` deterministic DPEA Phase-2 planner (line-budget subtree grouping that never orphans a child from its parent, affinity merge of undersized groups by relationship count, source-owned relationship assignment, `include_ids` scoping for --update); `prompts.py` builds per-partition prompts from the RESOURCES.md contract (schema, role vocabulary, criticality guidance, few-shot) plus store facts (component rich fields, capabilities, entities, rules, edge evidence, inbound/outbound counts) and the architecture-level prompt; `engine.py` orchestrates derive -> plan -> bounded-parallel invoke (ThreadPoolExecutor, `--max-parallel`) -> parse (tolerates markdown fences) -> clean-to-schema + inject engine-owned metadata (`ai_enhanced_at`, `ai_enhance_version=2`) -> validate with the SCORER'S OWN validators (loaded from `scripts/score-ai-enhancement-quality.py` by file path relative to the repo root, single rule set, wire-not-rewrite) -> retry once with the validator feedback -> stamp valid partitions via P7-1 `stamp_enrichment` (DigestIndex + `current_commit_sha`) -> project via `apply_enrichment_overlay` and run the scorer script as a subprocess gate. All-or-nothing per partition: a partition failing validation twice is recorded failed and NONE of its targets are written (never junk). No hardcoded paths: scorer + store located from the repo root and store-path args.
  - REAL FIXTURE-RUN PROOF (bounded, from a clean store): built a v2 store from `tests/fixtures/polyglot` (8 components, 1 http edge, 1 capability), then `python3 analyze.py enhance tests/fixtures/polyglot --store <db> --max-parallel 2` (model `sonnet` = claude-sonnet-4-6) ran ONE partition + the architecture narrative headlessly, enriched 8/8 components + 1/1 relationship + the architecture root in a single attempt, and the quality gate PASSED at 98.8% avg score, 100% coverage, cost $0.1262. Rows are provenance-stamped: e.g. `component:services/api` carries `derived_from_hash=ca0bfe8f...` and `commit_sha=7fd921dd...`, payload keys `{help_text, data_handled, criticality, architectural_role, tech_context, port_assessment, ai_enhanced_at, ai_enhance_version}`. UPDATE-ZERO PROOF: a second `--update` run with no source change reported "0 component target(s) (stale/missing + neighbours)", "nothing to enhance", enriched 0 components / 0 relationships / architecture=no, exit 0, and the gate still PASSED (existing rows fresh, 100% coverage). The no-op update is genuinely zero because the architecture narrative is regenerated only when its digest is stale or a target is in scope (provenance-grounded, I5), not literally always.
  - FAILURE-PATH PROOF (mocked invalid response): `tests/test_enhance_cli.py::test_invalid_response_is_rejected_and_targets_stay_unenriched` mocks the invoker to return an invalid `criticality` for one component on every attempt; the single fixture partition holds all components, so it fails validation, is retried once (attempts==2), is recorded in `failed_partitions == [0]`, `components_enriched == 0`, the store's component enrichment stays empty, and `report.ok is False` (exit code reflects partial failure). Mocking is used ONLY for this failure path; the happy-path proof above is real.
  - DETERMINISM + SCOPING TESTS: `tests/test_enhance_cli.py` (11 tests, all green) covers partition planning determinism (same plan regardless of input order), the component-cap split keeping the subtree root attached, affinity merge of small groups, `include_ids` scoping; prompt construction embedding the schema contract + store facts (`services/api`, `python`, `inbound_edges`, the relationship key) and the architecture root contract; dry-run invoking nothing (an ExplodingInvoker that raises if called); a real mocked full run writing 8 provenance-stamped rows and PASSING the real scorer subprocess gate; and the --update scenario (corrupt one stored digest -> update targets exactly `{services/api, services/web/src}` (stale + its http neighbour), then a no-change update enhances zero).
  - SKILL.md WRAPPER: `.claude/skills/ai-assist/SKILL.md` is now a thin wrapper. The DPEA phases are gone from the skill; it documents (1) build the v2 store + project via `analyze.py --engine v2 --split --store`, (2) `analyze.py enhance [--update]` with the cost-control flags, (3) re-project so the overlay lands in the viewer manifest, (4) validate, then keeps its interactive build/preview/deploy/monitor flow (former Steps 5-6) verbatim. RESOURCES.md is unchanged (it is the payload contract the CLI builds prompts from). The P2-5 hardcoded-path removal is preserved (no absolute paths reintroduced).
  - VERIFICATION: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 928 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`); the 11 new tests are green. `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` clean. Working tree clean after the run (proof artifacts live in the scratchpad, not the repo; no root `architecture.json`, no `.solution-explorer/` committed). DEVIATIONS: (1) the always-regenerate-architecture rule is implemented as regenerate-when-arch-digest-stale-or-target-in-scope, which is provenance-grounded (I5) and makes a no-op update genuinely zero-cost instead of re-spending on an unchanged narrative; a full run always regenerates. (2) Invocation is the `claude` CLI, not the Python Agent SDK, because the SDK is not installed here; the invoker is abstracted so swapping to the SDK later is a one-class change. (3) CI wiring is recorded as a Discovered follow-up (the `enhance` exit code + cost make it CI-ready, but `.github/` is outside this task's territory).

### P7-3: AI verification of inferred edges
- Status: DONE (elaborated and implemented 2026-07-13, Opus 4.8 Stream A). Branch program2/p7-verify.
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design: WORK-PLAN-2.md section 3 Phase 7 gate line (low-confidence edges get an AI verification verdict recorded with provenance; refuted edges are marked, not deleted); LENS-DESIGN.md section 9; invariants I1, I5, I15.
- Scope: an enrichment pass that examines inferred-confidence edges against source evidence and records a verdict (confirmed, refuted, uncertain) with provenance; refuted edges are marked and de-emphasized, never silently deleted; verdicts surface in viewer and MCP.
- Elaboration (Do / Accept / Verify), no scope change:
  - MECHANISM (recorded): an edge verdict is a provenance-stamped enrichment row, `target_kind='edge-verdict'`, `target_id=<source>|<target>|<type>` (the P7-1 `relationship_target_id`), payload `{status, reason}` with `status` in {confirmed, refuted, uncertain}. The digest is the P7-1 RELATIONSHIP digest of that edge (extended into `DigestIndex.for_target` for the new kind), so a verdict goes stale when either endpoint's code changes or the edge is retyped (I5). Edge verdicts ride the enrichment table because it is the only store surface NOT wiped by the derivation `_flush` (edges/findings/concerns rows are re-derived every run); the enrichment overlay is therefore the durable, provenance-consistent home, and the frozen component/symbol/relationship/architecture digest definitions are unchanged (this only adds the `edge-verdict` target-kind alias onto the existing relationship digest).
  - Do: (1) `analyzer/enrich/digest.py`: `DigestIndex.for_target` maps `edge-verdict` -> the relationship digest (additive alias; frozen definitions untouched). (2) `analyzer/enrich/passes.py` `verify_edges`: derive -> select `confidence=='inferred'` edges (bounded by `--max-targets`, deterministic order) -> per edge build a prompt (its evidence file/line/snippet from the store edge row plus each endpoint's summary: name, type, language, help_text/description) asking for a verdict + one-line reason grounded in the evidence -> parse+validate (status in the set, reason non-empty) -> stamp `edge-verdict` enrichment via P7-1 `stamp_enrichment`. `--update` re-verifies only stale/missing verdicts (staleness from the relationship digest); `--dry-run` invokes nothing; a failed/invalid verdict is recorded, not written (never junk). (3) `analyzer/enrich/verdicts.py` `apply_verdict_overlay`, wired into `analyzer/project/pipeline.py` after `apply_enrichment_overlay`: each relationship with an edge-verdict row gets `verdict={status, reason, stale?, derived_from_commit?}`, and a `refuted` verdict additionally sets `de_emphasized=true` on the relationship (marked + de-emphasized, NEVER deleted, the gate line). (4) `analyzer/enrich/verify_cli.py` `verify` subcommand wired into `analyzer/cli.py` (`analyze.py verify edges [root] --store <db> [--max-targets N] [--update] [--dry-run] [--model M] [--report F]`). Cost controls and failure honesty per the P7-2 patterns (bounded, dry-run, exit codes).
  - Accept: inferred edges get a verdict enrichment row stamped with the relationship digest; a changed endpoint flips the verdict's staleness; a refuted verdict marks + de-emphasizes the edge in the projection and never deletes it; `--max-targets` caps invocations; `--dry-run` invokes nothing; an invalid verdict is not written; a real bounded run over the polyglot fixture (1 inferred http edge) records a real verdict with a cost.
  - Verify: `pytest tests/ -q`, `ruff check`, and a real bounded `verify edges` run against `tests/fixtures/polyglot`.
- Evidence:
  - MECHANISM AS BUILT: an edge verdict is a provenance-stamped enrichment row `target_kind='edge-verdict'`, `target_id='<source>|<target>|<type>'`, payload `{status, reason}`. `DigestIndex.for_target('edge-verdict', key)` returns the relationship digest of that edge (analyzer/enrich/digest.py, additive alias; the four frozen digest definitions are unchanged). The pass (`analyzer/enrich/passes.py:verify_edges`) derives the arch (store-only), overlays existing enrichment for endpoint help text, selects `confidence=='inferred'` relationships in deterministic key order, builds a per-edge prompt (`prompts.build_edge_verify_prompt`) carrying the edge's own evidence (file/line/snippet from the store edge row) plus each endpoint's summary, validates the response (`status` in {confirmed, refuted, uncertain}, non-empty `reason`), and stamps via P7-1 `stamp_enrichment`. Storage rides the enrichment table because the derivation `_flush` rewrites edges/findings/concerns every run; the projection overlay (`analyzer/enrich/verdicts.py:apply_verdict_overlay`, wired into both `project_split` and `project_monolith` after `apply_enrichment_overlay`) is therefore the durable home. A `refuted` verdict sets `verdict={status,reason,stale?,derived_from_commit?}` AND `de_emphasized=true` on the relationship; the edge itself is never deleted (asserted: `store.edges()` still holds it).
  - REAL BOUNDED RUN (polyglot, 1 inferred http edge, `claude` CLI v2.1.110, model sonnet): `python3 analyze.py verify edges tests/fixtures/polyglot --store <db>` -> `verdicts: confirmed=1, done: 1, failed: 0, cost: $0.0436`. The verdict row `edge-verdict services/web/src|services/api|http` carries `derived_from_hash=e1deba934ce0...` (= the current relationship digest) and `commit_sha=fd65886b...`, payload status `confirmed`, reason: "The snippet at services/web/src/client.ts:8 contains the literal URL 'http://api:8000', which matches the target component 'services/api' (a Python api-server), indicat[ing a real dependency]." The model confirmed the real edge from its own evidence; a mocked run drives the refuted/uncertain/invalid paths.
  - TESTS (`tests/test_verify_edges.py`, 8 cases, all green): a confirmed verdict is stamped with the current relationship digest (provenance); a refuted verdict marks + de-emphasizes the edge in the projection and the edge is still in `store.edges()` (never deleted, the gate line); an uncertain verdict is recorded and NOT de-emphasized; an invalid `status` (both attempts) is recorded failed and no row is written (never junk); `--dry-run` invokes nothing (ExplodingInvoker); `--max-targets 0` caps to zero work with no invocation; the verdict goes stale when an endpoint's member file content_hash changes (both `enrichment_staleness` and the projection carry the stale marker, payload still served); `--update` re-verifies nothing on a no-change run and exactly the stale edge after an endpoint changes.
  - VERIFICATION: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 951 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` clean. Working tree clean of proof artifacts (they live in the scratchpad; no root `architecture.json`, no `.solution-explorer/` committed). DEVIATIONS: none in scope. The viewer is untouched (verdicts land in projections only; viewer rendering of the `verdict`/`de_emphasized` keys is later work per the "surface in viewer and MCP" scope, and old viewers ignore the optional keys).

### P7-4: Concern naming, intent conformance, finding verification
- Status: DONE (elaborated and implemented 2026-07-13, Opus 4.8 Stream A). Branch program2/p7-verify.
- Model: Opus 4.8 (no substitution)
- Design: LENS-DESIGN.md sections 9 and 8 (I15); TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md invariants I1, I5
- Scope: enrichment names concerns in domain language; declared intents (human-authored or enrichment-proposed from docs and observed architecture) are evaluated against the model with violations emitted as findings (the reflexion-model pattern); every finding is adversarially verified against its own evidence before surfacing, with unverified findings marked and never presented as fact. All outputs carry provenance per I5.
- Elaboration (Do / Accept / Verify), no scope change (three sub-passes share the P7-3 machinery: invoker, provenance stamping, staleness scoping, projection overlay, cost controls, dry-run, exit codes):
  - MECHANISM (recorded): all three sub-passes store output as provenance-stamped enrichment rows and surface it through `apply_verdict_overlay` (analyzer/enrich/verdicts.py). New digest kinds (analyzer/enrich/digest.py, additive): a CONCERN or FINDING digest is `membership_digest(kind, sorted member-component digests)`, so an AI name or verdict goes stale when a member component's code changes (I5); the frozen component/symbol/relationship/architecture definitions are unchanged. Target kinds: `concern` (name), `finding` (AI-generated intent-violation finding record), `finding-verdict` (adversarial verdict for any finding). Enrichment rows survive the derivation `_flush` (which rewrites the deterministic concerns/findings tables), so the overlay is the durable record; `store.set_finding_verification` is a same-session convenience for direct readers, recorded as best-effort (a re-derive resets the table; the overlay re-applies).
  - Sub-pass 1 CONCERN NAMING (`passes.name_concerns`, `analyze.py name concerns`): for each mechanical concern, a prompt (`prompts.build_concern_name_prompt`) from the concern's member facts (component name/type/language, files, evidence) asks for a domain-language `{name, description}`; stamped `concern` enrichment; overlay fills the projection concern's `name`/`description` (the P6-8 name slot) while the mechanical `title` stays.
  - Sub-pass 2 INTENT CONFORMANCE (`passes.check_intents`, `analyze.py verify intents`): declared intents load from `solution-explorer-intents.json` at the repo root or `--intents <path>` (`analyzer/enrich/intents.py`; schema `[{id, statement, scope?}]`, documented in the module docstring; a malformed file raises `IntentFileError` loudly). Each intent is evaluated against scope-selected store facts (components matching scope hints, plus in-scope concerns, capabilities, entities, edges) via `prompts.build_intent_conformance_prompt` (the reflexion-model pattern). A violation emits a `finding` enrichment row `kind='intent-violation'` with violating members, evidence (the intent statement + the model's reason), model confidence, `verification_status='unverified'` (sub-pass 3 flips it), and a deterministic rank_score; a now-satisfied intent deletes any prior violation row (no stale data). `--propose-intents` additionally proposes candidate intents from docs and observed architecture into the report ONLY (advisory; adoption is a human editing the file, never auto-adopted).
  - Sub-pass 3 FINDING VERIFICATION (`passes.verify_findings`, `analyze.py verify findings`): every finding (deterministic duplication/orphan/inconsistency from the findings table PLUS AI intent-violation findings from enrichment) that lacks a verdict (or, on `--update`, whose verdict is stale) gets an adversarial prompt (`prompts.build_finding_verify_prompt`) asking the model to REFUTE it from its own evidence. `verified` survives (status -> verified), `refuted` -> refuted (retained + `de_emphasized`, never deleted), `uncertain` -> stays unverified (never presented as fact, the DeepWiki lesson). Stamped `finding-verdict`; overlay flips the projection finding's `verification_status` and attaches the verdict/de-emphasis.
  - CLI shape (recorded): `analyze.py verify <edges|intents|findings|all> [root] --store <db> [--max-targets N] [--update] [--dry-run] [--model M] [--intents PATH] [--propose-intents] [--report F]` and `analyze.py name <concerns|all> [root] --store <db> [same cost-control flags]`, dispatched from `analyzer/cli.py:main` on the first token.
  - Accept: a concern gets a domain name stamped with the concern digest and it fills the projection name slot; the canonical audio fixture yields a violation finding with two members when there are two audio pipelines and no finding when there is one; intent proposals are advisory only; adversarial verification flips deterministic and intent-violation findings to verified/refuted and refuted ones are retained + de-emphasized; uncertain stays unverified; invalid responses are not written; staleness flips on member-code change; real bounded runs on the polyglot (findings) and concerns/audio fixtures record real verdicts with costs.
  - Verify: `pytest tests/ -q`, `ruff check`, real bounded `name concerns`, `verify intents`, and `verify findings` runs.
- Evidence:
  - CONCERN NAMING: `tests/fixtures/concerns` (svc_a via structlog, svc_b via loguru) derives `concern:logging` (2 members) plus an inconsistency finding and two orphans. REAL run: `python3 analyze.py name concerns tests/fixtures/concerns --store <db>` -> `named=1, cost $0.0431`; the stamped `concern:logging` row carries `derived_from_hash=a29e04f92604...` (= the current concern digest) and payload `{"name": "Application Logging", "description": "Both services import a shared logging library, indicating a common cross-cutting concern for capturing runtime application events."}`. Tests: the name is stamped with the concern digest (provenance) and fills the projection concern's `name` slot with the mechanical `title` retained; the name goes stale (both `enrichment_staleness` and the served payload) when a member file's content_hash changes.
  - INTENT CONFORMANCE, CANONICAL FIXTURE BOTH WAYS. Schema (documented in `analyzer/enrich/intents.py`): a JSON array of `{id (required, unique kebab-case), statement (required), scope (optional hint list)}`, or an object with an `intents` array; malformed content raises `IntentFileError`. The declared intent `tests/fixtures/intents/solution-explorer-intents.json` states "The system has exactly one audio pipeline implementation ...", scope `["audio","pipeline"]`. `tests/fixtures/intents/audio_two` has two audio pipeline packages (pipeline_alpha + pipeline_beta); `audio_one` has one. REAL run on audio_two (`analyze.py verify intents ... --intents ...`) -> `violation=1, cost $0.0447`; the model emitted `finding:intent-violation:single-audio-pipeline` (kind intent-violation, 2 members {services/pipeline_alpha, services/pipeline_beta}, confidence medium, unverified, digest `b0e0a0e5e0dd...`) with reason "The as-built facts show two independent pipeline packages, pipeline-alpha and pipeline-beta ...". Tests (mocked judgment keyed off the REAL store facts in the prompt so the extract -> scope-facts -> prompt -> finding path is exercised, only the verdict stubbed): two pipelines -> one violation finding with two members, surfaced in the projection findings index; one pipeline -> satisfied, zero finding rows; a now-satisfied re-check deletes the prior violation; no intents file is a clean no-op; proposals (`--propose-intents`) land in the report only and are never stored as intents.
  - FINDING VERIFICATION. REAL run on the polyglot's 5 orphan findings (`analyze.py verify findings tests/fixtures/polyglot --store <db>`) -> `uncertain=5, done 5, failed 0, cost $0.2173`. The orphan findings carry no per-member reference evidence (orphanhood is a graph-absence claim), so the model correctly declined to verify them and they stay unverified: honest behavior, never asserting an unproven finding (the DeepWiki lesson; LENS-DESIGN I15). Tests (`tests/test_enrich_p7_4.py`, mocked model): a deterministic finding flips to `verified`, another to `refuted`, and after a fresh re-derive (which resets the findings table to unverified) the projection overlay re-applies both verdicts, the refuted finding is retained with `de_emphasized=true` and never dropped; uncertain findings stay unverified; an invalid verdict is not written; dry-run invokes nothing; intent-violation findings are also verified through the same pass (verdict stamped `finding-verdict` keyed by the finding id).
  - PROJECTION SURFACING: `apply_verdict_overlay` is wired into `project_split` and `project_monolith` after `apply_enrichment_overlay`; it is a complete no-op when the store carries no verdict/name enrichment kinds, so the P5-6 parity fixtures and every non-verified projection are byte-identical (full suite green, parity tests included). Relationships gain optional `verdict`/`de_emphasized`; concerns gain optional `name`/`description`; findings gain `verification_status`/`verdict`/`de_emphasized`, and AI intent-violation findings are added to the ranked `findings` index. All optional keys, so old viewers ignore them (the ai_enhance optional-key precedent).
  - STORE HELPERS (additive, `analyzer/store/db.py`): `set_finding_verification(finding_id, status)` (same-session table update; the durable record is the enrichment overlay, recorded caveat) and `delete_enrichment(target_kind, target_id)` (removes a superseded verdict row and its FTS entry, used when a satisfied intent clears its stale violation). No schema change (SCHEMA_VERSION stays 4); verdicts/names ride the existing enrichment table's `target_kind`/`target_id`/`payload_json`/`derived_from_hash`/`commit_sha`.
  - VERIFICATION: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 951 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`); +23 new tests (8 in test_verify_edges.py, 15 in test_enrich_p7_4.py). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` clean. Working tree clean (proof artifacts in the scratchpad; no root `architecture.json` committed). DEVIATIONS: (1) intent-violation findings ride the enrichment table (target_kind 'finding'), NOT the deterministic findings table, because they are AI-generated (I1: the deterministic table holds only deterministic findings) and must survive the derivation `_flush`; the overlay merges them into the projection findings index. (2) `set_finding_verification` on the table is best-effort within a session; a re-derive resets it and the enrichment overlay is authoritative (recorded). (3) The viewer is untouched (surfacing of `name`/`verdict`/`verification_status` is later viewer work; old viewers ignore the optional keys). (4) The self-repo's broad P5-6 findings are not verified here (bounded fixtures only); a repo-wide verification run is CI/operator work with its own cost budget.
