## Phase 8: The query surface

### P8-1: MCP server
- Status: DONE (2026-07-14, PR program2/p8-mcp)
- Model: Opus 4.8 (no substitution)
- Design: TARGET-ARCHITECTURE.md section 8 (seven-tool table) as extended by LENS-DESIGN.md section 6 (se_rules, se_findings -> nine tools); invariants I3, I5, I9
- Scope: the nine tools (se_overview, se_search, se_component, se_symbol, se_refs, se_impact, se_coverage, plus se_rules and se_findings per LENS-DESIGN.md section 6) over the store, in-process reads only, evidence and confidence and staleness in every response; packaging decision recorded; registered and documented for Claude Code; integration-tested against the fixture stores and this repo.
- Elaboration (Do/Accept/Verify, scope-preserving):
  - DECISION (packaging, TARGET-ARCHITECTURE section 12): SAME PyPI package. The MCP server is a thin read layer over `analyzer.store`; a separate package would duplicate the store dependency and invite version skew. It ships as a new `analyzer/mcp/` subpackage with a console entry point `solution-explorer-mcp = analyzer.mcp:main` and an optional dependency group `mcp` (reserved for the official SDK upgrade path; the shipped server needs no third-party dependency).
  - DECISION (MCP protocol): the official `mcp` Python SDK is NOT installed in this environment (`pip show mcp` empty). Per the card directive, the server implements the MCP stdio transport directly: newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdin/stdout, handling `initialize`, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`, and `ping`. This keeps invariant I7 (local-first, zero required dependency). UPGRADE PATH: the nine tools live in `analyzer/mcp/tools.py` as pure `(FactStore, args) -> result` functions with JSON-Schema specs; swapping to `mcp.server.Server` is a transport change only, the tool registry is handed over unchanged.
  - DECISION (traversal, TARGET-ARCHITECTURE section 12, rustworkx vs CTE): DEFER rustworkx. Benchmarked the bounded-depth CTE (`store.traverse`) on this repo's own v2 store (52 components, 35 edges): 0.01 ms/call at depth 1, 0.02 ms at depth 3, 0.04 ms at depth 6 (1000-iteration average). se_impact uses the CTE for the ranked reachable set plus an in-memory BFS over `store.edges()` (loaded once, 0.08 ms) for the per-hop evidence chain. rustworkx is reconsidered only if a demonstrated large-graph need appears at Phase 9 scale.
  - DO: nine tools over a `--store PATH` (in-process SQLite reads only, I9). Every response is compact structured text by default (the token-efficiency thesis) and carries evidence file:line, confidence tier, and staleness inline where applicable; a `json: true` argument on every tool returns the same structured payload as JSON text for machine consumers. Responses are bounded: list tools take a `limit` and append an explicit "N more" truncation notice, never silently truncate. Staleness is computed at read time via `analyzer.enrich.staleness` / `DigestIndex` (I5), never stored. No LLM calls (I9).
  - ACCEPT: (1) stdio round-trip test spawns the server, runs `initialize` -> `tools/list` (nine tools) -> three representative `tools/call`, asserts well-formed JSON-RPC results; (2) per-tool response-shape tests against a fixture store; (3) truncation-notice test; (4) guardrail tests: unknown tool, malformed args -> JSON-RPC errors; missing store -> clean non-zero exit with a message, not a stack trace; (5) registration documented in README and proven working (`claude mcp add` where the environment allows, else the driven-stdio proof).
  - VERIFY: `pytest tests/ -q` green; `ruff check` clean; the stdio round-trip and registration proofs recorded in Evidence; no `architecture.json` committed.
- Evidence:
  - PACKAGE: new `analyzer/mcp/` subpackage in the SAME `solution-explorer` PyPI package (packaging decision, TARGET-ARCHITECTURE section 12): `__init__.py` (packaging rationale in the docstring), `context.py` (a cached read-only `StoreContext` over one `FactStore`), `tools.py` (the nine `ToolSpec`s + pure handlers + JSON-Schema), `server.py` (`MCPServer` dispatch + `serve_stdio` + `run`), `cli.py` (`solution-explorer-mcp --store PATH`), `__main__.py` (`python -m analyzer.mcp`). `pyproject.toml`: console entry `solution-explorer-mcp = analyzer.mcp:main` and an optional `mcp` dependency group reserved for the official-SDK upgrade path (the shipped server needs no third-party dependency, invariant I7). Territory respected: viewer/, .github/, action.yml, infrastructure/, packages/cli/, scripts/ untouched; `analyzer/store/` NOT modified (the thin query surface it already exposes plus the enrich staleness/digest readers were sufficient, so no new store helpers were needed).
  - PROTOCOL: `pip show mcp` is empty (SDK not installed), so per the card the server implements the MCP stdio transport directly: newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdin/stdout handling `initialize`, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`, `ping`; unknown method -> -32601, unknown tool / malformed args -> -32602, parse error -> -32700. UPGRADE PATH recorded in `server.py`: the tools are transport-agnostic, so swapping to `mcp.server.Server` is a transport change only.
  - TRAVERSAL: rustworkx DEFERRED. Benchmarked the bounded-depth CTE `store.traverse` on this repo's own v2 store (52 components, 35 edges): 0.011 ms/call depth 1, 0.021 ms depth 3, 0.039 ms depth 6 (1000-iter avg); `store.edges()` full load 0.081 ms. se_impact uses an in-memory BFS over `store.edges()` (loaded once) for the ranked set plus the per-hop evidence chain; the CTE gives the same set. rustworkx is reconsidered only at Phase 9 scale.
  - RESPONSE SHAPES (compact text default; `json:true` returns the same `data` dict): se_overview -> component/role counts, capability-by-kind, entity/rule/concern/finding counts (unverified count), coverage summary, enrichment fresh/stale/unknown, AI narrative with staleness. se_search -> ranked hits {kind,id,context} + truncation note. se_component -> role, files, capabilities (evidence+confidence), rules, entities touched, edges in/out (evidence+confidence+origin+AI verdict), enrichment {payload,stale,derived_from_commit}, activity {commits,authors,last_modified}. se_symbol -> signature, docstring, preview, file:line, containing component, parent chain, symbol enrichment. se_refs -> importers/importees at component granularity with an explicit scope note (TARGET-ARCHITECTURE 10; a symbol id resolves to its component). se_impact -> {affected_count, affected:[{id,depth,via:{from,to,type,confidence,evidence}}], truncation_note}. se_coverage -> summary or filtered rows. se_rules -> filtered rules with plain-language name from `rule` enrichment when present (dormant until a producer runs; honest scope) and staleness. se_findings -> ranked findings with verification_status always present and unverified marked, plus concern memberships when a component filter is given.
  - STDIO ROUND-TRIP PROOF (mandatory): `tests/test_mcp.py::test_stdio_round_trip` spawns `python -m analyzer.mcp --store <fixture.db>` as a real subprocess and drives initialize -> notifications/initialized -> tools/list (asserts 9 tools) -> se_overview, se_search, and se_impact(json) tools/call, asserting well-formed JSON-RPC results and a parseable json-mode payload. `test_stdio_missing_store_exits_cleanly` asserts exit code 2 and "store not found" on stderr.
  - REGISTRATION PROOF (environment allowed the full path): reinstalled editable so the console script registers (`which solution-explorer-mcp` resolves; `--version` -> 0.1.0). `claude mcp add se-selfrepo -- solution-explorer-mcp --store <selfrepo.db>` then `claude mcp list` shows `se-selfrepo ... ✓ Connected` (Claude Code completed initialize + tools/list against the live server). A headless `claude -p` query with `--allowedTools mcp__se-selfrepo__se_overview,se_impact` returned correct results driven THROUGH the tools: 52 components, 601/643 parsed, and se_impact('viewer/src/utils', depth 2) = 6 affected (5 at hop 1, 1 at hop 2). Temporary registration removed afterward (clean config).
  - TESTS: `tests/test_mcp.py` 23 tests: nine-tool registry + json-flag presence, per-tool response-shape tests against a fixture polyglot store (extract+derive in-memory), fresh-vs-stale enrichment marking (I5) on se_component, truncation-notice tests for se_findings and se_search, guardrail tests (unknown tool -> KeyError, missing/malformed args -> ToolError, bad id, bad limit/depth), in-process JSON-RPC dispatch + error-code tests, and the two subprocess stdio tests.
  - README: new "## MCP Server (for AI agents)" section: build-store-then-serve, `claude mcp add`, generic MCP config JSON (with the `python -m analyzer.mcp` fallback), the nine-tool table, and the bounded-response note.
  - VERIFICATION: `pytest tests/ -q` -> 974 passed, 1 xfailed (pre-existing `test_detect_ports`, unrelated). `ruff check analyzer/ tests/ scripts/` clean. No `architecture.json` written (conftest guard green); stores built only in the scratchpad. DEVIATIONS: se_rules plain-language name path is present but dormant (no enrichment producer writes `rule` rows yet; same structure-before-producer discipline as P7-1), surfaced honestly in the tool output and the card. se_refs is import/dependency-level, not a compiler-grade symbol call graph, per TARGET-ARCHITECTURE section 10, and says so in every response.

### P8-3: AI front door for deployed projections
- Status: DONE (2026-07-19, PR pending; live deployed-site dogfood on unamentis-ios remains the orchestrator's post-deploy gate)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Design intent: an AI pointed at a deployed solution-explorer site must consume the enumeration efficiently (owner ambition: a tenth to a hundredth of the tokens of raw-repo reading) without reverse-engineering the JSON layout.
- Scope: the projection emits a static entry document at the site root (llms.txt convention for discoverability plus a machine-readable ai.json, both versioned): what this dataset is, the endpoint map (manifest, coverage + inventory, activity, search shards, detail shards, findings, tours), the recommended walk order for common question shapes (overview first, findings first for review, search-then-detail for lookup), and the token-economy contract (fetch shards, never the whole tree). Viewer build copies it into dist so every deploy carries it.
- Accept: DOGFOOD IS THE GATE: an agent session answers a fixed battery of real questions about unamentis-ios using ONLY the deployed site URLs (no repo access, no MCP), correctly, with fetch counts recorded; same battery on the self-repo projection; the front door document is discoverable at /llms.txt and /ai.json on both.
- Verify: projection tests, one real deployed-site agent run per repo recorded in the card Evidence; PR protocol.
- Design decision (KISS, documented per card guidance): the projection emits `ai.json` + `llms.txt` into the projection root in BOTH modes (split: beside manifest.json, so they land at `/architecture/ai.json` and `/architecture/llms.txt` after the composite action's `cp -r architecture/* .../public/architecture/`; monolith: beside the single architecture.json with the endpoint map pointing at that one file). The viewer ships DATASET-INDEPENDENT static pointer files `viewer/public/ai.json` + `viewer/public/llms.txt` that vite copies to the SITE ROOT (`/ai.json`, `/llms.txt`) on every build; they redirect an agent to `./architecture/`. This required ZERO deploy-machinery changes (the existing `cp -r architecture/*` already carries the projection's front door; vite's public/ copy already carries the pointers). The site-root pointers are tuned to the split layout, which is every real (DPEA/default) deploy; a monolith served directly is still self-consistent because its ai.json sits beside its architecture.json and points at it.
- Implementation: `analyzer/project/frontdoor.py` (FRONT_DOOR_VERSION=1, `build_front_door`, `write_front_door`), wired into `analyzer/project/pipeline.py` project_split + project_monolith (emitted last, after search/coverage/activity, so only files actually written are listed = link integrity), exported from `analyzer/project/__init__.py`, recorded on ProjectionResult (ai_json_path, llms_txt_path). ai.json is `sort_keys=True` byte-stable; llms.txt is a fixed line order. manifest_sections carries per-dataset `present` flags resolved against what actually lands in the entry document (coverage/activity/component_detail_index are writer-injected, not on the arch dict, so they are resolved from the projection inputs, not `arch.get`).
- Tests: `tests/test_project_frontdoor.py` (10 tests, real pipeline on the polyglot fixture): both modes emit both files; split endpoint link-integrity (every concrete endpoint + every listed search shard + the detail filename rule for every indexed component resolves on disk); search section matches search/manifest.json; llms.txt links resolve; manifest-section presence is accurate against the real manifest; byte-determinism across two runs; additive-only (manifest bytes never name the front door, dataset valid with front door deleted = viewer never reads these); build_front_door monolith filename flow-through and bad-mode rejection.
- Verify RESULT: pytest tests/ = 1064 passed, 1 xfailed, 1 failed (the known worktree-environmental `.git`-gitlink coverage-ledger failure, unrelated); ruff clean; viewer `npm ci && npx vitest run` (333 passed) && `npm run lint` clean && `npx tsc -b` clean && `npm run build` OK; confirmed `dist/ai.json` (442 B) and `dist/llms.txt` (684 B) present after build.
- Evidence (self-repo dry-run, compact split projection of this repo = 55 components, 633 files, 316,450 lines, 15,731,574 B of source; the orchestrator runs the live unamentis-ios site battery post-deploy). Bootstrap: fetch `/ai.json` pointer (442 B) -> `/architecture/ai.json` (8,641 B). Battery answered using ONLY files a static server serves:
  - (a) "5 most critical components": fetch `manifest.json` (685,698 B); ranked by findings + relationship coupling -> viewer/src, viewer/src/__tests__, viewer/src/utils, viewer/src/components, viewer/src/hooks. FETCHES: 1 (manifest).
  - (b) "any findings about duplication?": REUSE manifest.json (0 extra B); `.findings` filtered on category=duplication -> YES, 42 duplication findings. FETCHES: 0 additional.
  - (c) "which component owns analyzer/store/ids.py?": fetch `search/manifest.json` (231 B) + `search/search-0000.json` (636,262 B); the ref_kind=file entry's `component` field -> `analyzer/store`. FETCHES: 2.
  - TOTAL unique bytes for the whole battery incl. bootstrap: 8,641 + 685,698 + 231 + 636,262 = 1,330,832 B (~1.33 MB) vs 15.73 MB raw source (~8.5% of source bytes, and far fewer tokens because the fetches are targeted structured JSON, not whole-file reads). Discoverable at `/ai.json` and `/llms.txt` (pointers) and `/architecture/ai.json` + `/architecture/llms.txt` (dataset front door): confirmed emitted.

## Phase 9: Scale proof and release

### P10-1: SBOM and supply chain surfaces
- Status: DONE (2026-07-20, PR program2/p10-1-sbom; FIX-FIRST review addressed 2026-07-20, 9 findings resolved, see the review-fix note in Evidence). Deterministic SBOM built by parsing manifests and lockfiles only (no package-manager execution, no network), emitted as CycloneDX 1.5 sbom.json beside ai.json in both split and monolith modes, plus a viewer-native supply chain surface fed by a compact supply_chain section in the projection. Eight ecosystems supported. HONEST self-repo dogfood (SHIPPING, after excluding test/fixture manifests per finding 1): 2 ecosystems (npm, PyPI), 565 dependencies (46 direct / 519 transitive), 519 exact-pinned / 42 range / 4 unpinned, 2 language targets, 0 parse warnings. Test/fixture manifests are kept and accounted separately (8 ecosystems, 53 dependencies, 2 parse warnings), excluded from the shipping counts and the default CycloneDX components.
- Model: Opus 4.8 after a design pass
- Scope: build an accurate, complete Software Bill of Materials per repo, emitted with every projection and rendered as a supply chain view. Dependencies from every manifest the repo carries (package.json/lock, pyproject/poetry/requirements, Package.swift/Package.resolved, go.mod/sum, Gemfile.lock, Cargo.lock, Podfile.lock, csproj/nuget, gradle/maven), with resolved versions, pin status, and direct-vs-transitive where lockfiles allow. Language target/SDK versions (requires-python, swift-tools-version, dotnet TargetFramework, node engines) surfaced separately, not buried. Standard format: emit CycloneDX JSON as the interchange artifact beside a viewer-native surface. Everything evidence-bearing (which manifest line) and deterministic.
- Design pass first: exact per-ecosystem sources, transitive resolution limits without running package managers, and how the supply chain view relates to the existing vendored/dependency detection.
- Design decisions (this card was the design pass):
  1. Emission. A CycloneDX 1.5 JSON document (sbom.json, specVersion pinned to "1.5", written sort_keys deterministically) beside ai.json in both split and monolith modes. NO timestamp at all (revised per review finding 9) and no random serial number: serialNumber is a content-derived urn:uuid (uuid5 over the sorted component identities), so the whole document is byte-identical across runs on the same inputs, independent of the projection's generated_at. A separate viewer-native `supply_chain` section rides in the manifest (split) and monolith so the viewer never parses CycloneDX.
  2. Sources, deterministic parsing only. npm (package.json direct + package-lock v1/v2/v3, yarn.lock, pnpm-lock.yaml transitives; line-scanned, no YAML dependency), PyPI (pyproject PEP 621 dependencies + optional-dependencies, poetry deps, requirements*.txt, poetry.lock), Swift (Package.swift + Package.resolved v1/v2/v3), Go (go.mod require + `// indirect`, go.sum noted), Ruby (Gemfile + Gemfile.lock specs), Cargo (Cargo.toml + Cargo.lock), CocoaPods (Podfile + Podfile.lock PODS), NuGet (*.csproj PackageReference + packages.lock.json Direct/Transitive). Each dependency carries name, declared constraint, resolved version when a lockfile provides it, pin status (exact-pinned / range / unpinned, classified per ecosystem so a bare 1.2.3 is exact in npm but a caret range in Cargo), direct-or-transitive scope, and evidence (manifest file plus line where cheap). Ecosystems absent from the repo do not appear. Every found manifest is accounted; a manifest that fails to parse is a loud warning plus a parse_error record, never a silent drop.
  3. Language targets surfaced separately (owner directive). requires-python, node engines, swift-tools-version, the go directive, dotnet TargetFramework, and the Ruby version land in a dedicated `targets` list in the supply_chain section AND as CycloneDX properties on the metadata component.
  4. Vendored code. The coverage inventory's `vendored` group is referenced (not re-walked) as vendored components with an evidence pointer, read from the coverage dict at emission time.
  5. Viewer. A supply chain surface behind the existing entry-bar plus overlay pattern (SupplyChainEntry near the coverage/findings bars opens the SupplyChainSurface overlay). Dense and factual: language targets called out at the top, then per-ecosystem dependency tables (name, version, pin badge, direct/transitive badge, evidence link), a scope filter defaulting to direct, vendored and parse-warning sections, and a link to sbom.json. Tooltips through tooltipCopy. Mobile-safe (rows wrap at 390px, body scrolls, no horizontal page scroll), dark mode. A one-line scope note states plainly this is an inventory, not a vulnerability scan (no security posture claimed): the no-theater rule, since the tool ships no scanner.
  6. ai.json. Adds the sbom.json endpoint (split and monolith), a `what does this depend on` walk order, a supply_chain manifest_sections entry, and an llms.txt line.
  7. Multi-repo. Per-member sbom.json falls out of each member's own projection; a top-level multi-repo projection (no single scan root) emits none. Solution-level aggregation is a recorded follow-up, not built (Discovered).
- Evidence:
  - Analyzer: new `analyzer/sbom/` package (models, purl + per-ecosystem pin classification, ecosystems/ with one module per ecosystem plus a TOML helper, collector with a pruned deterministic walk, cyclonedx). Projection wiring: new `analyzer/project/sbom_emit.py`, minimal registration in `analyzer/project/pipeline.py` (stamps `supply_chain` onto the arch so it rides in the manifest and monolith unchanged; writes sbom.json), and the ai.json/llms.txt additions in `analyzer/project/frontdoor.py`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_sbom.py` (34 tests) against real manifest+lockfile fixture pairs under `tests/fixtures/sbom/` (per-ecosystem parsing, pin classification, direct-vs-transitive, parse-error loudness, determinism byte-for-byte, CycloneDX 1.5 structural validity and lowercase-normalized purls, vendored seam, projection emission in split and monolith with the endpoint in ai.json, the self repo end to end asserting react/zustand/pydantic with correct pin flags and fixture exclusion, plus a locking test per review finding 1-7). Full analyzer suite: 1175 pass, 1 xfail, 1 known worktree environmental failure (`.git` is a file in a worktree so it is not a single ledger-directory row: test_coverage_ledger, pre-existing and unrelated).
  - Viewer: types (`SupplyChain*`), `viewer/src/supplyChain/model.ts`, store slice (`supplyChainOpen`/open/close, reset on reload), `SupplyChainEntry.tsx`, `SupplyChainSurface.tsx` (with a ranked-behind test/fixture section), tooltipCopy `supplyChain` group, `resolveJsonModule` enabled so the surface test loads real analyzer output. Tests: `viewer/src/__tests__/supplyChain.test.tsx` (14 tests) against real emitted `supply_chain` sections committed under `__tests__/fixtures/`. Full viewer suite: 371 pass, lint clean, tsc clean, build succeeds.
  - Review fixes (2026-07-20, FIX-FIRST, all 9 findings, each with a real-fixture locking test): (1) test/fixture/example manifests excluded from the shipping SBOM by path segment and marked origin=test (kept, accounted, ranked behind, excluded from CycloneDX by default); the collector also honors .gitignore so a workstation-local ignored manifest is not a shipping source. (2) npm v2/v3 lockfile preserves distinct (name, version) pairs so multiple installed versions of one package all survive. (3) pnpm v5/v6 slash-separated lockfiles parse, and an unrecognized package-key format warns loudly instead of returning garbage. (4) pin classification treats x/X/* as a wildcard only as a whole version component, so `1.0.0-linux` is exact and `1.2.x` is a range. (5) requirements.txt `==` exact pins populate a resolved version and a versioned purl. (6) Go `replace` directives are applied (replacement module and version reported, original recorded in the declared field, local-path replace read as unpinned). (7) requirements `-r`/`--requirement` includes are followed (bounded, cycle-guarded, missing target warns) and `-e git+` VCS editables are recorded pinned to their ref. (8) a bare NuGet `Version` is a floating minimum (range), only `[1.0.0]` is exact. (9) sbom.json carries no timestamp, so the CycloneDX document is byte-identical across runs on the same inputs regardless of generated_at (verified).

Six checklist items, each id + label + status (present | absent | not_applicable) + evidence (path plus matched detail, or an explicit null gap) + a short CRA-clause reference: security_md (SECURITY.md at root/.github/docs), cvd_contact (.well-known/security.txt Contact, or an email/URL/reporting section in SECURITY.md), sbom (reads the P10-1 supply_chain section; present with the sbom.json path and shipping component count, honest at zero, absent when no manifests emitted a SBOM), dependency_update_config (dependabot.yml or renovate.json), support_statement (a Supported Versions section or a SUPPORT.md), and signed_commits (git log --format=%G? over the last 50 commits, reported as a hygiene OBSERVATION that never emits a finding). ## Multi-repo execution

Milestones from `docs/remediation/MULTI-REPO-DESIGN.md`. M1 composes the
single-repo v2 engine over member repos; cross-repo edges (M2), solution MCP
(M3), and solution rules/enrichment/tours (M4) build on it.

### M1: Solution manifest and composed multi-repo projection
- Status: DONE (2026-07-19, PR pending review)
- Model: Opus 4.8
- Scope: A solution manifest (`solution-explorer.solution/v1`, YAML) with a loud-individual-rejection loader; `analyze.py --solution <file>` composes the existing v2 engine per member (each keeps its own store under the member repo, warm and incremental exactly as standalone) into `members/<slug>/` UNCHANGED split projections plus a solution layer (member-index `manifest.json`, per-member coverage summaries never blended, a solution `ai.json`/`llms.txt` front door teaching descent into members); viewer renders a member index for a solution root and opens each member with the existing viewer experience via a `?data=` param. No cross-repo edges (explicitly out of scope for M1).
- Evidence:
  - NEW PACKAGE `analyzer/solution/`: `manifest.py` (loader; `solution-explorer.solution/v1`; PyYAML reused as the declared optional import with loud degradation, escalated to a hard error because the manifest is mandatory input; malformed members rejected loudly and skipped, structurally-broken files raise `SolutionManifestError`; git-URL members parsed and stored but marked unresolved with a loud note, no remote clone in M1), `compose.py` (per-member `extract_repo`/`extract_activity`/`derive_all`/`project_split` into `members/<slug>/`, one `generated_at` minted once and shared for byte-stable output, member order = manifest order), `frontdoor.py` (solution `ai.json`/`llms.txt` that reuse the per-member front-door names and teach descent into `members/<slug>/ai.json`).
  - CLI `--solution` wired in `analyzer/cli.py` (routed before the engine dispatch); summary prints per-member coverage lines plus a solution roll-up line with NO blended denominator (per design decision 5). Example manifest at `solution-explorer.solution.example.yml`.
  - VIEWER: `utils/dataSource.ts` resolves a sanitized `?data=` base (default `./architecture`; rejects schemes, protocol-relative, root-absolute, and `..` traversal) and is threaded through `App.tsx` (manifest fetch) and `store.ts` (detail/coverage/activity fetches). `components/SolutionIndex.tsx` renders the member index (mobile-first: single-column at phone width, responsive grid wider, finger targets, no hover-gated affordances, no horizontal scroll) with per-member coverage badges/stats; each member is an `<a href="?data=members/<slug>">` opening the standard viewer over that member's dataset. Types in `types.ts` (`SolutionManifest`, `SOLUTION_MANIFEST_KIND`).
  - TESTS: `tests/test_solution.py` (15 tests, all through the REAL engine over synthetic member repos, no mocks): composition shape, member-projection BYTE PARITY with a standalone scan, byte-stable determinism, no-blended-denominator, solution front door; negatives: wrong schema / missing name / empty members / non-mapping hard errors, malformed member rejected loudly with rest loading, duplicate slug, PyYAML-missing loud, missing member path is a loud per-member error with rest composing, git-URL member parsed-but-unresolved. `viewer/src/__tests__/dataSource.test.ts` (6 tests: base resolution, sanitization/traversal rejection, member link building).
  - VERIFIED: full `pytest tests/ -q` = 1107 passed, 1 xfailed, 1 failed (the known worktree-environmental `.git`-as-file coverage-ledger test, unrelated); ruff clean; viewer `tsc -b`, `vitest run` (339 passed), `eslint`, and `vite build` all green. Browser-driven end-to-end: solution root renders the member index (title, per-member badges/stats, git-URL member shown "not analyzed"); clicking a member loads the standard viewer over that member's dataset.
  - MEMBER LINKING (KISS route, documented): the viewer keys on `manifest.kind === "solution-explorer-solution"` to render the index; a `?data=<relative-base>` query param overrides the fetch base so a member loads through the unchanged single-repo code path. The whole composed tree deploys at `./architecture/` exactly like a single repo; members live at `./architecture/members/<slug>/`.

## Engineering strategy (robustness and regression)

Design authorities: `docs/remediation/ROBUSTNESS-STRATEGY.md` (gating,
self-validation, resilience, the two paradigms, the parity contract) and
`REGRESSION-STRATEGY.md` (golden-master diffing, blue/green slots, the golden
corpus). These cards are DESIGN-CARDED; the owner green-lights builds. Nothing
here is started.

### R1: Output-contract plus honest-gap backbone
- Status: DONE (all waves 1-5 built). Backbone module, derive-driver adoption,
  project-emitter isolation, the enrich + MCP boundaries, and the
  cross-reference / count-consistency postconditions are all landed. Every
  producer boundary (each derive pass, each projection emitter, each enrichment
  partition and the architecture narrative, each MCP tool) now records a
  deterministic honest gap or degrades to a well-formed error instead of
  crashing or shipping a fracture.
- Scope: generalize the coverage ledger's completeness guarantee to every
  producer (each derive pass, each emitter, each MCP tool, each enrichment pass)
  via Pydantic v2 output models and completeness postconditions (shape and
  completeness, never value), with per-unit exception isolation in the driver so
  a failing unit degrades to a deterministic honest gap instead of shipping a
  fracture or crashing the run. Highest value, most on-brand.
- Wave 1 delivered (2026-07-20, wt/r1-backbone): `analyzer/contracts.py`, the
  zero-dependency backbone (invariant I7): `ProducerGap` (Pydantic-when-available
  / dataclass fallback, mirroring analyzer/models.py), `Isolator` (per-unit
  try/except bulkhead recording a deterministic honest gap and returning a
  caller default), `require`/`PostconditionError` (shape-and-completeness only,
  never value), and deterministic `finalize_gaps` (sorted, no traceback, no
  absolute paths). Applied to the derive driver: every pass in
  `derive_all` now runs under isolation, plus a `_check_output_contract`
  postcondition at handoff (presence, shape, and by-construction count
  consistency; NOT cross-ref resolution or total_components, which some healthy
  runs legitimately violate, deferred). Gaps ride an additive top-level
  `arch["gaps"]` array, emitted ONLY when non-empty so a healthy run is
  byte-identical (proven: `check flask` and `check fastapi` show no drift; the
  engine-parity and full-vs-incremental byte-parity suites pass). Gaps flow to
  the monolith and the split manifest automatically (write_monolith / manifest
  copy every arch key); viewer type `ProducerGap` + optional `gaps?` added
  (degrade-by-absence). Fail-before proved: pre-R1 a raising pass crashed the
  whole run; post-R1 the run completes with the honest gap. EXTRACT_TIER NOT
  bumped: gaps are a derive/project-tier signal, not a new EXTRACTED signal kind,
  so the extraction content-hash cache key is untouched.
- Wave 2 delivered (2026-07-21, wt/r1-wave2-emitters): project-emitter isolation
  in `project_split`/`project_monolith` (the other unguarded driver). Every
  emitter now runs under an `Isolator("project", gaps)`: `prepare_arch` git-info
  reads (gitinfo, Info.plist names), the enrichment and verdict overlays,
  coverage, activity, SBOM, CRA, changelog, search shards, the sidecar
  coverage/activity JSON, and the AI front door. A sidecar that raises records a
  deterministic honest `project.<name>` gap and is simply absent, never
  fracturing the run. The main artifact (the manifest in split mode,
  `architecture.json` in monolith mode) is written LAST so it carries every
  project-tier gap on its additive `gaps` key, and it degrades to a minimal but
  well-formed skeleton (identity fields, empty collections, zeroed stats, the
  same shape wave 1 gave `_assemble`) on its own failure, so the run always ships
  a viewer-loadable artifact that accounts for what failed. Derive-tier and
  project-tier gaps are merged (captured separately, re-finalized, never
  double-counted). SARIF at the CLI level (`project/run.py`) runs after the main
  projection has shipped, so its gap channel is the console: a SARIF failure now
  degrades to a loud honest message instead of crashing the CLI. Byte parity
  proven: a clean run adds no `gaps` key (the reorder of independent file writes
  does not change any file's bytes), the engine-parity and full-vs-incremental
  byte-parity suites pass, and `check flask` / `check fastapi` show no drift.
  Fail-before proved in `tests/test_project_isolation.py` (10 of 12 injection
  tests raise against the pre-wave-2 pipeline; post-wave-2 the run completes with
  the honest gap). EXTRACT_TIER NOT bumped: gaps stay a derive/project-tier
  signal, not a new EXTRACTED signal kind, so the extraction content-hash cache
  key is untouched.
- Waves 3+4 delivered (2026-07-21, wt/r1-wave34-enrich-mcp): the enrich and MCP
  producer boundaries. Wave 3 (enrich): `run_enhance` previously let an
  UNEXPECTED exception inside `_enhance_partition` re-raise through
  `fut.result()` and crash the whole enrichment run. The handled invoke and
  validation failures already degrade in-band to a failed `PartitionOutcome`;
  this extends the same all-or-nothing per-partition discipline to unexpected
  exceptions (one bad partition degrades to a recorded failure, reason scrubbed
  via the honest-gap backbone so it is deterministic, and the rest of the run
  still enriches). Retry and cost logic are untouched (that is R2). Wave 4 (MCP):
  each tool's result is validated for shape-and-completeness at the dispatch
  handoff (`call_tool` runs `_check_tool_result`): a non-ToolResult, a non-string
  `text`, or a non-dict `data` raises PostconditionError, which the server maps
  to a well-formed MCP error with per-tool attribution, never a crashed server
  loop and never the pre-wave silent garbage handoff (a structurally-incomplete
  result was shipped with `isError: false`, a false success). Shape-and-
  completeness only, never value validation. No breaker, no liveness (that is
  R4). Fail-before proved in `tests/test_enrich_partition_isolation.py` and
  `tests/test_mcp_result_postcondition.py`; existing enrich and MCP suites stay
  green. EXTRACT_TIER not bumped (gaps stay a derive/project-tier signal).
- Wave 5 delivered (2026-07-21, wt/r1-wave5-crossref): cross-reference and
  count-consistency postconditions at the derive assembly boundary, the checks
  wave 1 deferred pending per-corpus validation. `_check_output_contract` now
  also asserts component-id UNIQUENESS across the tree, relationship ENDPOINT
  RESOLUTION (every source and target resolves to a tree node id), and a
  total_components BOUND (at least the root-component count, at most the distinct
  tree-node ids). Empirically scoped and validated to produce ZERO gaps and no
  drift across flask, fastapi, the storyboard fixture, and the real iOS demo (530
  relationships, rich SwiftUI flows: 190 tree nodes vs 99 path components, 0
  unresolved endpoints, 0 dups). The earlier deferral worried UI targets
  reference ids outside the component map, but the flow passes ADD those UI-flow
  children to the assembled tree, so UI edges resolve to them; `total_components
  == tree-node count` stays unsafe (hence the bound, not equality). Ledger
  completeness is already an output postcondition at the project tier, so it is
  not re-checked here. Precision of the claim (adversarial review of PR #76): a
  WELL-FORMED graph never trips the contract (verified across 18 repos including
  every fixture and four real Swift/iOS repos, zero output-contract gaps); a
  genuinely malformed graph, such as the pre-existing swiftui_flow tab-id
  collision recorded in the Discovered table, IS surfaced as an honest gap,
  which is the contract working as designed, not a false positive. Fail-before
  proved (injected duplicate id, unresolved endpoint, inflated total_components
  each surface as a derive.output-contract gap). EXTRACT_TIER unchanged. Waves
  (2) project-emitter and (6) `_assemble` skeleton-fallback landed earlier
  (PR #74 and PR #73).

### R2: Retry, timeout, and cost ceiling on the AI-enrichment path
- Status: BUILT (2026-07-21, wt/r2-retry). Bounded, full-jitter, transient-only
  transport retry plus a per-run cost ceiling on the enrichment invoke, wired
  under the existing validation retry, with byte-invisible determinism.
- CRITICAL CORRECTION to the strategy: the outbound call is NOT an HTTP SDK call;
  it shells out to the `claude` CLI (`engine.ClaudeCliInvoker`, 600s per-attempt
  subprocess timeout already present). R2 is designed around that subprocess
  reality, and the per-attempt timeout is kept as-is.
- Classifier (`analyzer/enrich/retry.py::classify_outcome`, pure, exhaustively
  tested). Grounded in a real CLI observation (v2.1.x): a model-not-found returns
  exit 1 WITH a JSON envelope on stdout carrying `is_error: true` and
  `api_error_status: 404`. The pre-R2 invoker checked returncode first and never
  parsed that envelope, so it missed the status; the invoker now surfaces
  `api_error_status` as `InvokeResult.status_code`. Classification table:

  | Outcome surface | Decision |
  |---|---|
  | `result.ok` true | SUCCESS |
  | subprocess timeout / spawn failure (`invocation failed:` prefix) | TRANSIENT |
  | `api_error_status` in {408,425,429,500,502,503,504,529} | TRANSIENT |
  | free-text transient marker (rate limit, overloaded, timeout, ... ) when no status | TRANSIENT |
  | `api_error_status` any other 4xx (400/401/403/404/409/422) | DETERMINISTIC |
  | parse failure (`unparseable envelope:` prefix, exit 0 non-JSON) | DETERMINISTIC (never retried) |
  | any other nonzero exit | DETERMINISTIC |

  Structured status wins over free-text markers; the parse-failure prefix is
  checked first so a parse failure is never retried at this layer (the semantic
  validation retry in passes.py/_enhance_partition stays untouched, above this
  layer). Free-text markers are phrases only (never bare 3-digit numbers) so a
  "1500 tokens" message is not read as a 500.
- Retry policy (`RetryPolicy`, injectable): max_attempts=4 (1 initial + 3),
  full-jitter exponential backoff base 1.0s capped at 30.0s per sleep, per-invoke
  total-time budget 120.0s. Transient-only; the budget is checked before each
  retry so combined with the 600s per-attempt timeout a pathological repeated
  hang is capped to a couple of attempts, not four full timeouts. `RetryingInvoker`
  wraps the DEFAULT invoker only (an injected invoker is used as-is, so tests
  wrap their own); it returns the final attempt's result with cost SUMMED across
  all attempts, otherwise byte-identical, so a retried success stamps exactly like
  a first-try success.
- TOOLING DEVIATION (documented on purpose): the strategy recommends `stamina`.
  R2 does NOT use it. (1) Zero-dependency core (I7): stamina would be a new
  third-party dependency. (2) It fights the established determinism seam: stamina
  drives its own monotonic/sleep and its test switch disables retrying wholesale
  rather than making timing deterministic, so it cannot express the fake-clock /
  fake-sleep byte-parity tests this codebase uses. A small full-jitter policy over
  the existing injectable Clock/sleep/RNG seams is the honest fit: same algorithm,
  no new dependency, deterministic tests.
- Cost ceiling: per-run, summed across ALL attempts (retries counted, since the
  RetryingInvoker returns accumulated cost). Partitions are submitted
  INCREMENTALLY; once the ceiling is reached no new partitions launch, in-flight
  ones finish (soft ceiling, overshoot bounded by one in-flight batch), the rest
  are recorded "skipped: cost ceiling reached", and the architecture narrative is
  skipped too. All-or-nothing per partition preserved (a skipped partition is
  never half-stamped, status "skipped" not "failed", so the run exits with partial
  state, honestly reported). Default `--max-cost-usd 10.0` (DEFAULT_MAX_COST_USD):
  generous, since the known enrichment targets (the ~190-component iOS demo and
  this self-repo dogfood) each cost well under a dollar in practice, so the
  ceiling never truncates a legitimate run while bounding a pathological runaway;
  operators raise it for larger runs. It bounds enrichment only (a real-API,
  explicitly-invoked path) and does not touch deterministic analyzer output or the
  golden legs. New CLI flags: `--max-cost-usd` and `--retry-attempts` (documented
  defaults).
- Tests: `tests/test_enrich_retry.py` (32: classifier exhaustiveness, retry
  behavior, jitter bounds under seeded RNG, total-budget enforcement, transient-
  only discipline, cost summation, all with fake Invoker + fake Clock, no real
  sleep) and `tests/test_enrich_cost_ceiling.py` (6, engine-level over the polyglot
  fixture): ceiling stops launches and records honest skips, skipped partition
  stamps nothing, a retried success stamps BYTE-IDENTICALLY to a first-try success,
  and fail-before proofs (a raw transient invoker fails the partition; the wrapped
  one succeeds; no-ceiling accumulates unbounded). Full suite green (1467 passed,
  the one worktree-only coverage-ledger quirk excepted); engine/incremental
  byte-parity intact; healthy default analyzer output unchanged.
- SCOPE GUARDS honored: no persisted cross-run breaker, no MCP changes, no
  auto-update pipeline changes (all R4); partition validation logic and prompts
  untouched; parse failures stay deterministic fail-fast. The P7 verify passes
  (passes.py) share ClaudeCliInvoker and could adopt RetryingInvoker in a small
  follow-up, but R2's retry + ceiling live in the enrichment `run_enhance` path,
  which is exactly the card's surface. EXTRACT_TIER NOT bumped: retry and the cost
  ceiling are enrichment-runtime controls, not a new extracted signal kind, so the
  extraction content-hash cache key is untouched.

### R3: Maturity-channel gating
- Status: DONE (2026-07-21, wt/r3-maturity-gating). The in-repo, server-free
  maturity model plus its governance is built; the mechanism ships with an EMPTY
  production registry (the honest exemplar path, see below).
- Scope: an in-repo, server-free maturity model (experimental/beta/stable,
  default stable). Analyzer features module resolving a --channel; viewer
  build-time constant for hidden features plus a URL-param override for
  experimental-visible; active gates stamped into provenance (determinism
  preserved); governance (expiry, owner, archive-on-done) folded into the
  recurring dogfood gate.
- EXEMPLAR PATH REALITY CHOSE: tests-only, registry EMPTY. An honest survey of
  every candidate the strategy named (Phase A/B wave-1 C#/Java/C++ parse and
  their "not yet frozen" framework edges, hidden-field surfacing, the CRA
  checklist, AI-discovered edges, the per-language reference-extractor maturity
  table) found NOTHING that qualifies: each is already shipped, default-on, and
  in the public demo, not a feature that must be partially visible before it is
  done. Inventing a gate for show would be theater (VISION.md), so
  `features.REGISTERED_GATES` is `()` and the Gate Inventory starts empty with
  the governance in place. The mechanism is proven end to end by tests that
  register their own gate.
- Analyzer (`analyzer/features.py`, pure stdlib, invariant I7): channels
  stable(0)/beta(1)/experimental(2) with Rust-toolchain activation (a channel
  activates every feature at or below its instability level, so on the default
  stable channel beta and experimental are OFF); `resolve_channel` (precedence
  CLI `--channel` > `SOLUTION_EXPLORER_CHANNEL` env > default, invalid values
  rejected loudly at both sources); `Gate` (id + stability + owner + expires +
  summary); `gate_provenance` = the deterministic stamp. DETERMINISM RULE
  enforced: `gate_provenance` returns None on the stable channel AND whenever no
  non-stable gate is active, so a default run (the only value any golden/CI run
  uses) stamps NOTHING and is byte-identical. The stamp lands as an additive
  top-level `gate_provenance` key (channel + sorted active non-stable gates, id +
  stability only) that flows into the manifest/monolith automatically and is
  surfaced in the ai.json front door's dataset identity. Wired through
  `analyzer/cli.py` (`--channel`), `project/run.py` (resolve + thread), and
  `project/pipeline.py::prepare_arch` (stamp). Gating is a v2-engine mechanism.
- Viewer: two independent server-free mechanisms (`viewer/src/utils/channel.ts`).
  (1) BUILD-TIME constant `__EXPERIMENTAL_BUILD__` (vite.config define, default
  false) so genuinely-unfinished code guarded by `if (__EXPERIMENTAL_BUILD__)` is
  dead-code-eliminated from a production bundle; the honest reference consumer is
  an experimental-build-only diagnostic (no unfinished user feature exists). (2)
  RUNTIME channel via a `?channel=` URL-param override, default stable; the lens
  registry (`lenses/registry.ts`) gains an optional `maturity` (default stable)
  and `listAvailableLenses`/`resolveLensId` filter by the resolved channel. A
  visible non-stable lens is labeled explicitly (`maturitySuffix`, e.g.
  "Experimental Test (experimental)") in `LensSwitcher`. The viewer `Architecture`
  type gains an optional `gate_provenance` (degrade-by-absence; the viewer never
  requires it).
- EXTRACT_TIER NOT bumped: the gate-provenance stamp is a project-tier field
  computed from the resolved channel, not a new extracted signal kind, so the
  extraction content-hash cache key is untouched. ADDITIVE projection: old and
  default datasets omit `gate_provenance` and render unchanged.
- Tests: `tests/test_features_gating.py` (16: resolution precedence + validation,
  activation matrix, the determinism invariant that stable never stamps even with
  a gate registered, and end-to-end through the real pipeline over a real fixture
  store: default has no stamp; experimental with an EMPTY registry is
  byte-identical to default; experimental with a registered gate differs by
  EXACTLY the additive stamp and the front door surfaces it; beta does not
  activate an experimental gate). `viewer/src/__tests__/channel.test.tsx` (15:
  channel resolution + URL-param override, activation semantics, registry maturity
  filter, and a LensSwitcher render proving `?channel=experimental` shows the
  experimental lens WITH its label while the default channel hides it). Build-time
  DCE proven by grepping the built bundle: the EXPERIMENTAL_BUILD_DIAGNOSTIC
  sentinel is ABSENT from a production build and PRESENT in a
  `VITE_EXPERIMENTAL_BUILD=1` build. Both golden legs (`check flask` /
  `check fastapi`) show no drift; full pytest and viewer vitest green.
- SCOPE GUARDS honored: no flag server, no persisted breaker state, nothing from
  R4; no new base dependency (I7). Governance recorded in the Gate Inventory
  below and reviewed by the recurring dogfood gate (P9-0).

### Gate Inventory (maturity gates, card R3)

The live registry of maturity gates the R3 mechanism governs. Every gate carries
a maturity, an owner, and a graduation-or-removal expiry. The recurring dogfood
gate (P9-0) reviews this table and forces each gate past its expiry to GRADUATE
(promote a level, ending at stable, then archive the gate and delete its
machinery) or DIE (remove the gate and its code). Fowler's rule: a feature is not
done until its release gate is archived. A stale gate left to accumulate as
permanent config is the number-one documented failure mode and is a defect here.

Where gates live: analyzer gates in `analyzer/features.py` (`REGISTERED_GATES`,
mirrored here on add); viewer lens gates declare `maturity` on their
`viewer/src/lenses/registry.ts` entry; genuinely-unfinished viewer code uses the
`__EXPERIMENTAL_BUILD__` build-time constant. Prefer the keystone pattern (wire
the last connection in the final commit, no gate); reach for a gate only when a
feature must be partially visible before it is done.

| Gate id | Surface | Maturity | Owner | Expires | Graduation / removal criterion |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (none) | -- | -- | -- | -- | EMPTY BY DESIGN: no feature currently needs partial visibility before it is done. Adding a gate for show would be theater. |

### G1: Projection-diff tool
- Status: BUILT (owner green-lit; scripts/projection-diff.py + tests/test_projection_diff.py)
- Scope: a deterministic diff over two full projections reporting component,
  relationship, finding, coverage, inventory, entity, capability, and enrichment
  deltas, human- and CI-readable, so a change is judged improved vs regressed.
- Delivered: stdlib-only CLI over two monolith projections (old/blue, new/green).
  Text report for humans and PR comments; --format json is the complete
  machine-readable delta for CI; --exit-code turns a non-empty diff into exit 1
  for gating. Determinism is a hard contract (never reads a timestamp, every list
  sorted, byte-identical output for byte-identical inputs). Absent sections in an
  older dataset are treated as empty (additive-projection rule). Foundation that
  G3 (two-slot demo/dogfood diff) and G4 (full-vs-incremental parity) build on.
- Extended (2026-07-20, wt/g1-stats-supply): added three diffed sections that
  close the false-clean the identity-keyed sections miss (Discovered table,
  2026-07-20). `stats` roll-up scalar deltas (total_components/files/lines/
  symbols/relationships, total_size_bytes, per-language line counts) catch a
  gross extraction regression such as symbol extraction silently halving;
  `supply_chain` count-roll-up deltas (dependencies overall, per ecosystem, per
  count bucket including warnings and pin status) catch a broken SBOM pass;
  `concerns` added/removed by id. Additive (absent sections degrade to empty),
  deterministic, schema unchanged (solution-explorer/projection-diff/v1). Verified:
  `check flask` stays green (a fresh generation byte-agrees with the committed
  baseline on all three sections, so no re-baseline was needed), and the exact
  halved-symbols false-clean now registers drift (fail-before proved against the
  prior code).

### G3: Two-slot retention for demo and dogfood deploys
- Status: BUILT (2026-07-21, wt/g3-two-slot; uses G1). `scripts/blue-green-diff.py`
  wraps the G1 projection-diff as a NON-BLOCKING deploy-time health check: it
  loads the prior (blue) and new (green) projections, writes the G1 delta to the
  GitHub step summary, and uploads the full JSON diff as a `projection-diff` run
  artifact. It ALWAYS exits 0; a missing, unreachable, or malformed blue degrades
  to a loud honest note in the summary and the deploy continues, so the diff
  infrastructure can never break a deploy.
- Delivered: `--blue` accepts EITHER a local path OR an http/https/file URL (blue
  is read or fetched accordingly, stdlib urllib, 30s timeout); `--new` is the
  local green projection; `--summary-out` appends markdown (pass
  $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY), `--json-out` writes the deterministic artifact. The diff
  body is the G1 delta (single source of truth, loaded by importlib-by-path like
  the enrich scorer), so it stays byte-deterministic; the markdown header adds no
  timestamps. Wiring: (1) DOGFOOD `.github/workflows/architecture-viz.yml`
  `generate` job gained a `Blue/green projection diff (dogfood)` step (after the
  AI-enhancement merge) plus a conditional `Upload Projection Diff`, hash-pinned
  to the same v4.6.2 upload-artifact the workflow already uses. (2) DEMO
  `action.yml` gained an OPT-IN `diff-against-url` input (default empty = skipped,
  zero behavior change for existing consumers) and the matching diff + upload
  steps using the file's own `@v4` action convention.
- CORRECTION to the card premise (verified, not assumed): the card said fetch
  blue from a "live dogfood URL". There is none. VISION.md keeps the self-repo
  dogfood site LOCAL (no public deploy), and the workflow `deploy` job is
  currently SKIPPED (its `if:` gates on `vars.CLOUDFLARE_PROJECT_NAME`, which is
  unset). So git is the dogfood's native retention slot: the committed
  `architecture.json` (the AI-enhanced solution-explorer baseline) is blue and the
  freshly generated `viewer/public/architecture.json` is green. The URL-fetch
  capability still exists and is tested; the DEMO side uses it (the downstream
  installs do deploy to Cloudflare, whose prior-deployment URL is a fetchable
  blue).
- Acceptance proof (dogfood, real, pre-merge): a local end-to-end run diffed the
  committed baseline (name solution-explorer, analyzer 1.0.0, 23 components)
  against a fresh current-engine generation (analyzer 1.2.0, 81 components) and
  produced the expected large health-check delta (+58 components; changes across
  all 12 diffed sections) with a valid JSON artifact. The dogfood step also runs
  on the post-merge main push (the `generate` job runs unconditionally), so the
  step summary carries a real blue-versus-green diff every build.
- Tests: `tests/test_blue_green_diff.py`, 12 hermetic tests (local files and
  file:// URLs only, never the live network). The two contracts locked in:
  degrade-loudly-and-exit-0 (missing/unreachable/malformed blue, missing green)
  and correct-summary-plus-deterministic-JSON on a real diff, plus determinism,
  append-not-overwrite, and URL classification. Both golden legs stay green
  (analyzer output is untouched). EXTRACT_TIER NOT bumped: G3 is a deploy-time
  reporting tool over already-projected output; it adds no extracted signal kind,
  so the extraction content-hash cache key is untouched.
- HONEST OPEN EDGES: (1) the DEMO side is not verified end to end. The downstream
  UnaMentis deploy is broken on an owner-gated Cloudflare token, so adding
  `diff-against-url` to a live install and confirming a real demo diff is gated on
  the owner restoring the token; documented for one-line adoption in
  DEPLOYMENTS.md. (2) The dogfood workflow `deploy` job stays owner-gated off
  (no `CLOUDFLARE_PROJECT_NAME`), consistent with VISION's local-only dogfood; the
  diff runs in the `generate` job instead, so it is unaffected. Enabling a public
  dogfood deploy is an owner decision, not taken here.

### G4: Golden-corpus full-vs-incremental parity check at scale
- Status: BUILT (2026-07-21, wt/g4-parity; uses G1 and G2). A `parity <name>`
  subcommand on scripts/golden-corpus.py proves the incremental-equals-full
  contract on the frozen corpus at scale, so a daily full regeneration is
  provably unnecessary.
- Delivered: two checks, both required to pass. (1) NO-CHANGE parity: a cold full
  generation (store wiped) and a warm rerun (store reused) over the unchanged
  tree must match. (2) CHANGED-FILE parity: one source file is edited, a warm
  incremental run and a cold full run over the mutated tree must match (proving
  the changed-file path, not just no-change); the edit is always reverted so the
  frozen corpus is untouched. The comparison is stronger than the G1 semantic
  diff: `analyzer/parity.py` holds the SINGLE volatile-field allowlist (reused by
  the engine-parity fixture guard, so there is one list not two), and after
  stripping those fields the projections are compared BYTE-for-byte with list
  order preserved, catching an ordering regression a by-id diff would miss. A
  mismatch is a real engine finding, never papered over: it prints the G1 diff
  and exits 1. Validated on BOTH corpora locally: flask and fastapi each pass the
  no-change and the changed-file variants. The changed-file variant works cleanly
  on the shallow single-commit clone (the v2 engine is content-hash incremental,
  so it needs no git commit or --base-sha), so it is included rather than scoped
  out. CI: a `parity` job (both corpora) added to golden-corpus.yml as
  workflow_dispatch only, not per-PR: it runs four analyses per corpus (about 4x
  the `check` cost) and proves a contract that changes only with the incremental
  engine, so on-demand keeps PR CI fast while the per-PR `check` still catches
  projection regressions. Tests: `tests/test_golden_parity.py` (hermetic
  normalizer + harness-wiring + run_parity exit-code tests, plus an opt-in
  GOLDEN_CORPUS_NETWORK live parity test over both corpora). README updated.
- Scope: on the frozen corpus, prove a cold full regeneration and a warm
  incremental run produce the same projection. The real-data proof of the
  incremental-equals-full contract, so a daily full regeneration is provably
  unnecessary.

(2) `_scrub_reason` now collapses absolute paths to `.../<basename>` (deterministic, non-leaking, analogous to the hex-address scrub). | 2026-07-14 | Demo pre-flight (orchestrator) | analyzer/cli.py still defaults --max-file-size to 500000 and passes it into the v2 path, so v2 is bounded by default, contradicting the I2 explicit-opt-in intent (TARGET-ARCHITECTURE 4.1). Loud and ledgered (excluded:max_file_size rows render in the coverage panel), so honest but not as designed. On this repo the 6 exclusions are data/doc files (committed baselines, TASKS.md). | Small follow-up: default None on the v2 path (v1 keeps 500KB), adjust coverage-test expectations. Note the read-before-size-check Discovered item means the default never protected RAM anyway; fixing both together in the pre-P9 blob-handling task is sensible. Deferred past demo week for stability. RESOLVED 2026-07-14 (branch program2/i2-unbounded-default, PR "I2 conformance: v2 analyzes everything by default"): the argparse default for --max-file-size is now None. v2 forwards None (unbounded, 100 percent coverage by default); an explicit --max-file-size is honored and lands as an excluded:max_file_size ledger row. v1 restores 500_000 when the value is None, so v1 output and parity snapshots are byte-identical. Tests (tests/test_cli.py TestMaxFileSizeDefaultPerEngine): v2 default parses a >500KB file (fail-before: pre-fix v2 default excluded it, proven by stash/pop => the test failed with the file at excluded:max_file_size, 1/3 parsed), v1 default still excludes it with the "500,000 bytes" warning, explicit --max-file-size on v2 still excludes with the ledger row. The store-level extract_repo already defaulted max_file_size=None, so the coverage-ledger tests (which call extract_repo directly) were already unbounded and needed no change; no test asserted the old CLI default's exclusions. NOTE on the paired read-before-size-check row (2026-07-13): verified _enumerate still reads bytes before the size check (analyzer/extract/runner.py: read_bytes at the top of the per-file loop, the `size > max_file_size` check after), so a bound never prevented the read; removing the default does not worsen memory behavior. The stat-first blob mitigation remains open under the 2026-07-13 row for the pre-P9 large-repo work. |
| 2026-07-14 | P6-10 non-source inventory | Inventory bytes are read via `stat` at projection time (like the git remote in `prepare_arch`), so `coverage.json` byte totals depend on the working tree, not just the store. On a re-projection from a store with no tree the bytes are null (proven by `test_build_coverage_without_root_omits_bytes_but_keeps_inventory`), and group ranking still holds because bytes are only the secondary sort key after count. | Recorded as a deliberate design choice, not a bug: bytes are best-effort environment data. If a future consumer needs store-only byte determinism, persist per-file sizes for non-source rows in the coverage table (they are not stored today; only parsed files carry `size_bytes` in the `files` table). No action needed for the demo. |
| 2026-07-14 | P6-10 non-source inventory | The inventory classifies the NON-SOURCE family only (binary, unsupported_extension, empty_file, skipped_directory, vendored_repo), mirroring the CoverageBadge, rather than literally every non-parsed row. Source gaps (failed, oversized) are analyzable source and already surface loudly in the coverage gaps family, so folding them into a non-source taxonomy would misclassify a failed .py as "unknown non-source". | Recorded as the coherent reading of "repo totality": every file is still accounted for (source gaps in their family, non-source in the inventory), and `source_gap_total` is carried in the inventory section so a future surface can join the two without re-deriving. No action needed. |
| 2026-07-14 | P6-10 non-source inventory | The full per-file list of a huge group (e.g. 5,970 xcresult files) is NOT emitted inline; each group carries a bounded 200-path virtualized sample plus its true count, and the complete enumeration stays in the coverage ledger rows (which `coverage.json` already carries in full). This keeps `coverage.json` from doubling on a large repo and avoids re-implementing the Python classifier in TypeScript (the analyzer/viewer-must-agree caution). | Recorded. If a future need arises to filter the full ledger by inventory category in the viewer without the classifier, add an optional `category` field to non-source coverage rows at projection time (single source of truth stays in `analyzer/project/inventory.py`). Not needed now. |
| 2026-07-18 | Post-P6-10 demo cleanup (orchestrator) | The v2 enumerator does not consult .gitignore: the unamentis-ios local scan included an untracked, gitignored 107 MB TestResults.xcresult bundle (5,970 ledger rows), so a local working-tree scan can dominate the coverage picture with files that are by definition not repository content. The central repo was never polluted (zero xcresult files tracked; .gitignore already covered them); CI scans of fresh clones are unaffected. The owner's totality definition is the REPO: gitignored files are handled at the workstation and are not first-class citizens. | Cleanup done (local bundle deleted, demo regenerated: 651/712 ledger, 61 non-source, product assets dominant at 54 percent). FOLLOW-UP CARD CANDIDATE: teach _enumerate to honor .gitignore (or ledger such files as excluded:gitignored so they land in the non-source accounted family with an explanation), keeping fresh-clone parity byte-identical since clones contain no ignored files. Decide whether ignored-but-present files should appear at all under the repo-totality definition. RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (branch program2/analyzer-hygiene, PR pending, item 1 of 3): the v2 enumerator now consults .gitignore via a deterministic GitignoreMatcher (analyzer/utils.py, reusing analyzer/project/rules.compile_glob). Ignored files land as the new excluded:gitignored disposition (non-source family) and ignored DIRECTORIES prune to ONE row (the TestResults.xcresult fail-before now yields one row, not thousands, proven in tests/test_analyzer_hygiene.py). Inventory category workstation_ignored ("Gitignored workstation files", recommendation "No action. These are local-only by design."). Fresh-clone parity holds byte-identical (a clone has no ignored files; test_fresh_clone_parity_no_gitignore_no_change). SEMANTICS BOUNDARY as shipped (documented in code): honors root plus per-directory .gitignore at every nested level, patterns anchored to their own directory, negation (!) with last-match-wins across the ancestor chain, dir-only (trailing slash) patterns; ignored dirs are pruned early so a nested negation cannot re-include under an ignored parent (git's own rule); NOT supported: character classes [abc] (treated literally) and trailing-whitespace escaping. |

The P6-11 edits are analyzer stdout + inventory classifier only. Passes on a normal checkout. The named robustness follow-up (accept a `.git` pointer file as satisfying the pruned-git accounting) still stands for the next coverage-ledger-touching task. |
| 2026-07-19 | P6-11 vocabulary ordering | A storyboard or xib that lives inside a `.lproj` localization directory (for example `Base.lproj/Main.storyboard`) classifies as `localization`, not `ui_definition`, because the localization rule is ordered ahead of the new UI-definition rule (first match wins, more specific context wins). The P6-11 fixtures place the storyboard outside any `.lproj`, matching the common iOS layout where `LaunchScreen.storyboard` sits at the group root. | Deliberate and consistent with the module's context-over-extension discipline: a localized `Base.lproj/Main.storyboard` IS localization content. If a future need arises to always surface storyboards as UI regardless of an enclosing `.lproj`, move the `ui_definition` rule ahead of localization; not needed now (no such case in the unamentis-ios unknown set). |
| 2026-07-19 | P6-12 knowledge layer | The AI rule-writing pass ships as a dedicated `analyze.py identify unknowns` subcommand (behind the same first-token dispatch as `enhance`/`verify`/`name`), NOT folded into the `enhance` engine. The card said "the enhance pass ... appends a rule"; a standalone pass keeps the addition focused, testable through the existing `Invoker` seam, and honestly named (it IS an enrichment pass). It reuses the Phase 7 harness (invoker, retry-with-feedback, cost controls, dry-run, exit codes) and the pure `rules_writer.write_inventory_rules`. | Recorded as a scope-preserving placement choice. Composing `identify` into a single `enhance` orchestration run (so one command enriches AND retires unknowns) is a one-call follow-up when an operator wants the combined flow; the store/root/invoker plumbing already matches. |
| 2026-07-19 | P6-12 knowledge layer | Project-rule overrides (label/explanation/recommendation/flags) apply at the GROUP (category) level, resolved deterministically in rule-id order (a later id's set fields win), because inventory groups are keyed by category and one category can be fed by several rules plus the built-in. A rule's `category` reclassification is the always-on behavior; the text/flag overrides are the softer layer on top. | Recorded. The common case (one rule per category) is unambiguous. If per-rule sub-grouping is ever wanted (two visibly distinct sets inside one category), that is a projection-shape change (a sub-group key), deliberately out of the KISS bound here. |
| 2026-07-19 | P6-12 knowledge layer | The writer preserves existing rule entries by re-reading the RAW YAML (not the validated `InventoryRule`) and appending, so human-authored fields and any forward-compatible extra keys survive a write byte-for-value; only whole-file reformatting (key order, comment header) changes. True comment-preserving round-trip would need ruamel.yaml, outside the zero-required-dependency bound. | Recorded. Human rule VALUES are never edited (dedupe on (pattern,category) plus a human-pattern guard), which is the invariant the card names; formatting is machine-owned. If comment preservation is later required, add ruamel behind the optional `enrich`/`live` extras. |
| 2026-07-19 | P6-12 knowledge layer | Multi-repo v2 (`derive/multi.py`) does not load or apply per-repo project rules into the merged projection, consistent with the standing multi-repo capabilities/entities/rules/activity/coverage deferrals (single-repo v2 has full P6-12 behavior). The loader keys off one repo root; a merged run has many. | Flagged in the standing multi-repo deferral family (owner-flagged). Cross-store rule application needs per-repo-prefixed rule scoping, part of the same unowned multi-repo unification card. |
| 2026-07-19 | Campaign dogfood (self-repo inventory) | The self-repo projection surfaced 12 unknowns that are two distinct classes: (1) universal vocabulary gaps (empty .py files land unknown because excluded:empty_file classifies by path rules; go.mod and Gemfile dependency manifests; .venv, .wrangler, tsconfig.tsbuildinfo, egg-info tool droppings), and (2) the tool scanning ITS OWN state: the default store path <root>/.solution-explorer/index.db means the enumerator walks the tool's own directory and ledgers it as unknown non-source. | Class 1: the live identify-unknowns run writes project rules now, and the universal ones are upstream vocabulary candidates (the contribute path's first real payload). Class 2 is a product defect: the enumerator should account .solution-explorer/ as tool state (a pruned single row, like .git), never unknown; small follow-up alongside the excluded:gitignored enumerator card. RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (branch program2/analyzer-hygiene, PR pending, items 2 and 3 of 3). Item 2 (empty files): excluded:empty_file rows now classify into a dedicated inventory category empty_files ("Empty files", recommendation to remove or fill them, likely_unwanted false, gitignore_candidate false) via a disposition-based rule that beats the extension fall-through, so an empty tests/__init__.py no longer lands in unknown (fail-before test_empty_file_classifier_fail_before_was_unknown). Item 3 (tool state): the enumerator accounts .solution-explorer/ as ONE pruned row with disposition excluded:tool_state (reason "solution-explorer tool state"), inventory category tool_state ("Solution Explorer state", "Keep. The rules subdirectory travels with the repo; the store stays local."); nothing under it is enumerated or parsed. The committed rules file .solution-explorer/rules/inventory.yml is STILL read by the rules loader, which opens it by path (not through enumeration), verified by test_tool_state_pruned_but_rules_loader_still_reads_the_rule_file. NOTE: Class 1's universal vocabulary gaps (go.mod, Gemfile, .venv/.wrangler/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo/egg-info droppings) are NOT part of this wave; they remain upstream-vocabulary/contribute-path candidates as the row records. All three new dispositions are NON-SOURCE (never in the coverage denominator): NON_SOURCE_DISPOSITIONS updated in analyzer/project/inventory.py (coverage.py imports it) and mirrored in viewer CoverageBadge.tsx classifyDisposition, kept in sync by test_new_dispositions_are_non_source_in_every_mirror and the viewer CoverageBadge.test.tsx assertions. |
